Ivane Javakhishvili Tbilisi State University
George Tephnadze

Abstract




Abstract

Unlike the classical theory of Fourier series which deals with decomposition of a function into continuous waves, the Walsh functions are “rectangular waves”. Such waves have already been used frequently in the theory of signal transmission, codic theory, cryptography, filtering, image enhancement and digital signal processing.

The problems we have studied in this PhD thesis are central to Mathematical Analysis. They involve techniques which have been developed a great deal during the last three decades.

In this PhD thesis we are dealing with convergence and summability of partial sums, Fejér and Marcinkiewicz means with respect to one- and two-dimensional Walsh-Fourier series on the martingale Hardy spaces.

This thesis is focus to achieve the following main results:

•To find estimation of convergence and divergence of the subsequences of partial sums of the one-dimensional Walsh-Fourier series on the martingale Hardy spaces
H_{p}(G)
, when
0<p\leq 1
.

•To find necessary and sufficient conditions in terms of modulus of continuity of martingale Hardy spaces, for which subsequences of partial sums of the one-dimensional Walsh-Fourier series convergence in
H_{p}(G)
norm, when
0<p\leq 1
.

•To find estimation of convergence and divergence of the subsequences of Fejér means of the one-dimensional Walsh-Fourier series on the martingale Hardy spaces
H_{p}(G)
, when
0<p\leq 1/2
.

•To find necessary and sufficient conditions in terms of modulus of continuity of martingale Hardy spaces, for which subsequences of Fejér means of the one-dimensional Walsh-Fourier series converge in
H_{p}(G)
norm, when
0<p\leq 1/2
.

•To prove strong convergence of one-dimensional Fejér means with respect to Walsh system on the martingale Hardy spaces
H_{p}(G)
, when
0<p\leq 1/2
.

•To prove strong convergence of diagonal partial sums with respect to the two-dimensional Walsh-Fourier series on the martingale Hardy spaces
H_{p}(G^{2})
, when
0<p<1
.

•To prove strong convergence of Marcinkiewicz means with respect to the two-dimensional Walsh-Fourier series in
H_{2/3}(G^{2})
norm.

•To find necessary and sufficient conditions in terms of modulus of continuity of Hardy spaces, for which Marcinkiewicz means of the two-dimensional Walsh-Fourier series converge in
H_{2/3}(G^{2})
norm.

Contents

Key words:
Walsh group, Walsh system,
L_{p}
space, weak-
L_{p}
space, modulus of continuity, Walsh-Fourier coefficients, Walsh-Fourier series, partial sums, Lebesgue constants, Fejér means, Marcinkiewicz means, dyadic martingale, the one-dimensional Hardy space, the two-dimensional Hardy space, maximal operator, strong convergence.

Preface

The classical theory of Fourier series deals with decomposition of a
function into sinusoidal waves. Unlike these continuous waves the Vilenkin
(Walsh) functions are rectangular waves. Such waves have already been used
frequently in the theory of signal transmission, multiplexing, filtering,
image enhancement, codic theory, digital signal processing and pattern
recognition. The development of the theory of Vilenkin-Fourier series has
been strongly influenced by the classical theory of trigonometric series.
Because of this it is inevitable to compare results of Vilenkin series to
those on trigonometric series. There are many similarities between these
theories, but there exist differences also. Much of these can be explained
by modern abstract harmonic analysis, which studies orthonormal systems from the point of view of the structure of a topological group.

The problems studied in this PhD thesis are very important in Mathematical Analysis and its applications. In particular, we consider convergence and summability of partial sums, Fejér and Marcinkiewicz means with respect to the one- and two-dimensional Walsh-Fourier series in the martingale Hardy spaces. According to the problems considered in this PhD thesis, widely are used methods of real analysis combined with methods of abstract and non-linear harmonic analysis together with theory of approximation. Other research methods include theory of function spaces. They involve techniques which have been developed a great deal during the last three decades.

This PhD thesis consists the following chapters:

•Preliminaries

•Partial sums with respect to the one-dimensional Walsh-Fourier series on the martingale Hardy spaces

•Fejér means means with respect to the one-dimensional Walsh-Fourier series on the martingale Hardy spaces

•Convergence and summability of partial sums with respect to the two-dimensional Walsh-Fourier series on the martingale Hardy spaces

In Chapter 1 we first present some classical results and well-known facts, which are very important in the theory of Fourier analysis and is also very crucial for the further investigation of problems considered in this thesis. Moreover, there are presented results which are proved in the next chapters of this thesis and is emphasized the actuality of them, there is also shown some connections with known results.

In Chapter 2 we first define Walsh group and system, which are important to develop theory of harmonic analysis on locally compact Abelian groups. We consider some expressions and estimations of Lebesgue constants and Dirichlet kernels, give basic definitions and notation of the theory of martingale Hardy spaces and fundamental theorems which are very important to prove main results of this thesis. We also construct martingales which we use to prove sharpness of our positive results. Next, we find rate of convergence and divergence of the subsequences of partial sums with respect to the one-dimensional Walsh-Fourier series on the martingale Hardy spaces for
0<p\leq 1
. Finally, we apply these results to find necessary and sufficient conditions for the modulus of continuity which provide norm convergence of subsequences of the partial sums in the martingale Hardy spaces, for
0<p\leq 1
.

In Chapter 3 we investigate Fejér means with respect to the one-dimensional Walsh-Fourier series. First, we consider some expressions and estimations of Fejér kernels and find rate of convergence and divergence of the subsequences of Fejér means in the martingale Hardy spaces for
0<p\leq 1/2
. After that, we apply these results to find necessary and sufficient conditions for the modulus of continuity, which provide convergence of subsequences of Fejér means in the martingale Hardy spaces. Finally, we prove some new strong convergence theorems of Fejér means, for
0<p\leq 1/2
. We also prove sharpness of all our main results in this Chapter.

In Chapter 4 we investigate basic definitions and notation of partial sums and Marcinkiewicz means with respect to the two-dimensional Walsh-Fourier series. First, we consider some expressions and estimations of Marcinkiewicz kernels, give basic definitions and notation of the theory of the two-dimensional martingale Hardy spaces and fundamental theorems which are very important to prove main results of this thesis. Next, we present and prove strong convergence results of diagonal partial sums with respect to the two-dimensional Walsh-Fourier series in the martingale Hardy spaces for
0<p<1
. Moreover, we consider strong convergence results of Marcinkiewicz means with respect to the two-dimensional Walsh-Fourier series for
p=2/3
and find necessary and sufficient conditions for the modulus of continuity, which provide convergence in
H_{2/3}(G^{2})
norm of Marcinkiewicz means.

This PhD thesis is written as a monograph based on the following
publications:

[1] G. Tephnadze, Strong convergence of two-dimensional Walsh-Fourier series, Ukr. Math. J., 65, (6), (2013), 822-834.

[2] G. Tephnadze, Strong convergence theorems of Walsh-Fejér means, Acta Math. Hung., 142 (1) (2014), 244–259.

[3] K. Nagy and G. Tephnadze, Approximation by Walsh-Marcinkiewicz means on the Hardy space
H_{2/3}
, Kyoto J. Math., 54 (3), (2014), 641-652.

[4] G. Tephnadze, On the partial sums of Walsh-Fourier series, Colloquium Mathematicum, 141, 2 (2015), 227-242.

[5] G. Tephnadze, On the convergence of Fejér means of Walsh-Fourier series in the space
H_{p}
, J. Contemp. Math. Anal., 51, 2 (2016), 51-63.

[6] K. Nagy and G. Tephnadze, Strong convergence theorem for Walsh-Marcin- kiewicz means, Math. Inequal. Appl., 19, 1 (2016), 185–195.

1
Preliminaries

It is well-known that (for details see e.g.
[
30
]
and
[
47
]
) for every
p>1
there exists an absolute constant
c_{p}
, depending only on
p
, such that

\left\|S_{n}f\right\|_{p}\leq c_{p}\left\|f\right\|_{p},\text{ \ when \ }p>1\text{ \ and \ }f\in H_{1}({G}).

Moreover, Watari
[
76
]
(see also Gosselin
[
31
]
and Young
[
85
]
) proved that there exists an absolute constant
c
such that, for
n=1,2,...,

\displaystyle\lambda\mu\left(|S_{n}f|>\lambda\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle c\left\|f\right\|_{1},\ \ \ f\in L_{1}(G_{m}),\ \ \lambda>0.

On the other hand, it is also well-known that (for details see e.g.
[
1
]
and
[
47
]
) Walsh system is not Schauder basis in
L_{1}({G})
space. Moreover, there exists function
f\in H_{1}({G})
, such that partial sums with respect to Walsh system are not uniforml5y bounded in
L_{1}({G})
.

By applying Lebesgue constants

L(n):=\left\|D_{n}\right\|_{1}

we easily obtain that (for details see e.g.
[
2
]
and
[
47
]
) subsequences of partial sums
S_{n_{k}}f
with respect to Walsh system converge to
f
in
L_{1}
norm if and only if

\sup_{k\in\mathbb{N}}L(n_{k})\leq c<\infty.

(1.1)

Since
n
-th Lebesgue constant with respect to Walsh system, where

n=\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}n_{j}2^{j},(n_{j}\in Z_{2})

can be estimated by variation of natural number

V\left(n\right)=n_{0}+\overset{\infty}{\underset{k=1}{\sum}}\left|n_{k}-n_{k-1}\right|

and it is also well known that (for details see e.g.
[
12
]
and
[
47
]
) the following two-sided estimate is true

\frac{1}{8}V\left(n\right)\leq L(n)\leq V\left(n\right)

to obtain convergence of subsequences of partial sums
S_{n_{k}}f
with respect to Walsh system of
f\in L_{1}
in
f\in L_{1}
-norm.
Condition (
1.1
) can be replaced by

\sup_{k\in\mathbb{N}}V(n_{k})\leq c<\infty

It follows that (for details see e.g.
[
47
]
and
[
78
]
) subsequence of partial sums
S_{2^{n}}
are bounded from
H_{p}(G)
to
H_{p}(G)
for every
p>0
, from which we obtain that

\left\|S_{2^{n}}f-f\right\|_{H_{p}(G)}\rightarrow 0,\text{ \ as \ }n\rightarrow\infty,

(1.2)

On the other hand, (see e.g.
[
61
]
) there exist a martingale
f\in H_{p}(G)

\left(0<p<1\right),
such that

\underset{n\in\mathbb{N}}{\sup}\left\|S_{2^{n}+1}f\right\|_{weak-L_{p}(G)}=\infty.

The main reason of divergence of subsequence
S_{{2^{n}}+1}f
of partial sums it that (for details see
[
62
]
) Fourier coefficients of
f\in H_{p}(G)
are not uniformly bounded when
0<p<1
.

When
0<p<1
in
[
74
]
was investigated boundedness of subsequences of partial sums with respect to Walsh system from
H_{p}(G)
to
H_{p}(G)
. In particular, the following result is true:

Theorem T1.
Let
0<p<1
and
f\in H_{p}(G)
. Then there exists a absolute constant
c_{p},
depending only on
p
, such that

\left\|S_{m_{k}}f\right\|_{H_{p}(G)}\leq c_{p}\left\|f\right\|_{H_{p}(G)}

if and only if the following condition holds

\underset{k\in\mathbb{N}}{\sup}d\left(m_{k}\right)<c<\infty,

(1.3)

where

d\left(m_{k}\right):=\left|m_{k}\right|-\left\langle m_{k}\right\rangle.

In particular, Theorem T1 immediately follows:

Theorem T2.
Let
p>0
and
f\in H_{p}(G)
. Then there exists a absolute constant
c_{p},
depending only on
p
, such that

\left\|S_{2^{n}}f\right\|_{H_{p}(G)}\leq c_{p}\left\|f\right\|_{H_{p}(G)}

and

\left\|S_{2^{n}+2^{n-1}}f\right\|_{H_{p}(G)}\leq c_{p}\left\|f\right\|_{H_{p}(G)}.

On the other hand, we have the following result:

Theorem T3.
Let
p>0
. Then there exists a martingale
f\in H_{p}(G)
, such that

\sup_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\left\|S_{2^{n}+1}f\right\|_{{}_{H_{p}(G)}}=\infty.

Taking into account these results it is interesting to find behaviour of rate of divergence of subsequences of partials sums with respect to Walsh system of martingale
f\in H_{p}(G)
in the martingale Hardy spaces
H_{p}(G)
.

In the second chapter of this thesis (see also
[
63
]
) we investigate ebove mentioned problem. For
0<p<1
we have the following result:

Theorem
2.10
.
Let
f\in H_{p}(G)
. Then there exists a absolute constant
c_{p},
depending only on
p
, such that the following inequality is true

\text{ }\left\|S_{n}f\right\|_{H_{p}(G)}\leq c_{p}2^{d\left(n\right)\left(1/p-1\right)}\left\|f\right\|_{H_{p}(G)}.

(1.4)

On the other hand, if
0<p<1,

\left\{m_{k}:\text{ }k\geq 0\right\}
be increasing subsequence of natural numbers, such that

\sup_{k\in\mathbb{N}}d\left(m_{k}\right)=\infty

(1.5)

and

\Phi:\mathbb{N}_{+}\rightarrow[1,\infty)
be non-decreasing function satisfying the condition

\overline{\underset{k\rightarrow\infty}{\lim}}\frac{2^{d\left(m_{k}\right)\left(1/p-1\right)}}{\Phi\left(m_{k}\right)}=\infty,

then there exists a martingale
f\in H_{p}(G),
such that

\underset{k\in\mathbb{N}}{\sup}\left\|\frac{S_{m_{k}}f}{\Phi\left(m_{k}\right)}\right\|_{weak-L_{p}(G)}=\infty.

Theorem
2.11
easily follows the following corollary:

Corollary
2.11
.
Let
0<p<1
and
f\in H_{p}(G)
. Then there exists a absolute constant
c_{p},
depending only on
p
, such that

\text{ }\left\|S_{n}f\right\|_{H_{p}(G)}\leq c_{p}\left(n\mu\left\{\text{supp}\left(D_{n}\right)\right\}\right)^{1/p-1}\left\|f\right\|_{H_{p}(G)}.

On the other hand, if
0<p<1
and
\left\{m_{k}:\text{ }k\geq 0\right\}
be increasing sequence of natural numbers, such that

\sup_{k\in\mathbb{N}}m_{k}\mu\left\{\text{supp}\left(D_{m_{k}}\right)\right\}=\infty

and
\Phi:\mathbb{N}_{+}\rightarrow[1,\infty)

be non-decreasing function satisfying the condition

\overline{\underset{k\rightarrow\infty}{\lim}}\frac{\left(m_{k}\mu\left\{\text{supp}\left(D_{m_{k}}\right)\right\}\right)^{1/p-1}}{\Phi\left(m_{k}\right)}=\infty,

then there exists a martingale
f\in H_{p}(G),
such that

\underset{k\in\mathbb{N}}{\sup}\left\|\frac{S_{m_{k}}f}{\Phi\left(m_{k}\right)}\right\|_{weak-L_{p}(G)}=\infty.

In particular, we also get the proofs of Theorem T1 and Theorem T2.

In the second chapter of this thesis we also investigate case
p=1
. In this case the following result is true:

Theorem
2.12
.
Let
n\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
and
f\in H_{1}(G).
Then there exists a absolute constant
c,
such that

\left\|S_{n}f\right\|_{H_{1}(G)}\leq cV\left(n\right)\left\|f\right\|_{H_{1}(G)}.

(1.6)

Moreover, if
\left\{m_{k}:\text{ }k\geq 0\right\}
be increasing sequence of natural numbers
\mathbb{N}_{+},
such that

\sup_{k\in\mathbb{N}}V\left(m_{k}\right)=\infty

and
\Phi:\mathbb{N}_{+}\rightarrow[1,\infty)

be non-decreasing function satisfying the condition

\overline{\underset{k\rightarrow\infty}{\lim}}\frac{V\left(m_{k}\right)}{\Phi\left(m_{k}\right)}=\infty.

Then there exists a martingale
f\in H_{1}(G),
such that

\underset{{k\in\mathbb{N}}}{\sup}\left\|\frac{S_{m_{k}}f}{\Phi\left(m_{k}\right)}\right\|_{1}=\infty.

When
0<p<1
in
[
74
]
was proved boundedness of maximal operators of subsequences of partial sums from
H_{p}(G)
to
L_{p}(G)
. In particular, the following is true:

Theorem T4.
Let
0<p<1
and
f\in H_{p}(G)
. Then the maximal operator

\underset{k\in\mathbb{N}}{\sup}|S_{m_{k}}f|

is bounded from
H_{p}(G)
to
L_{p}(G)
, if and only if condition (
1.3
) is fulfilled.

In the special cases we obtain that the following is true:

Theorem T5.
Let
p>0
and
f\in H_{p}(G)
. Then there exists an absolute constant
c_{p},
depending only on
p
, such that

\left\|\sup_{n\in\mathbb{N}}|S_{2^{n}}f|\right\|_{p}\leq c_{p}\left\|f\right\|_{H_{p}(G)}

(1.7)

and

\left\|\sup_{n\in\mathbb{N}}|S_{2^{n}+2^{n-1}}f|\right\|_{p}\leq c_{p}\left\|f\right\|_{H_{p}(G)}.

On the other hand we have the following result:

Theorem T6.
Let
p>0
. Then there exists a martingale
f\in H_{p}(G)
, such that

\left\|\sup_{n\in\mathbb{N}}|S_{2^{n}+1}f|\right\|_{p}=\infty.

Above mentioned condition (
1.3
) is sufficient condition for the case
p=1
also, but there exist subsequences which do not satisfy this condition, but maximal operators of these subsequences of partial sums with respect to Walsh system are not bounded from
H_{1}(G)
to
L_{1}(G)
.

Such necessary and sufficient conditions which provides boundedness of maximal operators of subsequences of partial sums with respect to Walsh system from
H_{1}(G)
to
L_{1}(G)
is open problem.

In
[
62
]
and
[
74
]
was investigated boundedness of weighted maximal operators from
H_{p}(G)
to
L_{p}(G)
, when
0<p\leq 1
:

Theorem T7.
Let
0<p\leq 1
. Then weighted maximal operator

\overset{\sim}{S}_{p}^{\ast}f:=\underset{n\in\mathbb{N_{+}}}{\sup}\frac{\left|S_{n}f\right|}{\left(n+1\right)^{1/p-1}\log^{[p]}\left(n+1\right)}

is bounded from
H_{p}(G)
to
L_{p}(G)
, where
[p]
denotes integer part of
p
.

Moreover, for any non-decreasing function
\varphi:\mathbb{N}_{+}\rightarrow[1,

\infty)
satisfying the condition

\overline{\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}}\frac{\left(n+1\right)^{1/p-1}\log^{[p]}\left(n+1\right)}{\varphi\left(n+1\right)}=+\infty,

there exists a martingale
f\in H_{p}(G)

(0<p\leq 1),
such that

\sup_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\left\|\frac{S_{n}f}{\varphi\left(n\right)}\right\|_{p}=\infty.

According to negative result for weighted maximal operator of partial sums of Walsh-Fourier series we immediately get the following result:

Theorem S1.
There exists a martingale
f\in H_{p}(G)
,
(0<p\leq 1)
, such that

\underset{n\in\mathbb{N}}{\sup}\left\|S_{n}f\right\|_{p}=\infty.

On the other hand, boundedness of weighted maximal operators immediately follows the following estimation:

Theorem S2.
Let
0<p\leq 1
. Then there exists a absolute constant
c_{p}
, depending only on
p
, such that

\left\|S_{n}f\right\|_{p}\leq c_{p}{(n+1)^{1/p-1}\log^{[p]}(n+1)}\left\|f\right\|_{H_{p}(G)},\text{ \ for \ }0<p\leq 1,

where
[p]
denotes integer part of
p
.

By applying this inequality (see
[
60
]
) we find necessary and sufficient conditions for martingale
f\in H_{p}(G)
for which partial sums with respect to Walsh system of martingale
f\in H_{p}(G)
converge in
H_{p}(G)
norm.

Theorem T8.
Let
0<p\leq 1
,
[p]
denotes integer part of
p
,
f\in H_{p}(G)
and

\omega_{H_{p}(G)}\left(\frac{1}{2^{N}},f\right)=o\left(\frac{1}{2^{N(1/p-1)}N^{[p]}}\right),\text{ \ as \ }N\rightarrow\infty.

Then

\left\|S_{n}f-f\right\|_{p}\rightarrow 0,\text{ as }n\rightarrow\infty.

Moreover, there exists a martingale
f\in H_{p}(G)
, where
0<p<1
, such that

\omega_{H_{p}(G)}\left(\frac{1}{2^{N}},f\right)=O\left(\frac{1}{2^{N(1/p-1)}N^{[p]}}\right),\text{ \ as \ }N\rightarrow\infty

and

\left\|S_{n}f-f\right\|_{weak-L_{p}(G)}\nrightarrow 0,\,\,\,\text{ \ \ as \ \ }n\rightarrow\infty.

By taking these results into account, it is interesting to find necessary and sufficient conditions for modulus of continuity, such that subsequences of partial sums with respect to Walsh system of martingale
f\in H_{p}(G)
converge in
H_{p}(G)
norm.

In the second chapter of this thesis (see also
[
63
]
) we investigate this problem. By combining inequalities (
1.4
) and (
1.6
) we get the following theorem:

Theorem
2.16
.
Let
2^{k}<n\leq 2^{k+1}.
Then there exists an absolute constant
c_{p},
depending only on
p
, such that

\left\|S_{n}f-f\right\|_{H_{p}(G)}\leq c_{p}2^{d\left(n\right)\left(1/p-1\right)}\omega_{H_{p}(G)}\left(\frac{1}{2^{k}},f\right),\text{ \ \ \ }\left(0<p<1\right)

(1.8)

and

\left\|S_{n}f-f\right\|_{H_{1}(G)}\leq c_{1}V\left(n\right)\omega_{H_{1}(G)}\left(\frac{1}{2^{k}},f\right)

(1.9)

By applying inequality (
1.8
) the following result is proved in the second chapter:

Theorem
2.17
.
Let
0<p<1,

f\in H_{p}(G)
and
\{m_{k}:k\geq 0\}
be increasing sequence of natural number satisfying the condition

\omega_{H_{p}(G)}\left(\frac{1}{2^{\left|m_{k}\right|}},f\right)=o\left(\frac{1}{2^{d\left(m_{k}\right)\left(1/p-1\right)}}\right)\text{ as \ }k\rightarrow\infty.

Then

\left\|S_{m_{k}}f-f\right\|_{H_{p}(G)}\rightarrow 0\text{ \ as \ }k\rightarrow\infty.

(1.10)

On the other hand, if
\{m_{k}:k\geq 0\}
be increasing sequence of natural numbers satisfying the condition (
1.5
), then there exists a martingale
f\in H_{p}(G)
and subsequence
\{\alpha_{k}:k\geq 0\}\subset\{m_{k}:k\geq 0\},
for which

\omega_{H_{p}(G)}\left(\frac{1}{2^{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|}},f\right)=O\left(\frac{1}{2^{d\left(\alpha_{k}\right)\left(1/p-1\right)}}\right)\text{ as \ }k\rightarrow\infty\text{\ }

and

\limsup\limits_{k\rightarrow\infty}\left\|S_{\alpha_{k}}f-f\right\|_{weak-L_{p}(G)}>c_{p}>0\,\,\,\text{as\thinspace\thinspace\thinspace}k\rightarrow\infty,

(1.11)

where
c_{p}
is an absolute constant depending only on
p
.

According to this theorem we immediately get that the following result is true:

Corollary
2.18
.
Let
0<p<1,

f\in H_{p}(G)
and
\{m_{k}:k\geq 0\}
be increasing sequence of natural number, satisfying the condition

\omega_{H_{p}(G)}\left(\frac{1}{2^{\left|m_{k}\right|}},f\right)=o\left(\frac{1}{\left(m_{k}\mu\left(\text{supp}D_{m_{k}}\right)\right)^{1/p-1}}\right),\text{ \ as \ }k\rightarrow\infty.

Then (
1.10
) holds.

On the other hand, if
\{m_{k}:k\geq 0\}
be increasing sequence of natural number, satisfying the condition

\overline{\underset{k\rightarrow\infty}{\lim}}\frac{\left(m_{k}\mu\left\{\text{supp}\left(D_{m_{k}}\right)\right\}\right)^{1/p-1}}{\Phi\left(m_{k}\right)}=\infty,

then there exists a martingale
f\in H_{p}(G)
and subsequence
\{\alpha_{k}:k\geq 0\}\subset\{m_{k}:k\geq 0\}
such that

\omega_{H_{p}(G)}\left(\frac{1}{2^{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|}},f\right)=O\left(\frac{1}{\left(\alpha_{k}\mu\left(\text{supp}D_{\alpha_{k}}\right)\right)^{1/p-1}}\right),\text{\ \ as \ }k\rightarrow\infty

and (
1.11
) holds.

By applying (
1.9
) we prove that the following is true:

Theorem
2.19
.
Let
f\in H_{1}(G)
and
\{m_{k}:k\geq 0\}
be increasing sequence of natural number, satisfying the condition

\omega_{H_{1}(G)}\left(\frac{1}{2^{\left|m_{k}\right|}},f\right)=o\left(\frac{1}{V\left(m_{k}\right)}\right)\text{ \ as \ }k\rightarrow\infty.

Then

\left\|S_{m_{k}}f-f\right\|_{H_{1}(G)}\rightarrow 0\text{ \ as \ }k\rightarrow\infty.

Moreover, if
\{m_{k}:k\geq 0\}
be increasing sequence of natural number, satisfying the condition (
1.5
), then there exists a martingale
f\in H_{1}(G)
and subsequence
\{\alpha_{k}:k\geq 0\}\subset\{m_{k}:k\geq 0\}
for which

\omega_{H_{1}(G)}\left(\frac{1}{2^{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|}},f\right)=O\left(\frac{1}{V\left(\alpha_{k}\right)}\right)\text{ \ as
\ }k\rightarrow\infty

and

\limsup\limits_{k\rightarrow\infty}\left\|S_{\alpha_{k}}f-f\right\|_{1}>c>0\text{ \ \ as \ \ }k\rightarrow\infty,

where
c
is an absolute constant.

By applying Theorem
2.17
and Theorem
2.19
we immediately get proof of Theorem T8.

Weisz
[
79
]
consider convergence in norm of Fejér means of the one-dimensional Walsh-Fourier and proved the following:

Theorem We1.
Let
p>1/2
and
f\in H_{p}(G)
. Then there exists a absolute constant
c_{p}
, depending only on
p
, such that

\left\|\sigma_{k}f\right\|_{H_{p}(G)}\leq c_{p}\left\|f\right\|_{H_{p}(G)}.

Weisz (for details see e.g.
[
78
]
) also consider boundedness of subsequences of Fejér means
\sigma_{2^{n}}
of the one-dimensional Walsh-Fourier series from
H_{p}(G)
to
H_{p}(G)
when
p>0
:

Theorem We2.
Let
p>0
and
f\in H_{p}(G)
. Then

\left\|\sigma_{2^{k}}f-f\right\|_{H_{p}(G)}\rightarrow 0,\text{\ \ as \ \ }k\rightarrow\infty.

(1.12)

On the other hand, in
[
56
]
was proved the following result:

Theorem T9.
There exists a martingale
f\in H_{p}(G)

\left(0<p\leq 1/2\right)
such that

\underset{n\in\mathbb{N}}{\sup}\left\|\sigma_{2^{n}+1}f\right\|_{H_{p}(G)}=\infty.

Goginava
[
27
]
(see also
[
44
]
) proved that the following result is true:

Theorem Gog1.
Let
0<p\leq 1.
Then the sequence of operators
\left|\sigma_{2^{n}}f\right|
are not bounded from
H_{p}(G)
to
H_{p}(G)
.

When
0<p<1/2
then in
[
45
]
was proved bondedness of subsequences of Fejér means of the one-dimensional Walsh-Fourier from
H_{p}(G)
to
H_{p}(G)
. In particular, the following is true:

Theorem T10.
Let
0<p<1/2
and
f\in H_{p}(G)
. Then there exists a absolute constant
c_{p}
, depending only on
p
, such that

\left\|\sigma_{m_{k}}f\right\|_{H_{p}(G)}\leq c_{p}\left\|f\right\|_{H_{p}(G)}

estimation holds if and only if the condition (
1.3
) is fulfilled.

Theorem T10 immediately follows theorem of Weisz (see Theorem We2) and and also interesting results:

Theorem T11.
Let
p>0
and
f\in H_{p}(G)
. Then there exists an absolute constant
c_{p},
depending only on
p
, such that

\left\|\sigma_{2^{n}}f\right\|_{H_{p}(G)}\leq c_{p}\left\|f\right\|_{H_{p}(G)}

and

\left\|\sigma_{2^{n}+2^{n-1}}f\right\|_{H_{p}(G)}\leq c_{p}\left\|f\right\|_{H_{p}(G)}.

On the other hand, we have the following result:

Theorem T12.
Let
p>0
. Then there exists a martingale
f\in H_{p}(G)
, such that

\sup_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\left\|\sigma_{2^{n}+1}f\right\|_{{}_{H_{p}(G)}}=\infty.

According to above mentioned results it is interesting to find rate of divergence of subsequences
\sigma_{n_{k}}f
of Fejér means of the one-dimensional Walsh-Fourier series in the Hardy spaces
H_{p}(G)
.

In the third chapter of this thesis (see also
[
64
]
) we find rate of divergence of subsequences of Fejér means of the one-dimensional Walsh-Fourier series on the martingale Hardy spaces
H_{p}(G)
, when

0<p\leq 1/2
.

First, we consider case
p=1/2
:

Theorem
3.28
.
Let
n\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
and
f\in H_{1/2}(G).
Then there exists an absolute constant
c,
such that

\left\|\sigma_{n}f\right\|_{H_{1/2}(G)}\leq c{{V}^{2}}\left(n\right)\left\|f\right\|_{H_{1/2}(G)}.

(1.13)

Moreover, if
\left\{m_{k}:\text{ }k\geq 0\right\}
be increasing secuence of natural numbers, such that

\sup_{k\in\mathbb{N}}V\left(m_{k}\right)=\infty

and
\Phi:\mathbb{N}_{+}\rightarrow[1,\infty]

be non-decreasing function satisfying the conditions

\overline{\underset{k\rightarrow\infty}{\lim}}\frac{{V^{2}}\left(m_{k}\right)}{\Phi\left(m_{k}\right)}=\infty.

then there exists a martingale
f\in H_{1/2}(G),
such that

\underset{{k\in\mathbb{N}}}{\sup}\left\|\frac{\sigma_{m_{k}}f}{\Phi\left(m_{k}\right)}\right\|_{1/2}=\infty.

There was also considered case
0<p<1/2
and was proved that the following is true:

Theorem
3.29
.
Let
0<p<1/2
and
f\in H_{p}(G)
. Then there exists an absolute constant
c_{p},
depending only on
p
such that

\text{ }\left\|\sigma_{n}f\right\|_{H_{p}(G)}\leq c_{p}2^{d\left(n\right)\left(1/p-2\right)}\left\|f\right\|_{H_{p}(G)}.

(1.14)

On the other hand, if
0<p<1/2,

\left\{m_{k}:\text{ }k\geq 0\right\}
be increasing sequence of natural numbers satisfying the condition (
1.5
) and

\Phi:\mathbb{N}_{+}\rightarrow[1,\infty)
be non-decreasing function such that

\overline{\underset{k\rightarrow\infty}{\lim}}\frac{2^{d\left(m_{k}\right)\left(1/p-2\right)}}{\Phi\left(m_{k}\right)}=\infty,

then there exists a martingale
f\in H_{p}(G),
such that

\underset{k\in\mathbb{N}}{\sup}\left\|\frac{\sigma_{m_{k}}f}{\Phi\left(m_{k}\right)}\right\|_{weak-L_{p}(G)}=\infty.

From these results also follows proof of Theorem We2.

In 1975 Schipp
[
46
]
(see also
[
86
]
) proved that the maximal operator of Fejér means
\sigma^{*}
is of type weak-(1,1):

\mu\left(\sigma^{*}f>\lambda\right)\leq\frac{c}{\lambda}\left\|f\right\|_{1},\text{ \qquad}\left(\lambda>0\right).

By using Marcinkiewicz interpolation theorem it follows that
\sigma^{*}
is of strong type-
(p,p)
, when
p>1:

\left\|\sigma^{*}f\right\|_{p}\leq c\left\|f\right\|_{p},\text{ \qquad}\left(p>1\right).

The boundedness does not hold for
p=1,
but Fujji
[
15
]
(see also
[
84
]
) proved that maximal operator of Fejér means is bounded from
H_{1}(G)
to
L_{1}(G)
. Weisz in
[
80
]
generalized result of Fujii and proved that maximal operator of Fejér means is bounded from
H_{p}(G)
to
L_{p}(G)
, when
p>1/2.
Simon
[
48
]
construct the counterexample, which shows that boundedness does not hold when
0<p<1/2
. Goginava
[
21
]
(see also
[
9
]
and
[
10
]
) generalized this result for
0<p\leq 1/2
and proved that the following is true:

Theorem Gog2.
There exists a martingale
f\in H_{p}(G)

\left(0<p\leq 1/2\right)
such that

\underset{n\in\mathbb{N}}{\sup}\left\|\sigma_{n}f\right\|_{p}=\infty.

Weisz
[
81
]
(see also Goginava
[
23
]
) proved that the following is true:

Theorem We3.
Let
f\in H_{1/2}(G)
. Then there exists an absolute constant
c,
such that

\left\|\sigma^{*}f\right\|_{weak-L_{1/2}(G)}\leq c\left\|f\right\|_{H_{1/2}(G)}.

In
[
45
]
was considered boundedness of maximal operators of subsequences of Fejér means of the one-dimensional Walsh-Fourier series from
H_{p}(G)
to
L_{p}(G)
for
0<p<1/2
. In particular, the following is true:

Theorem T13.
Let
0<p<1/2
and
f\in H_{p}(G)
. Then the maximal operator

\overset{\sim}{\sigma}^{*}f:=\underset{k\in\mathbb{N}}{\sup}|\sigma_{m_{k}}f|

is bounded from
H_{p}(G)
to
L_{p}(G)
if and only if when condition (
1.3
) is fulfilled.

As consequences the following results are true:

Theorem T14.
Let
p>0
and
f\in H_{p}(G)
. Then there exists an absolute constant
c_{p}
depending only on
p,
such that

\left\|\sup_{n\in\mathbb{N}}|\sigma_{2^{n}}f|\right\|_{p}\leq c_{p}\left\|f\right\|_{H_{p}(G)}

(1.15)

and

\left\|\sup_{n\in\mathbb{N}}|\sigma_{2^{n}+2^{n-1}}f|\right\|_{p}\leq c_{p}\left\|f\right\|_{H_{p}(G)}.

On the other hand, we have the following negative result:

Theorem T15.
Let
0<p<1/2.
Then there exists a martingale
f\in H_{p}(G)
, such that

\left\|\sup_{n\in\mathbb{N}}|\sigma_{2^{n}+1}f|\right\|_{p}=\infty.

above mentioned condition is sufficient for the case
p=1/2
also, but there exists subsequences, which do not satisfy condition (
1.3
), but maximal operator of subsequences of Fejér means of the one-dimensional Walsh-Fourier series are bounded from
H_{1/2}(G)
to
L_{1/2}(G).

However, it is open problem to find necessary and sufficient conditions on the indexes, which provide boundedness of maximal operator of subsequences of Fejér means of the one-dimensional Walsh-Fourier series from
H_{1/2}(G)
to
L_{1/2}(G)
.

In
[
22
]
and
[
56
]
(see also
[
43
]
,
[
58
]
,
[
29
]
and
[
55
]
) is proved that the following is true:

Theorem GT1.
Let
0<p\leq 1/2
and
f\in H_{p}(G)
. Then the maximal operator

\overset{\sim}{\sigma}_{p}^{\ast}f:=\underset{n\in\mathbb{N}}{\sup}\frac{\left|\sigma_{n}f\right|}{\left(n+1\right)^{1/p-2}\log^{2[1/2+p]}\left(n+1\right)}

is bounded from
H_{p}(G)
to
L_{p}(G)
.

Moreover, for any nondecreasing function
\varphi:\mathbb{N}_{+}\rightarrow[1,

\infty)
satisfying the condition

\overline{\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}}\frac{\left(n+1\right)^{1/p-2}\log^{2[1/2+p]}\left(n+1\right)}{\varphi\left(n\right)}=+\infty,

there exists a martingale
f\in H_{p}(G)
,
(0<p<1/2)
such that

\sup_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\left\|\frac{\sigma_{n}f}{\varphi\left(n\right)}\right\|_{p}=\infty.

From the divergence of weighted maximal operators we immediately get that there exists a martingale
f\in H_{p}(G)

(0<p\leq 1/2)
, such that

\underset{n\in\mathbb{N}}{\sup}\left\|\sigma_{n}f\right\|_{p}=\infty.

and from the boundedness results of weighted maximal operators we immediately get that for any
f\in H_{p}(G)
there exists an absolute constant
c_{p}
, such that the following inequality holds true:

\left\|\sigma_{n}f\right\|_{p}\leq c_{p}{n^{1/p-2}\log^{2[1/2+p]}\left(n+1\right)}\left\|f\right\|_{H_{p}(G)},\text{ \ as \ }0<p\leq 1/2.

(1.16)

By applying inequality (
1.16
) in
[
60
]
was found necessary and sufficient conditions for modulus of continuity of martingale
f\in H_{p}(G)
, for which Fejér means of the one-dimensional Walsh-Fourier series converge in
H_{p}(G)
norm.

Theorem T16.
Let
0<p\leq 1/2
,
f\in H_{p}(G)
and

\omega_{H_{p}(G)}\left(\frac{1}{2^{N}},f\right)=o\left(\frac{1}{2^{N(1/p-2)}N^{2[1/2+p]}}\right),\text{\ as \ }N\rightarrow\infty.

Then

\left\|\sigma_{n}f-f\right\|_{p}\rightarrow 0,\text{ as }n\rightarrow\infty.

Moreover, there exists a martingale
f\in H_{p}(G)
, for which

\omega_{H_{1/2}(G)}\left(\frac{1}{2^{N}},f\right)=O\left(\frac{1}{2^{N(1/p-2)}N^{2[1/2+p]}}\right),\text{ \ as \ }N\rightarrow\infty

and

\left\|\sigma_{n}f-f\right\|_{p}\nrightarrow 0,\,\,\,\text{as\ \ }n\rightarrow\infty.

According above mentioned results, it is interesting to find necessary and sufficient conditions for the modulus of continuity, for which subsequences
\sigma_{n_{k}}f
of Fejér means of the one-dimensional Walsh-Fourier series converge in
H_{p}(G)
norm.

In the third chapter of this thesis we find necessary and sufficient conditions for the modulus of continuity, for which subsequences
\sigma_{n_{k}}f
of Fejér means of the one-dimensional Walsh-Fourier series converge in
H_{p}(G)
norm (see also
[
64
]
).

By applying inequality (
1.13
) for the case
p=1/2
the following necessary and sufficient conditions are found:

Theorem
3.33
.
Let
f\in H_{1/2}(G)
and
\{m_{k}:k\geq 0\}
be increasing sequence of natural numbers, such that

\omega_{H_{1/2}(G)}\left(\frac{1}{2^{\left|m_{k}\right|}},f\right)=o\left(\frac{1}{{V^{2}}\left(m_{k}\right)}\right)\text{ \ as \ }k\rightarrow\infty.

Then

\left\|\sigma_{m_{k}}f-f\right\|_{H_{1/2}(G)}\rightarrow 0\text{ \ as \ }k\rightarrow\infty.

Moreover, if
\{m_{k}:k\geq 0\}
be increasing sequence of natural numbers, such that (
1.5
) holds true, then there exists a martingale
f\in H_{1/2}(G)
and subsequence
\{\alpha_{k}:k\geq 0\}\subset\{m_{k}:k\geq 0\}
such that

\omega_{H_{1/2}(G)}\left(\frac{1}{2^{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|}},f\right)=O\left(\frac{1}{{V^{2}}\left(\alpha_{k}\right)}\right)\text{ \ as\ }k\rightarrow\infty

and

\limsup\limits_{k\rightarrow\infty}\left\|\sigma_{\alpha_{k}}f-f\right\|_{1/2}>c>0\,\,\,\text{as\thinspace\thinspace\thinspace}k\rightarrow\infty,

wher
c
is an absolute constant.

By applying inequality (
1.14
) we also investigate case
0<p<1/2
. In the third chapter of this thesis we prove that the following is true:

Theorem
3.34
.
Let
0<p<1/2,

f\in H_{p}(G)
and
\{m_{k}:k\geq 0\}
be increasing sequence of natural numbers, such that

\omega_{H_{p}(G)}\left(\frac{1}{2^{\left|m_{k}\right|}},f\right)=o\left(\frac{1}{2^{d\left(m_{k}\right)\left(1/p-2\right)}}\right),\text{ as \ }k\rightarrow\infty.

Then

\left\|\sigma_{m_{k}}f-f\right\|_{H_{p}(G)}\rightarrow 0,\text{ \ as \ }k\rightarrow\infty.

On the other hand, if
\{m_{k}:k\geq 0\}
be increasing sequence of natural numbers satisfying the condition (
1.5
), then there exists a martingale
f\in H_{p}(G)
and subsequence
\{\alpha_{k}:k\geq 0\}\subset\{m_{k}:k\geq 0\},
for which

\omega_{H_{p}(G)}\left(\frac{1}{2^{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|}},f\right)=O\left(\frac{1}{2^{d\left(\alpha_{k}\right)\left(1/p-2\right)}}\right),\text{ as \ }k\rightarrow\infty

and

\limsup\limits_{k\rightarrow\infty}\left\|\sigma_{\alpha_{k}}f-f\right\|_{weak-L_{p}(G)}>c_{p}>0,\text{\quad as\quad}k\rightarrow\infty,

where
c_{p}
is constant depending only on
p
.

However, Simon in
[
49
]
and
[
51
]
(see also
[
13
,
52
]
) consider strong convergence theorems of the one-dimensional Walsh-Fouriere series and proved the following:

Theorem Si1.
Let
0<p\leq 1
and
f\in H_{1}\left(G\right)
. Then there exists an absolute constant
c_{p}
depending only on
p,
such that the following inequality is true:

\frac{1}{\log^{[p]}n}\overset{n}{\underset{k=1}{\sum}}\frac{\left\|S_{k}f\right\|_{H_{p}(G)}}{k^{2-p}}\leq c_{p}\left\|f\right\|_{H_{p}(G)},

Analigical result for trigonometric system was proved in
[
53
]
, for unbounded Walsh systems in
[
17
]
.

In
[
57
]
was proved that the following is true:

Theorem T17.
for any
0<p<1
and non-decreasing function
\varphi:\mathbb{N}_{+}\rightarrow[1,

\infty)
satisfying the condition

\overline{\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}}\frac{{n^{2-p}}}{\varphi\left(n\right)}=+\infty,

there exists a martingale
f\in H_{p}(G)
, such that

\text{ }\underset{k=1}{\overset{\infty}{\sum}}\frac{\left\|S_{k}f\right\|_{weak-L_{p}(G)}^{p}}{\varphi(k)}=\infty,\text{\qquad}(0<p<1).

Theorem Si1 follows that if
f\in H_{1}(G)
then the following equalities are true:

\underset{n\rightarrow\infty}{\lim}\frac{1}{\log n}\overset{n}{\underset{k=1}{\sum}}\frac{\left\|S_{k}f-f\right\|_{1}}{k}=0

and

\underset{n\rightarrow\infty}{\lim}\frac{1}{\log n}\overset{n}{\underset{k=1}{\sum}}\frac{\left\|S_{k}f\right\|_{1}}{k}=\left\|f\right\|_{H_{1}(G)}.

When
0<p<1
and
f\in H_{p}(G),
then Theorem Si1 follows that there exists an absolute constant
c_{p}
depending only on
p,
such that

\frac{1}{n^{1/2-p/2}}\overset{n}{\underset{k=1}{\sum}}\frac{\left\|S_{k}f\right\|_{H_{p}(G)}^{p}}{k^{3/2-p/2}}\leq c_{p}\left\|f\right\|_{H_{p}(G)}^{p}.

Moreover,

\frac{1}{n^{1/2-p/2}}\overset{n}{\underset{k=1}{\sum}}\frac{\left\|S_{k}f-f\right\|_{H_{p}(G)}^{p}}{k^{{}^{3/2-p}}}=0.

It follows the following equality

\frac{1}{n^{1/2-p/2}}\overset{n}{\underset{k=1}{\sum}}\frac{\left\|S_{k}f\right\|_{H_{p}(G)}^{p}}{k^{3/2-p/2}}=\left\|f\right\|_{H_{p}(G)}^{p}.

In the third chapter of this thesis we consider strong convergence results of Fejér means of the one-dimensional Walsh-Fourier series. According to Theorem We1 and Theorem Gog2 we only have to consider case
0<p\leq 1/2
(for details see
[
59
]
, see also
[
4
]
,
[
5
]
,
[
7
]
,
[
8
]
,
[
12
]
):

Theorem
3.37
.
Let
0<p\leq 1/2
and
f\in H_{p}(G)
. Then there exists an absolute constant
c_{p}
depending only on
p,
such that

\frac{1}{\log^{\left[1/2+p\right]}n}\overset{n}{\underset{m=1}{\sum}}\frac{\left\|\sigma_{m}f\right\|_{H_{p}(G)}^{p}}{m^{2-2p}}\leq c_{p}\left\|f\right\|_{H_{p}(G)}^{p}.

Moreover, let
0<p<1/2
and
\Phi:\mathbb{N}_{+}\rightarrow[1,

\infty)
be non-decreasing, non-negative function, such that
\Phi\left(n\right)\uparrow\infty
and

\overline{\underset{k\rightarrow\infty}{\lim}}\frac{k^{2-2p}}{\Phi\left({k}\right)}=\infty.

Then there exists a martingale
f\in H_{p}(G),
such that

\underset{m=1}{\overset{\infty}{\sum}}\frac{\left\|\sigma_{m}f\right\|_{weak-L_{p}(G)}^{p}}{\Phi\left(m\right)}=\infty.

When
p=1/2
is was also proved that the following is true:

Theorem
3.38
.
Let
f\in H_{1/2}(G).
Then

\underset{n\in\mathbf{\mathbb{N}}_{+}}{\sup}\underset{\left\|f\right\|_{H_{p}(G)}\leq 1}{\sup}\frac{1}{n}\underset{m=1}{\overset{n}{\sum}}\left\|\sigma_{m}f\right\|_{1/2}^{1/2}=\infty.

Theorem
3.37
follows that if
f\in H_{1/2}(G)
then the following equalities are true:

\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}\frac{1}{\log n}\overset{n}{\underset{k=1}{\sum}}\frac{\left\|\sigma_{k}f-f\right\|_{H_{1/2}(G)}^{1/2}}{k}=0

and

\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}\frac{1}{\log n}\overset{n}{\underset{k=1}{\sum}}\frac{\left\|\sigma_{k}f\right\|_{H_{1/2}(G)}^{1/2}}{k}=\left\|f\right\|_{H_{1/2}(G)}^{1/2}.

When
0<p<1/2
and
f\in H_{p}(G),
then Theorem
3.37
follows that there exists an absolute constant
c_{p}
depending only on
p,
such that

\frac{1}{n^{1/2-p}}\overset{n}{\underset{k=1}{\sum}}\frac{\left\|\sigma_{k}f\right\|_{H_{p}(G)}^{p}}{k^{3/2-p}}\leq c_{p}\left\|f\right\|_{H_{p}(G)}^{p}.

Moreover,

\frac{1}{n^{1/2-p}}\overset{n}{\underset{k=1}{\sum}}\frac{\left\|\sigma_{k}f-f\right\|_{H_{p}(G)}^{p}}{k^{{}^{3/2-p}}}=0.

It follows that

\frac{1}{n^{1/2-p}}\overset{n}{\underset{k=1}{\sum}}\frac{\left\|\sigma_{k}f\right\|_{H_{p}(G)}^{p}}{k^{3/2-p}}=\left\|f\right\|_{H_{p}(G)}^{p}.

For the two-dimensional case (for details see e.g.
[
47
]
and
[
78
]
) the following is true:

Theorem S3.
Let
p>0
and
f\in H_{p}({G}^{2})
. Then

\left\|S_{2^{n},2^{n}}f-f\right\|_{p}\rightarrow 0,\text{ \ as \ }n\rightarrow\infty.

(1.17)

Moreover,

Theorem S4.
Let
p>0
and
f\in H_{p}({G}^{2})
. Then there exists an absolute constant
c_{p}
depending only on
p,
such that

\left\|\sup_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\left|S_{2^{n},2^{n}}f\right|\right\|_{p}\leq c_{p}\left\|f\right\|_{H_{p}({G}^{2})},

(1.18)

By applying Theorem S4 we can conclude that the following holds true (for details see e.g.
[
47
]
and
[
78
]
):

Theorem S5.
Let
p>0
and
f\in H_{p}({G}^{2})
. Then there exists an absolute constant
c_{p}
depending only on
p,
such that

\left\|S_{2^{n},2^{n}}f\right\|_{H_{p}({G}^{2})}\leq c_{p}\left\|f\right\|_{H_{p}({G}^{2})}.

(1.19)

On the other hand, (see
[
61
]
) the following is true:

Theorem T18.
Let
0<p\leq 1.
Then there exists a martingale
f\in H_{p}({G}^{2})
such that

\underset{n\in\mathbb{N}}{\sup}\left\|S_{n,n}f\right\|_{p}=\infty.

However, for the two-dimensional case Weisz
[
77
]
proved the following:

Theorem We4.
Let
\alpha\geq 0
and
f\in H_{p}({G}^{2}).
Then there exists an absolute constant
c_{p}
depending only on
p,
such that

\underset{n,m\geq 2}{\sup}\left(\frac{1}{\log n\log m}\right)^{\left[p\right]}\underset{2^{-\alpha}\leq k/l\leq 2^{\alpha},\text{ }\left(k,l\right)\leq\left(n,m\right)}{\sum}\frac{\left\|S_{k,l}f\right\|_{p}^{p}}{\left(kl\right)^{2-p}}\leq c_{p}\left\|f\right\|_{H_{p}({G}^{2})}^{p},

where
0<p\leq 1
and
\left[p\right]
denotes integer part of real number
p
.

Moreover, sharpness of of the rates of weights are proved
[
72
]
was proved that rate of the
{\left(kl\right)^{2-p}}
is sharp.

Goginava and Gogoladze in
[
28
]
generalized this result in the case when
\alpha=0
:

Theorem GG1.
Let
f\in H_{1}({G}^{2})
. Then there exists an absolute constant
c
, such that

\sum\limits_{n=1}^{\infty}\frac{\left\|S_{n,n}f\right\|_{1}}{n\log^{2}n}\leq c\left\|f\right\|_{H_{1}{({G}^{2})}}.

In
[
65
]
was proved that rate of the weights
\left\{n\log^{2}n\right\}_{n=1}^{\infty}
is sharp. The following is true:

Theorem T19.
Let

\Phi:\mathbb{N}\rightarrow[1,

\infty)
be non-decreasing function satisfying the condition
\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}\Phi\left(n\right)=+\infty.
Then

\underset{\left\|f\right\|_{H_{1}({G}^{2})}\leq 1}{\sup}\underset{n=1}{\overset{\infty}{\sum}}\frac{\left\|S_{n,n}f\right\|_{1}\Phi\left(n\right)}{n\log^{2}\left(n+1\right)}=\infty.

Theorem GG1 follows that if
f\in H_{1}({G}^{2}),
then

\frac{1}{\log^{1/2}n}\overset{n}{\underset{k=1}{\sum}}\frac{\left\|S_{k,k}f\right\|_{H_{1}({G}^{2})}}{k\log^{3/2}k}\leq c\left\|f\right\|_{H_{1}({G}^{2})},\text{ }

Moreover,

\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}\frac{1}{\log^{1/2}n}\overset{n}{\underset{k=1}{\sum}}\frac{\left\|S_{k,k}f-f\right\|_{H_{1}({G}^{2})}^{2/3}}{k\log^{3/2}k}=0

It follows the following equality

\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}\frac{1}{\log^{1/2}n}\overset{n}{\underset{k=1}{\sum}}\frac{\left\|S_{k,k}f\right\|_{H_{2/3}({G}^{2})}^{2/3}}{k{\log^{3/2}k}}=\left\|f\right\|_{H_{2/3}({G}^{2})}^{2/3}.

In the fourth chapter (see also
[
66
]
) of this thesis we consider strong convergence of Marcinkiewicz means with respect to the two-dimensional partial sums of Walsh-Fourier series when
0<p<1
:

Theorem
4.49
Let
0<p<1
and
f\in H_{p}({G}^{2}).
Then there exists an absolute constant
c_{p}
depending only on
p,
such that

\sum\limits_{n=1}^{\infty}\frac{\left\|S_{n,n}f\right\|_{p}^{p}}{n^{3-2p}}\leq c_{p}\left\|f\right\|_{H_{p}({G}^{2})}^{p}.

(1.20)

Moreover, if
0<p<1
and
\Phi:\mathbb{N}\rightarrow[1,

\infty)
be non-decreasing function satifying condition
\underset{n\rightarrow\infty}{\lim}\Phi\left(n\right)=+\infty
, then there exists martingale
f\in H_{p}({G}^{2})
such that

\underset{n=1}{\overset{\infty}{\sum}}\frac{\left\|S_{n,n}f\right\|_{weak-L_{p}(G^{2})}^{p}\Phi\left(n\right)}{n^{3-2p}}=\infty.

Theorem
4.49
follows that, if
0<p<1
and
f\in H_{p}({G}^{2}),
then there exists an absolute constant
c_{p}
depending only on
p,
such that

\frac{1}{n^{1/2-p}}\overset{n}{\underset{k=1}{\sum}}\frac{\left\|S_{k,k}f\right\|_{H_{p}({G}^{2})}^{p}}{k^{3/2-p}}\leq c_{p}\left\|f\right\|_{H_{p}({G}^{2})}^{p},

Moreover,

\frac{1}{n^{1/2-p}}\overset{n}{\underset{k=1}{\sum}}\frac{\left\|S_{k,k}f-f\right\|_{H_{p}({G}^{2})}^{p}}{k^{3/2-p}}=0.

It follows the following equality

\frac{1}{n^{1/2-p}}\overset{n}{\underset{k=1}{\sum}}\frac{\left\|S_{k,k}f\right\|_{H_{p}(G)}^{p}}{k^{3/2-p}}=\left\|f\right\|_{H_{p}(G)}^{p}.

Weisz (for details see e.g.
[
78
]
) consider Marcinkiewicz means with respect to the two-dimensional partial sums of Walsh-Fourier series and proved the following:

Theorem We5.
Let
p>2/3
and
f\in H_{p}({G}^{2})
. Then there exists an absolute constant
c_{p}
depending only on
p,
such that

\left\|\mathscr{M}_{n}f-f\right\|_{H_{p}({G}^{2})}\rightarrow 0,\text{ \ \ }n\rightarrow\infty.

Goginava
[
26
]
proved that the following is true:

Theorem Gog2.
Let
0<p\leq 2/3
. Then there exists a martingale
f\in H_{p}({G}^{2})
, such that

\underset{n\in\mathbb{N}}{\sup}\left\|\mathscr{M}_{n}f\right\|_{H_{p}({G}^{2})}=\infty.

Goginava in
[
24
]
consider subsequence
\mathscr{M}_{2^{n}}
of Marcinkiewicz means with respect to the two-dimensional partial sums of Walsh-Fourier series and proved that the following is true:

Theorem Gog3.
Let
p>1/2
and
f\in H_{p}({G}^{2})
. Then

\left\|\mathscr{M}_{2^{k}}f-f\right\|_{H_{p}({G}^{2})}\rightarrow 0,\text{ \ as \ }k\rightarrow\infty.

(1.21)

Moreover, there exists a martingale
f\in H_{p}({G}^{2}),

\text{ }(0<p\leq 1/2)
such that

\underset{n\in\mathbb{N}}{\sup}\left\|\mathscr{M}_{2^{n}}f\right\|_{H_{p}({G}^{2})}=\infty.

In
[
37
]
was investigated strong convergence theorems of Marcinkiewicz means with respect to the two-dimensional partial sums of Walsh-Fourier series when
0<p<2/3
:

Theorem NT1.
Let
0<p<2/3
and
f\in H_{p}({G}^{2})
. Then there exists an absolute constant
c_{p}
depending only on
p,
such that

\sum_{m=1}^{\infty}\frac{\left\|\mathscr{M}_{m}f\right\|_{H_{p}({G}^{2})}^{p}}{m^{3-3p}}\leq c_{p}\left\|f\right\|_{H_{p}({G}^{2})}^{p}.

Moreover, if
0<p<2/3
and
\Phi:\mathbb{N}_{+}\rightarrow[1,

\infty)
be non-decreasing function satisfying the condition
\Phi\left(n\right)\uparrow\infty
and

\overline{\lim_{k\rightarrow\infty}}\frac{k^{3-3p}}{\Phi\left(k\right)}=\infty,

then there exists a martingale
f\in H_{p}\left(G^{2}\right),
such that

{\sum_{m=1}^{\infty}}\frac{\left\|\mathscr{M}_{m}f\right\|_{weak-L_{p}({G}^{2})}^{p}}{\Phi\left(m\right)}=\infty.

Theorem NT1 follows that if
0<p<2/3
and
f\in H_{p}({G}^{2}),
then there exists an absolute constant
c_{p}
depending only on
p,
such that

\frac{1}{n^{1-3p/2}}\overset{n}{\underset{k=1}{\sum}}\frac{\left\|\mathscr{M}_{k}f\right\|_{H_{p}({G}^{2})}^{p}}{k^{2-3p/2}}\leq c_{p}\left\|f\right\|_{H_{p}({G}^{2})}^{p},

Moreover,

\frac{1}{n^{1-3p/2}}\overset{n}{\underset{k=1}{\sum}}\frac{\left\|\mathscr{M}_{k}f-f\right\|_{H_{p}({G}^{2})}^{p}}{k^{2-3p/2}}=0,

It follows the following equality:

\frac{1}{n^{1-3p/2}}\overset{n}{\underset{k=1}{\sum}}\frac{\left\|\mathscr{M}_{k}f\right\|_{H_{p}(G)}^{p}}{k^{2-3p/2}}=\left\|f\right\|_{H_{p}(G)}^{p}.

In the fourth chapter (see
[
35
]
) we consider strong convergence results of Marcinkiewicz means with respect to the two-dimensional partial sums of Walsh-Fourier series, when
p=2/3
:

Theorem
4.50
Let
f\in H_{2/3}({G}^{2}).
Then there exists an absolute constant
c,
such that

\frac{1}{\log n}\sum_{m=1}^{n}\frac{\left\|\mathscr{M}_{m}f\right\|_{H_{2/3}({G}^{2})}^{2/3}}{m}\leq c\left\|f\right\|_{H_{2/3}({G}^{2})}^{2/3}.

From these results we obtain that, if
f\in H_{2/3}({G}^{2})
then

\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}\frac{1}{\log n}\overset{n}{\underset{k=1}{\sum}}\frac{\left\|\mathscr{M}_{k}f-f\right\|_{H_{2/3}({G}^{2})}^{2/3}}{k}=0

and

\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}\frac{1}{\log n}\overset{n}{\underset{k=1}{\sum}}\frac{\left\|\mathscr{M}_{k}f\right\|_{H_{2/3}({G}^{2})}^{2/3}}{k}=\left\|f\right\|_{H_{2/3}({G}^{2})}^{2/3}.

For the two-dimensional case Weisz
[
83
]
proved that the following is true:

Theorem We6.
Let
p>2/3
and
f\in H_{p}\left(G^{2}\right)
. Then the maximal operator of Marcinkiewicz means with respect to the two-dimensional partial sums of Walsh-Fourier series
\mathscr{M}^{\ast}f
is bounded from
H_{p}\left(G^{2}\right)
to
L_{p}\left(G^{2}\right)
:

\left\|\mathscr{M}^{\ast}f\right\|_{p}\leq c_{p}\left\|f\right\|_{H_{p}(G^{2})},

where
c_{p}
is an absolute constant, depending only on
p
.

Goginava
[
25
]
also proved that the following is true:

Theorem Gog4.
Let
f\in H_{2/3}\left(G^{2}\right)
. Then there exists an absolute constant
c,
such that

\left\|\mathscr{M}^{\ast}f\right\|_{weak-L_{2/3}(G^{2})}\leq c\left\|f\right\|_{H_{2/3}}.

Moreover, there exists a martingale
f\in H_{p}({G}^{2}),

\text{ }(0<p\leq 2/3)
such that

\underset{n\in\mathbb{N}}{\sup}\left\|\mathscr{M}_{n}f\right\|_{p}=\infty.

Goginava
[
26
]
also consider restricted maximal operator of Marcinkiewicz means with respect to the two-dimensional partial sums of Walsh-Fourier series
\sup_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\left|\mathscr{M}_{2^{n}}\right|
and show that the following is true:

Theorem Gog5.
Let
p>1/2
and
f\in H_{p}({G}^{2}).
Then there exists an absolute constant
c_{p}
depending only on
p,
such that

\left\|\sup_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\left|\mathscr{M}_{2^{n}}f\right|\right\|_{p}\leq c_{p}\left\|f\right\|_{H_{p}({G}^{2})}.

(1.22)

Moreover, there exists a martingale
f\in H_{p}({G}^{2}),

\text{ }(0<p\leq 1/2)
such that

\underset{n\in\mathbb{N}}{\sup}\left\|\mathscr{M}_{2^{n}}f\right\|_{p}=\infty.

In
[
34
]
and
[
37
]
we investigate boundedness of weighted maximal operators when
0<p\leq 2/3
:

Theorem NT2.
Let
0<p\leq 2/3.
Then the maximal operator

\widetilde{\mathscr{M}}^{\ast}:=\sup_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\frac{\left|\mathscr{M}_{n}\right|}{{(n+1)}^{2/p-3}\log^{3[1/3+p]/2}\left(n+1\right)}

is bounded from
H_{p}(G^{2})
to
L_{p}(G^{2})
.

Moreover, if
\varphi:\mathbb{N}\rightarrow[1,\infty)
be non-decreasing function satisfying the condition

{\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}}\frac{n^{2/p-3}\log^{3[1/3+p]/2}\left(n\right)}{\varphi\left(n\right)}=+\infty,

then

\sup_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\left\|\frac{{\mathscr{M}}_{n}f}{\varphi\left(n\right)}\right\|_{p}=\infty.

From Theorem NT2 we get that for
0<p\leq 1/2
and
f\in H_{p}(G^{2})
, there exists a absolute constant
c_{p},
depending only on
p
, such that:

\left\|\mathscr{M}_{n}f\right\|_{p}\leq c_{p}{{(n+1)}^{2/p-3}\log^{3[1/3+p]/2}\left(n+1\right)}\left\|f\right\|_{H_{p}({G}^{2})}.

(1.23)

By applying inequality (
1.23
) in
[
37
]
(see also
[
38
]
) we obtain necessary and sufficient conditions for modulus of continuity of martingale
f\in H_{p}(G^{2})
, for which Marcinkiewicz means with respect to the two-dimensional partial sums of Walsh-Fourier series of
f\in H_{p}(G^{2})
converge in
H_{p}(G^{2})
norm.

Theorem NT3.
Let
1/2<p<2/3,

f\in H_{p}\left(G^{2}\right)\
and

\omega_{H_{p}({G}^{2})}\left(\frac{1}{2^{k}},f\right)=o\left(\frac{1}{2^{k\left(2/p-3\right)}}\right),\text{ as \ }k\rightarrow\infty.

Then

\left\|\mathscr{M}_{n}f-f\right\|_{H_{p}({G}^{2})}\rightarrow 0,\text{ as }n\rightarrow\infty.

Moreover, if
0<p<2/3,
then there exists a martingale
f\in H_{p}(G^{2}),
such that

\omega_{H_{p}({G}^{2})}\left(\frac{1}{2^{k}},f\right)=O\left(\frac{1}{2^{k\left(2/p-3\right)}}\right),\text{ as \ }k\rightarrow\infty

and

\left\|\mathscr{M}_{n}f-f\right\|_{weak-L_{p}({G}^{2})}\nrightarrow 0,\text{ \ as \ }n\rightarrow\infty.

If we apply again (
1.23
) and improve method which was investigated in
[
37
]
in the fourth chapter (see also
[
36
]
) we obtain that the following is true:

Theorem
4.52
.
Let
f\in H_{2/3}(G^{2})
and

\omega_{H_{2/3}({G}^{2})}\left(\frac{1}{2^{k}},f\right)=o\left(\frac{1}{{k}^{3/2}}\right),\text{ as \ }k\rightarrow\infty.

Then

\left\|\mathscr{M}_{n}f-f\right\|_{H_{2/3}({G}^{2})}\rightarrow 0,\text{ as }n\rightarrow\infty.

On the other hand, there exists a martingale
f\in H_{2/3}(G^{2}),
such that

\omega_{H_{2/3}({G}^{2})}\left(\frac{1}{2^{k}},f\right)=O\left(\frac{1}{{k}^{3/2}}\right),\text{ as \ }k\rightarrow\infty

and

\left\|\mathscr{M}_{n}f-f\right\|_{2/3}\nrightarrow 0,\text{ \ as \ }n\rightarrow\infty.

2
Partial sums with respect to the one-dimensional Walsh-Fourier series on the martingale Hardy spaces

2.1
Basic notations

Denote by
\mathbb{N}_{+}
the set of the positive integers and by
\mathbb{N}:=\mathbb{N}_{+}\cup\{0\}
the set of non-negative integers. Denote by
Z_{2}
the additive group of integers modulo-
2,
which contains only two elements
Z_{2}:=\{0,1\},
group operation is modulo-
2
sum and all sets are open.

Define the group
G
as the complete direct product of the groups
Z_{2}
with the product of the discrete topologies
Z_{2}
. The direct product
\mu
of the measures
\mu_{n}\left(\{j\}\right):=1/2,\ (j\in Z_{2})
is the Haar measure on
G
with
\mu\left(G\right)=1.

The elements of
G
are represented by sequences

x:=(x_{0},x_{1},...,x_{j},...)\qquad\left(x_{k}=0,1\right).

It is easy to give a base for the neighbourhood of
G

I_{0}\left(x\right):=G,

I_{n}(x):=\{y\in G\mid y_{0}=x_{0},...,y_{n-1}=x_{n-1}\}\text{ }(x\in G,\text{ }n\in\mathbb{N}).

Set
I_{n}:=I_{n}\left(0\right)
for any
n\in\mathbb{N}
and
\overline{I_{n}}:=G

\backslash

I_{n}
.

It is evident that

\overline{I_{M}}=\left(\overset{M-2}{\underset{k=0}{\bigcup}}\overset{M-1}{\underset{l=k+1}{\bigcup}}I_{l+1}\left(e_{k}+e_{l}\right)\right)\bigcup\left(\underset{k=0}{\bigcup\limits^{M-1}}I_{M}\left(e_{k}\right)\right)=\underset{k=0}{\bigcup\limits^{M-1}}I_{k}\backslash I_{k+1}.

(2.1)

If
n\in\mathbb{N},
then it can be uniquely expressed as

n=\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}n_{j}2^{j}

where
n_{j}\in Z_{2}

~(j\in\mathbb{N})
and only a finite number of
n_{j}
s differ from zero.

Set

\left\langle n\right\rangle:=\min\{j\in\mathbb{N},n_{j}\neq 0\}\text{ \ \ and \ \ \ }\left|n\right|:=\max\{j\in\mathbb{N},n_{j}\neq 0\},

It is evident that
2^{\left|n\right|}\leq n\leq 2^{\left|n\right|+1}.

Let

d\left(n\right):=\left|n\right|-\left\langle n\right\rangle,\text{ \ for any \ }n\in\mathbb{N}.

Denote by
V(n)
variation of natural number
n\in\mathbb{N}

V\left(n\right)=n_{0}+\overset{\infty}{\underset{k=1}{\sum}}\left|n_{k}-n_{k-1}\right|.

Define
k
-th Rademacher functions by

r_{k}\left(x\right):=\left(-1\right)^{x_{k}}\text{\qquad}\left(\text{ }x\in G,\text{ }k\in\mathbb{N}\right).

By using Rademacher functions we define Walsh system
w:=(w_{n}:n\in\mathbb{N})

G
as:

w_{n}(x):=\underset{k=0}{\overset{\infty}{\prod}}r_{k}^{n_{k}}\left(x\right)=r_{\left|n\right|}\left(x\right)\left(-1\right)^{\underset{k=0}{\overset{\left|n\right|-1}{\sum}}n_{k}x_{k}}\text{\qquad}\left(n\in\mathbb{N}\right).

The norm (quasi-norm) of space
L_{p}(G),\ \left(0<p<\infty\right)
is defined as

\left\|f\right\|_{p}^{p}:=\left(\int_{G}\left|f(x)\right|^{p}d\mu(x)\right).

and norm (quasi-norm) of space
weak-L_{p}(G)
is defined by

\left\|f\right\|_{weak-L_{p}(G)}^{p}:=\underset{\lambda>0}{\sup}\lambda^{p}\mu\left(x\in{G}:|f|>\lambda\right)<+\infty.

Walsh system is orthonormal and complete in
L_{2}\left(G\right)
(see
[
47
]
).

For any
f\in L_{1}\left(G\right)
the numbers

\displaystyle\widehat{f}\left(n\right):=\int\limits_{G}f(x)w_{n}(x)d\mu({x})

are called
n
-th Walsh-Fourier coefficient of
f
.

n
-th partial sum is denoted by

S_{n}(f;{x}):=\sum\limits_{i=0}^{n-1}\widehat{f}\left(i\right)w_{i}\left(x\right).

Dirichlet kernels are defined by

D_{n}\left(x\right):=\sum\limits_{i=0}^{n-1}w_{i}\left(x\right).

We also define the following maximal operators

\displaystyle{S}^{\ast}f

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sup_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\left|S_{{n}}f\right|

\displaystyle\widetilde{S}_{\#}^{\ast}f

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sup_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\left|S_{2^{n}}f\right|

The
\sigma
-algebra generated by the intervals
I_{n}(x)

with measure
2^{-n}
is denoted by
\digamma_{n}\left(n\in\mathbb{N}\right).
Conditional exponential operator with respect to

\digamma_{n}\left(n\in\mathbb{N}\right)
is denoted by
E_{n}
and it is given by

\displaystyle E_{n}f(x)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle S_{2^{n}}f\left(x\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{k=0}^{2^{n}-1}\widehat{f}\left(k\right)w_{k}(x)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\left|I_{n}\left(x\right)\right|}\int_{I_{n}\left(x\right)}f(x)d\mu(x),

where
\left|I_{n}\left(x\right)\right|=2^{-n}
denotes length of set

I_{n}\left(x\right)
.

Sequence
f=\left(f_{n},\text{ }n\in\mathbb{N}\right)
of functions
f_{n}\in L_{1}\left(G\right)
is called dyadic martingale (for details see
[
39
]
,
[
47
]
) if

\left(i\right)

f_{n}
is measurable with respect to
\sigma-
algebras
\digamma_{n}
for any
n\in\mathbb{N}
,

\left(ii\right)

E_{n}f_{m}=f_{n}
for any
n\leq m
.

The maximal function of a martingale
f
is defined by

f^{\ast}=\sup_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\left|f_{n}\right|.

In the case
f\in L_{1}\left(G\right),
the maximal functions are also be given by:

f^{\ast}\left(x\right)=\sup\limits_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\frac{1}{\mu\left(I_{n}\left(x\right)\right)}\left|\int\limits_{I_{n}\left(x\right)}f\left(u\right)d\mu\left(u\right)\right|.

For
0<p<\infty
the Hardy martingale space
H_{p}\left(G\right)

consists of all martingales, for which

\left\|f\right\|_{H_{p}\left(G\right)}:=\left\|f^{\ast}\right\|_{p}<\infty.

A bounded measurable function
a
is said to be a
p
-atom if there exists an
dyadic interval
I
, such that

\int_{I}ad\mu=0,\text{ \ \ }\left\|a\right\|_{\infty}\leq\mu\left(I\right)^{-1/p},\text{ \ \ supp}\left(a\right)\subset I.\qquad

It is easy to show that for martingale
f=(f_{n},n\in\mathbb{N})
and for any
{k}\in{\mathbb{N}}
there exists a limit

\widehat{f}\left(k\right):=\lim_{{n}\rightarrow\infty}\int_{{G}}f_{{{n}}}\left({x}\right)w_{{k}}\left({x}\right)d\mu\left({x}\right)

and it is called
k
-th Walsh-Fourier coefficients of
f
.

If
f_{0}\in L_{1}\left(G\right)
and
f:=(E_{n}f_{0}:n\in\mathbb{N})
is regular martingale then

\displaystyle\widehat{f}\left(k\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{{G}}f\left({x}\right)w_{k}(x)d\mu\left({x}\right)=\widehat{f_{0}}\left(k\right),\ \ k\in\mathbb{N}.

The modulus of continuity in
H_{p}(G)
space is defined by

\omega_{H_{p}\left(G\right)}\left(\frac{1}{2^{n}},f\right):=\left\|f-S_{2^{n}}f\right\|_{H_{p}\left(G\right)}.

It is important to describe how can be understood difference
f-S_{2^{n}}f
, where
f
be martingale
S_{2^{n}}f
is a function:

Remark 2.1

Let
0<p\leq 1.
Since

S_{2^{n}}f=f^{\left(n\right)}\in L_{1}(G),\text{ \ where \ }f=\left(f^{\left(n\right)}:n\in\mathbb{N}\right)\in H_{p}(G)

and

\displaystyle\left(S_{2^{k}}f^{\left(n\right)}:k\in\mathbb{N}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(S_{2^{k}}S_{2^{n}},k\in\mathbb{N}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(S_{2^{0}}f,\ldots,S_{2^{n-1}}f,S_{2^{n}}f,S_{2^{n}}f,\ldots\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(f^{\left(0\right)},\ldots,f^{\left(n-1\right)},f^{\left(n\right)},f^{\left(n\right)},\ldots\right).

Under the difference
f-S_{2^{n}}f
we mean the following martingale:

f:=(\left(f-S_{2^{n}}f\right)^{\left(k\right)},\ \ k\in\mathbb{N})

where

\left(f-S_{2^{n}}f\right)^{\left(k\right)}=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}0,&k=0,\ldots,n,\\
f^{\left(k\right)}-f^{\left(n\right)},&k\geq n+1,\end{array}\right.

Consequently, the norm
\left\|f-S_{2^{n}}f\right\|_{H_{p}\left(G\right)}
is understood as
H_{p}
-norm of

f-S_{2^{n}}f=(\left(f-S_{2^{n}}f\right)^{\left(k\right)},k\in\mathbb{N})

.

Watari
[
75
]
showed that there are strong connections between

\omega_{p}\left(\frac{1}{2^{n}},f\right),\text{ \ }E_{2^{n}}\left(L_{p},f\right)\text{ \ \ and\ \ \ }\left\|f-S_{2^{n}}f\right\|_{p},\ \ p\geq 1,\text{ \ }n\in\mathbb{N}.

In particular,

\frac{1}{2}\omega_{p}\left(\frac{1}{2^{n}},f\right)\leq\left\|f-S_{2^{n}}f\right\|_{p}\leq\omega_{p}\left(\frac{1}{2^{n}},f\right)

and

\frac{1}{2}\left\|f-S_{2^{n}}f\right\|_{p}\leq E_{2^{n}}\left(L_{p},f\right)\leq\left\|f-S_{2^{n}}f\right\|_{p}.

2.2
Auxiliary lemmas

First we present and prove equalities and estimations of Dirichlet kernel and Lebesgue constants with respect to the one-dimensional Walsh-Fourier systems (see Lemmas
2.2
-
3.26
).

First equality of the following Lemma is proved in
[
47
]
and second identity is proved in
[
18
]
:

Lemma 2.2

Let
j,n\in\mathbb{N}
. Then

D_{j+2^{n}}=D_{2^{n}}+w_{2^{n}}D_{j},\text{ \ when \ }j\leq 2^{n},

and

D_{2^{n}-j}=D_{2^{n}}-\psi_{2^{n}-1}{D_{j}},\text{ \ when \ }j<2^{n}.

The following estimation of Dirichlet kernel with respect to the one-dimensional Walsh-Fourier systems is proved in
[
47
]
:

Lemma 2.3

Let
n\in\mathbb{N}
. Then

D_{2^{n}}\left(x\right)=\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}2^{n},\text{ \ if \ }x\in I_{n},\\
0,\text{ \ if \ }x\notin I_{n},\end{array}\right.

and

D_{n}=w_{n}\overset{\infty}{\underset{k=0}{\sum}}n_{k}r_{k}D_{2^{k}}=w_{n}\overset{\infty}{\underset{k=0}{\sum}}n_{k}\left(D_{2^{k+1}}-D_{2^{k}}\right),\text{ \ for \ }n=\overset{\infty}{\underset{i=0}{\sum}}n_{i}2^{i}.

The following two-sided estimations of Lebesgue constants with respect to the one-dimensional Walsh-Fourier systems is proved in
[
47
]
and second equality is proved in
[
14
]
:

Lemma 2.4

Let
n\in\mathbb{N}.
Then

\frac{1}{8}V\left(n\right)\leq\left\|D_{n}\right\|_{1}\leq V\left(n\right)

and

\frac{1}{n\log n}\underset{k=1}{\overset{n}{\sum}}V\left(k\right)=\frac{1}{4\log 2}+o\left(1\right).

Hardy martingale space
H_{p}\left(G\right)
for any
0<p\leq 1
can be characterize by simple functions which are called
p
-atoms. The following is true (for details see
[
50
]
,
[
78
]
and
[
82
]
):

Lemma 2.5

A martingale
f=\left(f_{n},\text{ }n\in\mathbb{N}\right)
belongs to
H_{p}(G)\left(0<p\leq 1\right)
if and only if there exists a sequence of
p
-atoms of

\left(a_{k},k\in\mathbb{N}\right)
and sequence of real numbers

\left(\mu_{k},\text{ }k\in\mathbb{N}\right)
such that for all
n\in\mathbb{N}
,

\qquad\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\mu_{k}S_{2^{n}}a_{k}=f_{n}

(2.2)

and

\qquad\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\left|\mu_{k}\right|^{p}<\infty.

Moreover,

\left\|f\right\|_{H_{p}(G)}\backsim\inf\left(\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\left|\mu_{k}\right|^{p}\right)^{1/p},

where the infimum is taken over all decomposition of
f
of the form (
2.2
).

The next five Examples of martingales will be used many times to prove sharpness of our main results. Such counterexamples first appear in the papers of Goginava
[
24
]
(see also
[
23
]
). Such constructions of martingales are also used in the papers
[
3
]
,
[
4
]
,
[
11
]
,
[
32
]
,
[
33
]
,
[
36
]
,
[
40
]
,
[
41
]
,
[
54
]
,
[
59
]
,
[
63
]
,
[
64
]
,
[
66
]
,
[
67
]
,
[
68
]
,
[
72
]
,
[
73
]
,
[
71
]
,
[
73
]
.
For the one-dimensional case we use martingales which were used in
[
74
]
. So, we leave out the details of proof.

Example 2.6

Let
0<p\leq 1,

\left\{\lambda_{k}:k\in\mathbb{N}\right\}

be sequence of real numbers

\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\left|\lambda_{k}\right|^{p}\leq c_{p}<\infty

(2.3)

and
\left\{a_{k}:k\in\mathbb{N}\right\}
be sequence of
p
-atoms, given by

a_{k}(x):={2^{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|(1/p-1)}}\left(D_{2^{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|+1}}(x)-D_{2^{{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|}}}(x)\right),

where
\left|\alpha_{k}\right|:=\max

\{j\in\mathbb{N}:

\left(\alpha_{k}\right)_{j}\neq 0\}
and
\left(\alpha_{k}\right)_{j}

denotes
j
-th binary coefficients of real number of
\alpha_{k}\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
. Then

\,f=\left(f_{n}:\text{ }n\in\mathbb{N}\right),
where

f_{n}(x):=\sum_{\left\{k:\text{ }\left|\alpha_{k}\right|<n\right\}}\lambda_{k}a_{k}(x)

is martingale, which belongs to
H_{p}(G)
for any
0<p\leq 1
.

It is easy to show that

\widehat{f}(j)

(2.4)

=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}{\lambda_{k}2^{(1/p-1)\left|\alpha_{k}\right|}},&j\in\left\{2^{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|},...,2^{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|+1}-1\right\},\text{ }k\in\mathbb{N}_{+},\\
0,&\text{\thinspace}j\notin\bigcup\limits_{k=1}^{\infty}\left\{2^{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|},...,2^{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|+1}-1\right\}.\end{array}\right.

Let

2^{\left|\alpha_{l-1}\right|+1}\leq j\leq 2^{\left|\alpha_{l}\right|},

l\in\mathbb{N}_{+}.

Then

\displaystyle S_{j}f

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle S_{2^{\left|\alpha_{l-1}\right|+1}}

(2.5)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{\eta=0}^{l-1}{\lambda_{\eta}2^{{}_{\left|\alpha_{\eta}\right|}(1/p-1)}}\left(D_{2^{\left|\alpha_{\eta}\right|+1}}-D_{2^{\left|\alpha_{\eta}\right|}}\right).

Let
2^{\left|\alpha_{l}\right|}\leq j<2^{\left|\alpha_{l}\right|+1},

l\in\mathbb{N}_{+}.
Then

\displaystyle S_{j}f

(2.6)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle S_{2^{\left|\alpha_{l}\right|}}+{\lambda_{l}2^{(1/p-1)\left|\alpha_{l}\right|}w_{2^{\left|\alpha_{l}\right|}}D_{j-2^{{}_{\left|\alpha_{l}\right|}}}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{\eta=0}^{l-1}{\lambda_{\eta}2^{(1/p-1)\left|\alpha_{\eta}\right|}}\left(D_{2^{{\left|\alpha_{\eta}\right|+1}}}-D_{2^{{\left|\alpha_{\eta}\right|}}}\right)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle{\lambda_{l}2^{(1/p-1)\left|\alpha_{l}\right|}w_{2^{\left|\alpha_{l}\right|}}D_{j-2^{\left|\alpha_{l}\right|}}}.

Moreover, for the modulus of continuity for
0<p\leq 1
we have the following estimation:

\omega_{H_{p}}\left(\frac{1}{2^{n}},f\right)=O\left(\sum_{\left\{k:\text{ }\left|\alpha_{k}\right|\geq n\right\}}^{\infty}\left|\lambda_{k}\right|^{p}\right)^{1/p},\text{ \ \ as \ \ }n\rightarrow\infty.

(2.7)

By applying Lemma
2.5
we easily obtain that the following is true (see
[
82
]
):

Lemma 2.7

Let
0<p\leq 1
and
T
be
\sigma
-sub-linear operator, such that, for any
p
-atom
a
,

\int\limits_{G}\left|Ta({x})\right|^{p}d\mu({x})\leq c_{p}<\infty.

Then

\left\|Tf\right\|_{p}\leq c_{p}\left\|f\right\|_{H_{p}(G)}.

(2.8)

In addition, if
T
is bounded from
L_{\infty}(G)
to
L_{\infty}(G)
then to prove (
2.8
) it is suffices to show that

\int\limits_{\overset{-}{I}}\left|Ta({x})\right|^{p}d\mu({x})\leq c_{p}<\infty,

for every
p
-atom
a
, where
I
denotes support of the atom
a
.

In the concrete cases the norm of Hardy martingale spaces can be calculated by simpler formulas (for details see
[
50
]
,
[
78
]
and
[
79
]
):

Lemma 2.8

If
g\in L_{1}\left(G\right)
and
f:=(E_{n}g:n\in\mathbb{N})
be regular martingale, then
H_{p}\left(G\right)\text{ \ for \ }0<p\leq 1
norm can be calculated by

\left\|f\right\|_{H_{p}(G)}=\left\|\sup\limits_{n\in\mathbb{N}}|S_{2^{n}}g|\right\|_{p}.

The following lemmas are proved in
[
59
]
,
[
63
]
,
[
64
]
.

Lemma 2.9

Let
0<p\leq 1
,
2^{k}\leq n<2^{k+1}
and
S_{n}f
be
n
-th partial sum with respect to the one-dimensional Walsh-Fourier series, where
f\in H_{p}(G)
. Then for any fixed
n\in\mathbb{N}
,

\displaystyle\left\|S_{n}f\right\|_{H_{p}(G)}^{p}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left\|\sup_{0\leq l\leq k}\left|S_{2^{l}}f\right|\right\|_{p}^{p}+\left\|S_{n}f\right\|_{p}^{p}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left\|\widetilde{S}_{\#}^{\ast}f\right\|_{p}^{p}+\left\|S_{n}f\right\|_{p}^{p}.

Proof
:
Let consider the following martingales

f_{\#}:=\left(S_{2^{k}}S_{n}f,\text{ }k\in\mathbb{N}_{+}\right)

=\left(S_{2^{0}},S_{2^{k}}f,\text{ }S_{n}f,...,S_{n}f,...\right),

Hence, Lemma
2.8
immediately follows that

\displaystyle\left\|S_{n}f\right\|_{H_{p}(G)}^{p}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left\|\sup_{0\leq l\leq k}\left|S_{2^{l}}f\right|\right\|_{p}^{p}+\left\|S_{n}f\right\|_{p}^{p}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left\|\widetilde{S}_{\#}^{\ast}f\right\|_{p}^{p}+\left\|S_{n}f\right\|_{p}^{p}.

Lemma is proved.

2.3
Boundedness of subsequences of partial sums with respect to the one-dimensional Walsh-Fourier series on the martingale Hardy spaces

In this section we consider boundedness of subsequences of partial sums with respect to the one-dimensional Walsh-Fourier series in the martingale Hardy spaces (for details see
[
63
]
).

Theorem 2.10

a) Let
0<p<1
and
f\in H_{p}(G)
. Then there exists an absolute constant
c_{p}
depending only on
p,
such that

\text{ }\left\|S_{n}f\right\|_{H_{p}(G)}\leq c_{p}2^{d\left(n\right)\left(1/p-1\right)}\left\|f\right\|_{H_{p}(G)}.

b) Let
0<p<1,

\left\{m_{k}:\text{ }k\in\mathbb{N}_{+}\right\}
be non-negative, increasing sequence of natural numbers such that

\sup_{k\in\mathbb{N}}d\left(m_{k}\right)=\infty

(2.9)

and
\Phi:\mathbb{N}_{+}\rightarrow[1,\infty)
be non-decreasing function satisfying the condition

\overline{\underset{k\rightarrow\infty}{\lim}}\frac{2^{d\left(m_{k}\right)\left(1/p-1\right)}}{\Phi\left(m_{k}\right)}=\infty.

(2.10)

Then there exists a martingale
f\in H_{p}(G)
such that

\underset{k\in\mathbb{N}}{\sup}\left\|\frac{S_{m_{k}}f}{\Phi\left(m_{k}\right)}\right\|_{weak-L_{p}(G)}=\infty.

Proof
:
Suppose that

\left\|2^{\left(1-1/p\right)d\left(n\right)}S_{n}f\right\|_{p}\leq c_{p}\left\|f\right\|_{H_{p}(G)}.

(2.11)

By combining Lemma
2.9
and inequalities (
1.7
) and (
2.11
), since
2^{\left(1-1/p\right)d\left(n\right)}\leq c_{p}
we obtain that

\displaystyle\left\|2^{\left(1-1/p\right)d\left(n\right)}S_{n}f\right\|_{H_{p}(G)}^{p}

(2.12)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left\|2^{\left(1-1/p\right)d\left(n\right)}S_{n}f\right\|_{p}^{p}+2^{\left(1-1/p\right)d\left(n\right)}\left\|\widetilde{S}_{\#}^{\ast}f\right\|_{p}^{p}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle c_{p}\left\|f\right\|_{H_{p}(G)}^{p}+c_{p}\left\|\widetilde{S}_{\#}^{\ast}f\right\|_{p}^{p}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle c_{p}\left\|f\right\|_{H_{p}(G)}^{p}.

By combining Lemma
2.7
and (
2.12
) it is suficies to show that

\int\limits_{G}\left|2^{\left(1-1/p\right)d\left(n\right)}S_{n}a\right|^{p}d\mu\leq c_{p}<\infty,

(2.13)

for every
p
-atom
a
, with support
I
, such that

\mu\left(I\right)=2^{-M}
.

Without loss the generality we may assume that
p
-atom
a
has support
I=I_{M}.
Then it is easy to see that
S_{n}a=0,
where
2^{M}

\geq n
.
So, we may assume that
2^{M}<n
. Since
\left\|a\right\|_{\infty}\leq 2^{M/p}
we can conclude that

\displaystyle\left|2^{\left(1-1/p\right)d\left(n\right)}S_{n}a\left(x\right)\right|

(2.14)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2^{\left(1-1/p\right)d\left(n\right)}\left\|a\right\|_{\infty}\int_{I_{M}}\left|D_{n}\left(x+t\right)\right|d\mu\left(t\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2^{M/p}2^{\left(1-1/p\right)d\left(n\right)}\int_{I_{M}}\left|D_{n}\left(x+t\right)\right|d\mu\left(t\right).

Let
x\in I_{M}
. Since
V\left(n\right)\leq 2d\left(n\right),
by using first estimations of Lemma
2.4
we can conclude that

\displaystyle\left|2^{\left(1-1/p\right)d\left(n\right)}S_{n}a\right|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2^{M/p}2^{\left(1-1/p\right)d\left(n\right)}V\left(n\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2^{M/p}d\left(n\right)2^{\left(1-1/p\right)d\left(n\right)}

and

\displaystyle\int_{I_{M}}\left|2^{\left(1-1/p\right)d\left(n\right)}S_{n}a\right|^{p}d\mu

(2.15)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle d\left(n\right)2^{\left(1-1/p\right)d\left(n\right)}<c_{p}<\infty.

Let
t\in I_{M}
and
x\in I_{s}\backslash I_{s+1},\,
where
0\leq s\leq M-1<\left\langle n\right\rangle
or
0\leq s<\left\langle n\right\rangle\leq M-1.
Then
x+t

\in I_{s}\backslash I_{s+1}
and if we use both equality of Lemma
2.3
we get that
D_{n}\left(x+t\right)=0
and it follows that

\left|2^{\left(1-1/p\right)d\left(n\right)}S_{n}a\left(x\right)\right|=0.

(2.16)

Let
x\in I_{s}\backslash I_{s+1},

\,\left\langle n\right\rangle\leq s\leq M-1.
Then
x+t\in I_{s}\backslash I_{s+1},
where
t\in I_{M}
. Then by using again both equality of Lemma
2.3
we have that

\left|D_{n}\left(x+t\right)\right|\leq\sum_{j=0}^{s}n_{j}2^{j}\leq c2^{s}.

If we apply again (
2.14
) we can conclude that

\displaystyle\left|2^{\left(1-1/p\right)d\left(n\right)}S_{n}a\left(x\right)\right|

(2.17)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2^{\left(1-1/p\right)d\left(n\right)}2^{M/p}\frac{2^{s}}{2^{M}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2^{\left<n\right>\left(1/p-1\right)}2^{M(1/p-1)}\frac{2^{s}}{2^{\left|n\right|\left(1/p-1\right)}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2^{\left\langle n\right\rangle\left(1/p-1\right)}2^{s}.

By identity (
2.1
) and inequalities (
2.16
) and (
2.17
) we find that

\displaystyle\int_{\overline{I_{M}}}\left|2^{\left(1-1/p\right)d\left(n\right)}S_{n}a\left(x\right)\right|^{p}d\mu\left(x\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\overset{M-1}{\underset{s=\left\langle n\right\rangle}{\sum}}\int_{I_{s}\backslash I_{s+1}}\left|2^{\left\langle n\right\rangle\left(1/p-1\right)}2^{s}\right|^{p}d\mu\left(x\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle c\overset{M-1}{\underset{s=\left\langle n\right\rangle}{\sum}}\frac{2^{\left\langle n\right\rangle\left(1-p\right)}}{2^{s\left(1-p\right)}}\leq c_{p}<\infty.

Now, we prove part b) of Theorem
2.10
. By using condition (
2.10
) there exists sequence of natural numbers
\left\{\alpha_{k}:\text{ }k\in\mathbb{N}_{+}\right\}\subset\left\{m_{k}:\text{ }k\in\mathbb{N}_{+}\right\},
such that

\sum_{\eta=0}^{\infty}\frac{\Phi^{p/2}\left(\alpha_{\eta}\right)}{2^{d\left(\alpha_{\eta}\right)\left(1-p\right)/2}}<\infty,

(2.18)

Let
f=\left(f_{n},n\in\mathbb{N}_{+}\right)\in H_{p}(G)
be a martingale from the Example
2.6
, where

\lambda_{k}=\frac{\Phi^{1/2}\left(\alpha_{k}\right)}{2^{d\left(\alpha_{k}\right)\left(1/p-1\right)/2}}.

(2.19)

Then, if we use (
2.18
) we obtain that condition (
2.3
) is fulfilled and it follows that
f=\left(f_{n},n\in\mathbb{N}_{+}\right)\in H_{p}(G).

If we apply (
2.4
) when
\lambda_{k}
are given by the formula (
2.19
) then we get that

\widehat{f}(j)

(2.20)

=\left\{\begin{array}[]{c}\Phi^{1/2}\left(\alpha_{k}\right)2^{\left(\left|\alpha_{k}\right|+\left\langle\alpha_{k}\right\rangle\right)\left(1/p-1\right)/2},\text{ \ if \thinspace\thinspace}j\in\left\{2^{{}_{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|}},...,2^{{}_{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|+1}}-1\right\},\text{ }k\in\mathbb{N}_{+}\\
0,\text{ \ if \ }j\notin\bigcup\limits_{k=0}^{\infty}\left\{2^{{}_{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|}},...,2^{{}_{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|+1}}-1\right\}.\text{ }\end{array}\right.

In the view of (
2.6
) when
\lambda_{k}
are given by (
2.19
) we get that

\displaystyle\frac{S_{\alpha_{k}}f}{\Phi\left(\alpha_{k}\right)}

(2.21)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\Phi\left(\alpha_{k}\right)}\sum_{\eta=0}^{k-1}\Phi^{1/2}\left(\alpha_{\eta}\right)2^{\left(\left|\alpha_{\eta}\right|+\left\langle\alpha_{\eta}\right\rangle\right)\left(1/p-1\right)/2}\left(D_{2^{\left|\alpha_{\eta}\right|+1}}-D_{2^{\left|\alpha_{\eta}\right|}}\right)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\frac{2^{\left(\left|\alpha_{k}\right|+\left\langle\alpha_{k}\right\rangle\right)\left(1/p-1\right)/2}w_{2^{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|}}D_{\alpha_{k}-2^{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|}}}{\Phi^{1/2}\left(\alpha_{k}\right)}:=I+II.

by using (
2.18
) for
I
we have that

\displaystyle\left\|I\right\|_{weak-L_{p}(G)}^{p}

(2.22)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\Phi^{p}\left(\alpha_{k}\right)}\sum_{\eta=0}^{k-1}\frac{\Phi^{p/2}\left(\alpha_{\eta}\right)}{2^{d\left(\alpha_{\eta}\right)\left(1-p\right)/2}}\left\|2^{\left|\alpha_{\eta}\right|\left(1/p-1\right)}\left(D_{2^{\left|\alpha_{\eta}\right|+1}}-D_{2^{\left|\alpha_{\eta}\right|}}\right)\right\|_{weak-L_{p}(G)}^{p}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\Phi^{p}\left(\alpha_{k}\right)}\sum_{\eta=0}^{\infty}\frac{\Phi^{p/2}\left(\alpha_{\eta}\right)}{2^{d\left(\alpha_{\eta}\right)\left(1-p\right)/2}}\leq c<\infty.

Let
x\in I_{\left\langle\alpha_{k}\right\rangle}\backslash I_{\left\langle\alpha_{k}\right\rangle+1}.
Since

\left|\alpha_{k}\right|\neq\left\langle\alpha_{k}\right\rangle
and

\left\langle\alpha_{k}-2^{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|}\right\rangle=\left\langle\alpha_{k}\right\rangle.

By using both inequalities of Lemma
2.3
we get that

\displaystyle\left|D_{\alpha_{k}-2^{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|}}(x)\right|

(2.23)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left|\left(D_{2^{\left\langle\alpha_{k}\right\rangle+1}}(x)-D_{2^{\left\langle\alpha_{k}\right\rangle}}(x)\right)+\overset{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|-1}{\underset{j=\left\langle\alpha_{k}\right\rangle+1}{\sum}}\left(\alpha_{k}\right)_{j}\left(D_{2^{i+1}}(x)-D_{2^{i}}(x)\right)\right|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left|-D_{2^{\left\langle\alpha_{k}\right\rangle}}(x)\right|=2^{\left\langle\alpha_{k}\right\rangle}

and

\displaystyle\left|II\right|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{2^{\left(\left|\alpha_{k}\right|+\left\langle\alpha_{k}\right\rangle\right)\left(1/p-1\right)/2}}{\Phi^{1/2}\left(\alpha_{k}\right)}\left|D_{\alpha_{k}-2^{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|}}\left(x\right)\right|

(2.24)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{2^{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|\left(1/p-1\right)/2}2^{\left\langle\alpha_{k}\right\rangle\left(1/p+1\right)/2}}{\Phi^{1/2}\left(\alpha_{k}\right)}.

By combining (
2.22
) and (
2.24
) we get that

\displaystyle\left\|\frac{S_{\alpha_{k}}f}{\Phi\left(\alpha_{k}\right)}\right\|_{weak-L_{p}(G)}^{p}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\left\|II\right\|_{weak-L_{p}(G)}^{p}-\left\|I\right\|_{weak-L_{p}(G)}^{p}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\frac{2^{\left(\left|\alpha_{k}\right|\right)\left(1/p-1\right)/2}2^{\left\langle\alpha_{k}\right\rangle\left(1/p+1\right)/2}}{\Phi^{1/2}\left(\alpha_{k}\right)}\mu\left\{x\in G:\text{ }\left|II\right|\geq\frac{2^{\left(\left|\alpha_{k}\right|\right)\left(1/p-1\right)/2}2^{\left\langle\alpha_{k}\right\rangle\left(1/p+1\right)/2}}{\Phi^{1/2}\left(\alpha_{k}\right)}\right\}^{1/p}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\frac{2^{\left(\left|\alpha_{k}\right|\right)\left(1/p-1\right)/2}2^{\left\langle\alpha_{k}\right\rangle\left(1/p+1\right)/2}}{\Phi^{1/2}\left(\alpha_{k}\right)}\left(\mu\left\{I_{\left\langle\alpha_{k}\right\rangle}\backslash I_{\left\langle\alpha_{k}\right\rangle+1}\right\}\right)^{1/p}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle c\frac{2^{d\left(\alpha_{k}\right)\left(1/p-1\right)/2}}{\Phi^{1/2}\left(\alpha_{k}\right)}\rightarrow\infty,\ \text{ \ as \ }k\rightarrow\infty.

The proof of Theorem
2.10
is complete.

Corollary 2.11

a) Let
n\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
,
0<p<1
and
f\in H_{p}(G)
. Then there exists an absolute constant
c_{p},
depending only on
p
such that

\text{ }\left\|S_{n}f\right\|_{H_{p}(G)}\leq c_{p}\left(n\mu\left\{\text{supp}\left(D_{n}\right)\right\}\right)^{1/p-1}\left\|f\right\|_{H_{p}(G)}.

b) Let
0<p<1,

\left\{m_{k}:\text{ }k\in\mathbb{N}_{+}\right\}
be increasing sequence of natural numbers, such that

\sup_{k\in\mathbb{N}}m_{k}\mu\left\{\text{supp}\left(D_{m_{k}}\right)\right\}=\infty

(2.25)

and

\Phi:\mathbb{N}_{+}\rightarrow[1,\infty)
be non-decreasing function satisfying the condition

\overline{\underset{k\rightarrow\infty}{\lim}}\frac{\left(m_{k}\mu\left\{\text{supp}\left(D_{m_{k}}\right)\right\}\right)^{1/p-1}}{\Phi\left(m_{k}\right)}=\infty.

(2.26)

Then there exists a martingale
f\in H_{p}(G)
such that

\underset{k\in\mathbb{N}}{\sup}\left\|\frac{S_{m_{k}}f}{\Phi\left(m_{k}\right)}\right\|_{weak-L_{p}(G)}=\infty.

Proof
:
By applying both inequalities of Lemma
2.3
we get that

I_{\left\langle n\right\rangle}\backslash I_{\left\langle n\right\rangle+1}\subset\text{supp}\left\{D_{n}\right\}\subset I_{\left\langle n\right\rangle}\text{ \ and \ }2^{-\left\langle n\right\rangle-1}\leq\mu\left\{\text{supp}\left(D_{n}\right)\right\}\leq 2^{-\left\langle n\right\rangle}.

Hence,

\frac{2^{d\left(n\right)\left(1/p-1\right)}}{4}\leq\left(n\mu\left\{\text{supp}\left(D_{n}\right)\right\}\right)^{1/p-1}\leq 2^{d\left(n\right)\left(1/p-1\right)}.

Corollary
2.11
is proved.

Theorem 2.12

a) Let
n\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
and
f\in H_{1}(G).
Then there exists an absolute constant
c,
such that

\left\|S_{n}f\right\|_{H_{1}(G)}\leq cV\left(n\right)\left\|f\right\|_{H_{1}(G)}.

b) Let
\left\{m_{k}:\text{ }k\in\mathbb{N}_{+}\right\}
be non-negative increasing sequence of natural numbers such that

\sup_{k\in\mathbb{N}}V\left(m_{k}\right)=\infty

(2.27)

and
\Phi:\mathbb{N}_{+}\rightarrow[1,\infty)
be non-decreasing function satisfying the condition

\overline{\underset{k\rightarrow\infty}{\lim}}\frac{V\left(m_{k}\right)}{\Phi\left(m_{k}\right)}=\infty.

(2.28)

Then there exists a martingale
f\in H_{1}(G),
such that

\underset{{k\in\mathbb{N}}}{\sup}\left\|\frac{S_{m_{k}}f}{\Phi\left(m_{k}\right)}\right\|_{1}=\infty.

Proof
:
Since

\left\|\frac{S_{n}f}{V\left(n\right)}\right\|_{1}\leq\left\|f\right\|_{1}\leq\left\|f\right\|_{H_{1}(G)}

(2.29)

by combining Lemmas
2.9
and (
2.29
) we can conclude that

\displaystyle\left\|\frac{S_{n}f}{V\left(n\right)}\right\|_{H_{1}(G)}

(2.30)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left\|\frac{S_{n}f}{V\left(n\right)}\right\|_{1}+\frac{1}{V\left(n\right)}\left\|\widetilde{S}_{\#}^{\ast}f\right\|_{1}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle c\left\|f\right\|_{H_{1}(G)}+c\left\|\widetilde{S}_{\#}^{\ast}f\right\|_{1}\leq c\left\|f\right\|_{H_{1}(G)}.

Now prove second part of Theorem
2.12
. Let
\left\{m_{k}:\text{ }k\in\mathbb{N}_{+}\right\}
be increasing sequence of natural numbers and function
\ \Phi:\mathbb{N}_{+}\rightarrow[1,\infty)
satisfies conditions (
2.27
) and (
2.28
). Then there exists non-negative, increasing sequence
\left\{\alpha_{k}:k\in\mathbb{N}_{+}\right\}\subset\left\{m_{k}:k\in\mathbb{N}_{+}\right\}
such that
