\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\frac{\Phi^{1/2}\left(\alpha_{k}\right)}{V^{1/2}\left(\alpha_{k}\right)}\leq\beta<\infty.

(2.31)

Let
f=\left(f_{n},n\in\mathbb{N}_{+}\right)
be martingale from Example
2.6
, where

\text{\ }\lambda_{k}=\frac{\Phi^{1/2}\left(\alpha_{k}\right)}{V^{1/2}\left(\alpha_{k}\right)}.

(2.32)

By applying condition (
2.31
) we can conclude that condition (
2.3
) is fulfilled and it follows that
f=\left(f_{n},n\in\mathbb{N}_{+}\right)\in H_{1}(G).

In the view of (
2.4
) when
\lambda_{k}
are given by (
2.32
) we get that

\widehat{f}(j)=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\frac{\Phi^{1/2}\left(\alpha_{k}\right)}{V^{1/2}\left(\alpha_{k}\right)},&\text{ \ if \ }j\in\left\{2^{{}_{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|}},...,2^{{}_{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|+1}}-1\right\},\text{ }k=0,1,...\\
0,&\text{ \ if \ }j\notin\bigcup\limits_{k=0}^{\infty}\left\{2^{{}_{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|}},...,2^{{}_{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|+1}}-1\right\}.\text{ }\end{array}\right.

(2.33)

Analogously to (
2.21
) if we apply (
2.6
) when
\lambda_{k}
are given by (
2.32
) we get that

\displaystyle S_{\alpha_{k}}f

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{\eta=0}^{k-1}\frac{\Phi^{1/2}\left(\alpha_{\eta}\right)}{V^{1/2}\left(\alpha_{\eta}\right)}\left(D_{2^{\left|\alpha_{\eta}\right|+1}}-D_{2^{\left|\alpha_{\eta}\right|}}\right)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\frac{\Phi^{1/2}\left(\alpha_{k}\right)}{V^{1/2}\left(\alpha_{k}\right)}w_{2^{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|}}D_{\alpha_{k}-2^{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|}}.

By applying first estimation of Lemma
2.4
and (
2.31
) we can conclude that

\displaystyle\left\|\frac{S_{\alpha_{k}}f}{\Phi\left(\alpha_{k}\right)}\right\|_{1}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\frac{\Phi^{1/2}\left(\alpha_{k}\right)}{\Phi\left(\alpha_{k}\right)V^{1/2}\left(\alpha_{k}\right)}\left\|D_{\alpha_{k}-2^{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|}}\right\|_{1}

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\Phi\left(\alpha_{k}\right)}\sum_{\eta=0}^{k-1}\frac{\Phi^{1/2}\left(\alpha_{\eta}\right)}{V^{1/2}\left(\alpha_{\eta}\right)}\left\|D_{2^{\left|\alpha_{\eta}\right|+1}}-D_{2^{\left|\alpha_{\eta}\right|}}\right\|_{1}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\frac{V\left(\alpha_{k}-2^{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|}\right)\Phi^{1/2}\left(\alpha_{k}\right)}{8\Phi\left(\alpha_{k}\right)V^{1/2}\left(\alpha_{k}\right)}

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\Phi\left(\alpha_{k}\right)}\sum_{\eta=0}^{\infty}\frac{\Phi^{1/2}\left(\alpha_{\eta}\right)}{V^{1/2}\left(\alpha_{\eta}\right)}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\frac{cV^{1/2}\left(\alpha_{k}\right)}{\Phi^{1/2}\left(\alpha_{k}\right)}\rightarrow\infty,\text{ \ as \ }k\rightarrow\infty.

Theorem
2.12
is proved.

Corollary 2.13

Let
n\in\mathbb{N}
,
0<p\leq 1
and
f\in H_{p}(G)
. Then there exists an absolute constant
c_{p},
depending only on
p,
such that

\left\|S_{2^{n}}f\right\|_{H_{p}(G)}\leq c_{p}\left\|f\right\|_{H_{p}(G)}.

(2.34)

Proof
:
To prove Theorem
2.13
we only have to show that

\left|2^{n}\right|=n,\text{ \ \ }\left\langle 2^{n}\right\rangle=n-1\text{ \ და \ }d\left(2^{n}\right)=0.

By applying first part of Theorem
2.10
we immediately get that (
2.34
) for any
0<p\leq 1
and proof of Corollary
2.13
is proved.

Corollary 2.14

Let
n\in\mathbb{N}
,
0<p\leq 1
and
f\in H_{p}(G)
. Then there exists an absolute constant
c_{p},
depending only on
p
such that

\left\|S_{2^{n}+2^{n-1}}f\right\|_{H_{p}(G)}\leq c_{p}\left\|f\right\|_{H_{p}(G)}.

(2.35)

Proof
:
Since

\left|2^{n}+2^{n-1}\right|=n,\left\langle 2^{n}+2^{n-1}\right\rangle=n-1\text{ \ and \ }d\left(2^{n}+2^{n-1}\right)=1

by first part of Theorem
2.10
we get that (
2.35
) holds, for any
0<p\leq 1
and proof of Corollary
2.14
is complete.

Corollary 2.15

Let
n\in\mathbb{N}
and
0<p<1.
Then there exists a martingale
f\in H_{p}(G),
such that

\underset{n\in\mathbb{N}}{\sup}\left\|S_{2^{n}+1}f\right\|_{weak-L_{p}(G)}=\infty.

(2.36)

On the other hand, there exists an absolute constant
c
, such that

\left\|S_{2^{n}+1}f\right\|_{H_{1}(G)}\leq c\left\|f\right\|_{H_{1}(G)}.

(2.37)

Proof
:
Since

\left|2^{n}+1\right|=n,\left\langle 2^{n}+1\right\rangle=0\text{
\ და \ }d\left(2^{n}+1\right)=n.

(2.38)

by applying second part of Theorem
2.10
we get that there exists a martingale
f=\left(f_{n},n\in\mathbb{N}_{+}\right)\in H_{p}(G),
for
0<p<1,
such that (
2.36
) holds.

On the other hand, proof of (
2.37
) follows simple observation that

V(2^{n}+1)=4<\infty.

Corollary
2.15
is proved.

2.4
Modulus of continuity and convergence in norm of subsequences of partial sums with respect to the one-dimensional Walsh-Fourier series on the martingale Hardy spaces

In this section we apply Theorem
2.10
and Theorem
2.12
to find necessary and sufficient conditions for modulus of continuity, for which subsequences of partial sums with respect to the one-dimensional Walsh-Fourier series are bounded in the martingale Hardy spaces.

First, we prove the following estimation:

Theorem 2.16

Let
n\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
and
2^{k}<n\leq 2^{k+1}.
Then there exists an absolute constant
c_{p},
depending only on
p
such that

\left\|S_{n}f-f\right\|_{H_{p}(G)}\leq c_{p}2^{d\left(n\right)\left(1/p-1\right)}\omega_{H_{p}(G)}\left(\frac{1}{2^{k}},f\right),\text{ \ \ \ }(f\in H_{p}(G))\text{ \ \ \ }\left(0<p<1\right)

(2.39)

and

\left\|S_{n}f-f\right\|_{H_{1}(G)}\leq c_{1}V\left(n\right)\omega_{H_{1}(G)}\left(\frac{1}{2^{k}},f\right),\text{ \ \ \ }(f\in H_{1}(G)).

(2.40)

Proof
:
Let
0<p<1
and
2^{k}<n\leq 2^{k+1}.
By applying first part of Theorem
2.10
we get that

\displaystyle\left\|S_{n}f-f\right\|_{H_{p}(G)}^{p}

(2.41)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle c_{p}\left\|S_{n}f-S_{2^{k}}f\right\|_{H_{p}(G)}^{p}+c_{p}\left\|S_{2^{k}}f-f\right\|_{H_{p}(G)}^{p}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle c_{p}\left\|S_{n}\left(S_{2^{k}}f-f\right)\right\|_{H_{p}(G)}^{p}+c_{p}\left\|S_{2^{k}}f-f\right\|_{H_{p}(G)}^{p}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle c_{p}\left(1+2^{d\left(n\right)\left(1-p\right)}\right)\omega_{H_{p}(G)}^{p}\left(\frac{1}{2^{k}},f\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle c_{p}2^{d\left(n\right)\left(1-p\right)}\omega_{H_{p}(G)}^{p}\left(\frac{1}{2^{k}},f\right).

The proof of (
2.40
) is analogical to (
2.39
). Analogously to (
2.39
) we can also prove estimation (
2.40
). So, we leave out the details.

Theorem
2.16
is proved.

Theorem 2.17

a) Let
k\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
,
0<p<1,

f\in H_{p}(G)
and
\{m_{k}:k\in\mathbb{N}_{+}\}
be increasing sequence of natural numbers, such that

\omega_{H_{p}(G)}\left(\frac{1}{2^{\left|m_{k}\right|}},f\right)=o\left(\frac{1}{2^{d\left(m_{k}\right)\left(1/p-1\right)}}\right)\text{ \ as \ }k\rightarrow\infty.

(2.42)

Then

\left\|S_{m_{k}}f-f\right\|_{H_{p}(G)}\rightarrow 0\text{ \ as \ }k\rightarrow\infty.

(2.43)

b) Let
\{m_{k}:k\in\mathbb{N}_{+}\}
be increasing sequence of natural numbers,such that condition (
2.9
) is fulfilled. Then there exists a martingale
f\in H_{p}(G)
and increasing sequence of natural numbers
\{\alpha_{k}:k\in\mathbb{N}_{+}\}\subset\{m_{k}:k\in\mathbb{N}_{+}\},
such that

\omega_{H_{p}(G)}\left(\frac{1}{2^{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|}},f\right)=O\left(\frac{1}{2^{d\left(\alpha_{k}\right)\left(1/p-1\right)}}\right)\text{ \ as \ }k\rightarrow\infty\text{\ }

and

\limsup\limits_{k\rightarrow\infty}\left\|S_{\alpha_{k}}f-f\right\|_{weak-L_{p}(G)}>c_{p}>0,\text{ \ as \ }k\rightarrow\infty,

(2.44)

where
c_{p}
is an absolute constant depending only on
p
.

Proof
:
Let
0<p<1
,
f\in H_{p}(G)
and
\{m_{k}:k\in\mathbb{N}_{+}\}
be increasing sequence of natural numbers, such that condition (
2.42
) is fulfilled. By combining Theorem
2.16
and estimation (
2.39
) we get that (
2.43
) holds true.

Now, prove second part of Theorem
2.17
. In the view of (
2.9
) we simply get that there exists sequence
\{\alpha_{k}:k\in\mathbb{N}_{+}\}\subset\{m_{k}:k\in\mathbb{N}_{+}\},
such that

\ 2^{d\left(\alpha_{k}\right)}\uparrow\infty,\,\,\,\,\text{ \ as\ }\ \ k\rightarrow\infty\text{, \ \ \ }2^{2\left(1/p-1\right)d\left(\alpha_{k}\right)}\leq 2^{\left(1/p-1\right)d\left(\alpha_{k+1}\right)}.

(2.45)

Let
f=\left(f_{n},n\in\mathbb{N}\right)
be a martingale from Example
2.6
, such that

\lambda_{i}={2^{-(1/p-1)d\left(\alpha_{i}\right)}}.

(2.46)

By applying (
2.45
) we obtain that condition (
2.3
) is fulfilled and it follows that
\ f\in H_{p}(G).

By applying (
2.4
), whene
\lambda_{k}
are given by (
2.46
), then

\widehat{f}(j)=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}2^{\left(1/p-1\right)\left\langle\alpha_{k}\right\rangle},&\text{ \ if
\ }j\in\left\{2^{{}_{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|}},...,2^{{}_{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|+1}}-1\right\},\text{ }k\in\mathbb{N}_{+},\\
0,&\text{\ if \ }j\notin\bigcup\limits_{k=0}^{\infty}\left\{2^{{}_{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|}},...,2^{{}_{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|+1}}-1\right\}.\text{ }\end{array}\right.

(2.47)

By combining (
2.45
) and (
2.7
) we have that

\displaystyle\omega_{H_{p}(G)}(\frac{1}{2^{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|}},f)

(2.48)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sum\limits_{i=k}^{\infty}\frac{1}{2^{\left(1/p-1\right)d\left(\alpha_{i}\right)}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle O\left(\frac{1}{2^{\left(1/p-1\right)d\left(\alpha_{k}\right)}}\right),\text{ \ as \ }k\rightarrow\infty.

By using (
2.23
) we get that

\left|D_{\alpha_{k}-2^{\left\langle\alpha_{k}\right\rangle}}\right|\geq 2^{\left\langle\alpha_{k}\right\rangle},\text{ \ \ \ \ where \ \ \ }I_{\left\langle\alpha_{k}\right\rangle}\backslash I_{\left\langle\alpha_{k}\right\rangle+1}.

In the view of (
2.6
) we can conclude that

S_{\alpha_{k}}f=S_{2^{|\alpha_{k}|}}f+2^{\left(1/p-1\right)\left\langle\alpha_{k}\right\rangle}{w_{2^{|\alpha_{k}|}}D_{{\alpha_{k}}-2^{|\alpha_{k}|}}},

Since

\displaystyle\|D_{\alpha_{k}}\|_{weak-L_{p}(G)}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle 2^{\left\langle\alpha_{k}\right\rangle}\mu\left\{x\in I_{\left\langle\alpha_{k}\right\rangle}\backslash I_{\left\langle\alpha_{k}\right\rangle+1}:\text{ }\left|D_{\alpha_{k}}\right|\geq 2^{\left\langle\alpha_{k}\right\rangle}\right\}^{1/p}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle 2^{\left\langle\alpha_{k}\right\rangle}\left(\mu\left\{I_{\left\langle\alpha_{k}\right\rangle}\backslash I_{\left\langle\alpha_{k}\right\rangle+1}\right\}\right)^{1/p}\geq 2^{\left\langle\alpha_{k}\right\rangle\left(1-1/p\right)},

if we apply (
1.2
) (see also Theorem T2) we obtain that

\displaystyle\|f-S_{\alpha_{k}}f\|_{weak-L_{p}(G)}^{p}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle 2^{\left(1-p\right)\left\langle\alpha_{k}\right\rangle}\|{w_{2^{|\alpha_{k}|}}D_{{\alpha_{k}}-2^{|\alpha_{k}|}}}\|_{weak-L_{p}(G)}^{p}

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle\|f-S_{2^{|\alpha_{k}|}}f\|_{weak-L_{p}(G)}^{p}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle c-o(1)>c>0,\text{\ \ \ as \ \ }k\rightarrow\infty.

Proof of Theorem
2.17
is complete.

Corollary 2.18

a) Let
0<p<1,

f\in H_{p}(G)
and
\{m_{k}:k\in\mathbb{N}_{+}\}
be increasing sequence of natural numbers, such that

\omega_{H_{p}(G)}\left(\frac{1}{2^{\left|m_{k}\right|}},f\right)=o\left(\frac{1}{\left(m_{k}\mu\left(\text{supp}D_{m_{k}}\right)\right)^{1/p-1}}\right)\text{ \ as\ }k\rightarrow\infty.

(2.49)

Then (
2.43
) holds.

b) Let
\{m_{k}:k\in\mathbb{N}_{+}\}
be increasing sequence of natural numbers, such that

\sup_{k\in\mathbb{N}_{+}}m_{k}\mu\left\{\text{supp}\left(D_{m_{k}}\right)\right\}=\infty.

(2.50)

Then there exist a martingale
f\in H_{p}(G)
and sequence
\{\alpha_{k}:k\in\mathbb{N}_{+}\}\subset\{m_{k}:k\in\mathbb{N}_{+}\},
such that

\omega_{H_{p}(G)}\left(\frac{1}{2^{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|}},f\right)=O\left(\frac{1}{\left(\alpha_{k}\mu\left(\text{supp}D_{\alpha_{k}}\right)\right)^{1/p-1}}\right)\text{\ \ as \ }k\rightarrow\infty

and (
2.44
) holds.

Theorem 2.19

a) Let
f\in H_{1}(G)
and
\{m_{k}:k\in\mathbb{N}_{+}\}
be increasing sequence of natural numbers, such that

\omega_{H_{1}(G)}\left(\frac{1}{2^{\left|m_{k}\right|}},f\right)=o\left(\frac{1}{V\left(m_{k}\right)}\right)\text{ \ as \ }k\rightarrow\infty.

(2.51)

Then

\left\|S_{m_{k}}f-f\right\|_{H_{1}(G)}\rightarrow 0\text{ \ as \ }k\rightarrow\infty.

(2.52)

b) Let
\{m_{k}:k\in\mathbb{N}_{+}\}
be increasing sequence of natural numbers, such that condition (
2.27
) is fulfilled. Then there exists a martingale
f\in H_{1}(G)
and increasing sequence of natural numbers

\{\alpha_{k}:k\in\mathbb{N}_{+}\}\subset\{m_{k}:k\in\mathbb{N}_{+}\}
such that

\omega_{H_{1}(G)}\left(\frac{1}{2^{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|}},f\right)=O\left(\frac{1}{V\left(\alpha_{k}\right)}\right)\text{ \ as
\ }k\rightarrow\infty

and

\limsup\limits_{k\rightarrow\infty}\left\|S_{\alpha_{k}}f-f\right\|_{1}>c>0\,\,\,\text{\ as \ }k\rightarrow\infty,

(2.53)

where
c
is an absolute constant.

Proof
:
Let
f\in H_{1}(G)
and
\{m_{k}:k\in\mathbb{N}_{+}\}
be increasing sequence of natural numbers, such that (
2.51
). By applying Theorem
2.16
we get that condition (
2.52
) is fulfilled.

Now, we prove second part of Theorem
2.19
. By applying (
2.27
) we conclude that there exists sequence
\{\alpha_{k}:k\in\mathbb{N}_{+}\}\subset\{m_{k}:k\in\mathbb{N}_{+}\}
, such that

V(\alpha_{k})\uparrow\infty,\text{ \ \ as \ \ }k\rightarrow\infty\text{ \ \ and \ \ }V^{2}(\alpha_{k})\leq V(\alpha_{k+1})\text{ \ \ }k\in\mathbb{N}_{+}.

(2.54)

Let
f=\left(f_{n},n\in\mathbb{N}_{+}\right)
be a martingale from the Example
2.6
, where

\lambda_{k}=\frac{1}{V(\alpha_{k})}.

By applying (

) we conclude that (
2.3
) is fulfilled and we conclude that
f=\left(f_{n},n\in\mathbb{N}_{+}\right)\in H_{1}(G).

In the view of (
2.4
) we have that

\widehat{f}(j)=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\frac{1}{V(\alpha_{k})},&\text{ თუ \thinspace\thinspace}j\in\left\{2^{{}_{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|}},...,2^{{}_{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|+1}}-1\right\},\text{ }k=0,1,...\\
0,&\text{\ თუ \thinspace\thinspace\thinspace}j\notin\bigcup\limits_{k=0}^{\infty}\left\{2^{{}_{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|}},...,2^{{}_{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|+1}}-1\right\}.\text{ }\end{array}\right.

(2.55)

According to (
2.7
) we get that

\displaystyle w_{H_{1}(G)}(1/2^{n},f)

(2.56)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left\|f-S_{2^{n}}f\right\|_{H_{1}(G)}\leq\sum\limits_{i=n+1}^{\infty}\frac{1}{V(\alpha_{i})}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle O\left(\frac{1}{V(\alpha_{n})}\right),\text{\ \ as \ \ }n\rightarrow\infty.

By applying (
2.6
) we can conclude that

S_{\alpha_{k}}f=S_{2^{|\alpha_{k}|}}f+\frac{w_{2^{|\alpha_{k}|}}D_{{\alpha_{k}}-2^{|\alpha_{k}|}}}{V(\alpha_{k})},

If we use (
1.2
) and Theorem T2 we get that

\displaystyle\|f-S_{\alpha_{k}}f\|_{1}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\|\frac{w_{2^{|\alpha_{k}|}}D_{{\alpha_{k}}-2^{|\alpha_{k}|}}}{V(\alpha_{k})}\|_{1}-\|f-S_{2^{|\alpha_{k}|}}f\|_{1}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\frac{V(\alpha_{k}-2^{|\alpha_{k}|})}{8V(\alpha_{k})}-o(1)>c>0,\text{\ \ as \ \ }k\rightarrow\infty.

The proof of Theorem
2.19
is proved.

follows the following corollaries which are

:

Corollary 2.20

a) Let
0<p<1,

f\in H_{p}(G)
and

\left(0<p<1\right),

\left\|S_{k}f-f\right\|_{H_{1}(G)}\rightarrow 0,\text{\ as \ }k\rightarrow\infty.

b) There exists a martingale
f\in H_{1}(G),

3
Fejér means with respect to the one-dimensional Walsh-Fourier series on the martingale Hardy spaces

3.1
Basic notations

Fot the one-dimensional case Fejér means with respect to the one-dimensional Walsh-Fourier series
\sigma_{n}
is defined by:

\displaystyle\qquad\sigma_{n}f(x)

\displaystyle:

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{k=1}^{n}S_{k}f(x)\text{\qquad\ \ \ }\left(\text{ }n\in\mathbb{N}_{+}\right).

The following equality is true (for details see
[
2
]
and
[
47
]
):

\sigma_{n}f\left(x\right)=\frac{1}{n}\overset{n-1}{\underset{k=0}{\sum}}\left(D_{k}\ast f\right)\left(x\right)

=\left(f\ast K_{n}\right)\left(x\right)=\int_{G_{m}}f\left(t\right)K_{n}\left(x-t\right)d\mu\left(t\right).

where

\displaystyle K_{n}(x)

\displaystyle:

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n}\overset{n}{\underset{k=1}{\sum}}D_{k}(x)\text{ \qquad\thinspace}\left(\text{ }n\in\mathbb{N}_{+}\text{ }\right)

In the literature
K_{n}
is called
n
-th Fejér kernel.

We also define the following maximal operators

\displaystyle{\sigma}^{\ast}f

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sup_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\left|\sigma_{{n}}f\right|

\displaystyle\widetilde{\sigma}_{\#}^{\ast}f

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sup_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\left|\sigma_{2^{n}}f\right|.

.

For any natural number
n\in\mathbb{N}
we also need the following expression

n=\sum_{i=1}^{s}2^{n_{i}},\text{ \qquad\thinspace}n_{1}<n_{2}<...<n_{s}.

Set

n^{\left(i\right)}:=2^{n_{1}}+...+2^{n_{i-1}},\text{ \ }i=2,...,s

and

\mathbb{A}_{0,2}:=\left\{n\in\mathbb{N}:\text{ }n=2^{0}+2^{2}+\sum_{i=3}^{s_{n}}2^{n_{i}}\right\}.

Then, for any natural number
n\in\mathbb{N}
there exists numbers

0\leq l_{1}\leq m_{1}\leq l_{2}-2<l_{2}\leq m_{2}\leq...\leq l_{s}-2<l_{s}\leq m_{s}

such that it can be written as

n=\sum_{i=1}^{s}\sum_{k=l_{i}}^{m_{i}}2^{k},

where
s
is depending on
n
.

It is evident that

s\leq V\left(n\right)\leq 2s+1.

3.2
Auxiliary lemmas

The following equality and estimation of Fejér kernels with respect to the one-dimensional Walsh-Fourier series is proved in
[
47
]
:

Lemma 3.22

Let
n\in\mathbb{N}
and
n=\sum_{i=1}^{s}2^{n_{i}},

n_{1}<n_{2}<...<n_{s}
. Then

nK_{n}=\sum_{r=1}^{s}\left(\underset{j=r+1}{\overset{s}{\prod}}w_{2^{n_{j}}}\right)2^{n_{r}}K_{2^{n_{r}}}+\sum_{t=2}^{s}\left(\underset{j=t+1}{\overset{s}{\prod}}w_{2^{n_{j}}}\right)n^{\left(t\right)}D_{2^{n_{t}}},

and

\sup_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\int_{G}\left|K_{n}\left(x\right)\right|d\mu\left(x\right)\leq c<\infty,

where
c
is an absolute constant.

The following equality is proved in
[
47
]
(see also
[
16
]
):

Lemma 3.23

Let
n>t
and
t,n\in\mathbb{N}
. Then we have the following expression for
2^{n}
-th Fejér kernels with respect to the one-dimensional Walsh-Fourier series:

K_{2^{n}}\left(x\right)=\left\{\begin{array}[]{c}\text{ }2^{t-1},\text{\ if \ }x\in I_{n}\left(e_{t}\right),\\
\frac{2^{n}+1}{2},\text{ \ if \ }x\in I_{n},\text{\ }\\
0,\text{ \ otherwise.\ }\end{array}\right.

The following estimation is proved by Goginava
[
22
]
:

Lemma 3.24

Let
x\in I_{l+1}\left(e_{k}+e_{l}\right),\ \ k=0,...,M-2,\ \ l=0,...,M-1.

Then

\int_{I_{M}}\left|K_{n}\left(x+t\right)\right|d\mu\left(t\right)\leq\frac{c2^{l+k}}{n2^{M}},\text{ \ where \ }n>2^{M}.

Let
x\in I_{M}\left(e_{k}\right),m=0,...,M-1.
Then

\int_{I_{M}}\left|K_{n}\left(x+t\right)\right|d\mu\left(t\right)\leq\frac{c2^{k}}{2^{M}},\text{ \ \ for \ \ }n>2^{M},

where
c
is an absolute constant.

The following estimations of Fejér kernels with respect to the one-dimensional Walsh-Fourier series is proved in
[
64
]
:

Lemma 3.25

Let

n=\sum_{i=1}^{r}\sum_{k=l_{i}}^{m_{i}}2^{k},

where

m_{1}\geq l_{1}>l_{1}-2\geq m_{2}\geq l_{2}>l_{2}-2>...>m_{s}\geq l_{s}\geq 0.

Then

\left|nK_{n}\right|\leq c\sum_{A=1}^{r}\left(2^{l_{A}}\left|K_{2^{l_{A}}}\right|+2^{m_{A}}\left|K_{2^{m_{A}}}\right|+2^{l_{A}}\sum_{k=l_{A}}^{m_{A}}D_{2^{k}}\right)+cV\left(n\right),

where
c
is an absolute constant.

Proof
:
Let

n=\sum_{i=1}^{r}2^{n_{i}},n_{1}>n_{2}>...>n_{r}\geq 0.

By using Lemma
3.22
for
n
-th Fejér kernels we can conclude that

\displaystyle nK_{n}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{A=1}^{r}\left(\underset{j=1}{\overset{A-1}{\prod}}w_{2^{n_{j}}}\right)\left(\left(2^{n_{A}}K_{2^{n_{A}}}+\left(2^{n_{A}}-1\right)D_{2^{n_{A}}}\right)\right)

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle\sum_{A=1}^{r}\left(\underset{j=1}{\overset{A-1}{\prod}}w_{2^{n_{j}}}\right)\left(2^{n_{A}}-1-n^{\left(A\right)}\right)D_{2^{n_{A}}}=I_{1}-I_{2}.

For
I_{1}
we have the following equality

\displaystyle I_{1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{v=1}^{r}\left(\underset{j=1}{\overset{v-1}{\prod}}\underset{i=l_{j}}{\overset{m_{j}}{\prod}}w_{2^{i}}\right)\left(\sum_{k=l_{v}}^{m_{v}}\left(\underset{j=k+1}{\overset{m_{v}}{\prod}}w_{2^{j}}\right)\left(2^{k}K_{2^{k}}-\left(2^{k}-1\right)D_{2^{k}}\right)\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{v=1}^{r}\left(\underset{j=1}{\overset{v-1}{\prod}}\underset{i=l_{j}}{\overset{m_{j}}{\prod}}w_{2^{i}}\right)\left(\sum_{k=0}^{m_{v}}-\sum_{k=0}^{l_{v}-1}\right)\left(\underset{j=k+1}{\overset{m_{v}}{\prod}}w_{2^{j}}\right)\left(2^{k}K_{2^{k}}-\left(2^{k}-1\right)D_{2^{k}}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{v=1}^{r}\left(\underset{j=1}{\overset{v-1}{\prod}}\underset{i=l_{j}}{\overset{m_{j}}{\prod}}w_{2^{i}}\right)\left(\sum_{k=0}^{m_{v}}\left(\underset{j=k+1}{\overset{m_{v}}{\prod}}w_{2^{j}}\right)\left(2^{k}K_{2^{k}}-\left(2^{k}-1\right)D_{2^{k}}\right)\right)

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle\sum_{v=1}^{r}\left(\underset{j=1}{\overset{v}{\prod}}\underset{i=l_{j}}{\overset{m_{j}}{\prod}}w_{2^{i}}\right)\left(\sum_{k=0}^{l_{v}-1}\left(\underset{j=k+1}{\overset{l_{v}-1}{\prod}}w_{2^{j}}\right)\left(2^{k}K_{2^{k}}-\left(2^{k}-1\right)D_{2^{k}}\right)\right).

Since

2^{n}-1=\sum_{k=0}^{n-1}2^{k}

and

\left(2^{n}-1\right)K_{2^{n}-1}=\sum_{k=0}^{n-1}\left(\prod_{j=k+1}^{n-1}w_{2^{j}}\right)\left(2^{k}K_{2^{k}}-\left(2^{k}-1\right)D_{2^{k}}\right),

we obtain that

\displaystyle I_{1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{v=1}^{r}\left(\underset{j=1}{\overset{v-1}{\prod}}\underset{i=l_{j}}{\overset{m_{j}}{\prod}}w_{2^{i}}\right)\left(2^{m_{v}+1}-1\right)K_{2^{m_{v}+1}-1}

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle\sum_{v=1}^{r}\left(\underset{j=1}{\overset{v}{\prod}}\underset{i=l_{j}}{\overset{m_{j}}{\prod}}w_{2^{i}}\right)\left(2^{l_{v}}-1\right)K_{2^{l_{v}}-1}.

If we apply estimations

\left|K_{2^{n}}\right|\leq c\left|K_{2^{n-1}}\right|

and

\left|K_{2^{n}-1}\right|\leq c\left|K_{2^{n}}\right|+c\

we get that

\left|I_{1}\right|\leq c\sum_{v=1}^{r}\left(2^{l_{v}}\left|K_{2^{l_{v}}}\right|+2^{m_{v}}\left|K_{2^{m_{v}}}\right|+cr\right).

(3.1)

Let
l_{j}<n_{A}\leq m_{j},
where
j=1,...,s.
Then

n^{\left(A\right)}\geq\sum_{v=l_{j}}^{n_{A}-1}2^{v}\geq 2^{n_{A}}-2^{l_{j}}

and

2^{n_{A}}-1-n^{\left(A\right)}\leq 2^{l_{j}}.

If
l_{j}=n_{A}
where
j=1,...,s,
then

n^{\left(A\right)}\leq 2^{m_{j-1}+1}<2^{l_{j}}.

By using these estimations we can conclude that

\left|I_{2}\right|\leq c\sum_{v=1}^{r}2^{l_{v}}\sum_{k=l_{v}}^{m_{v}}D_{2^{k}}.

(3.2)

By combining (
3.1
)-(
3.2
) we get the proof of Lemma
3.25
.

The following estimations of Fejér kernels with respect to the one-dimensional Walsh-Fourier series is proved in
[
64
]
:

Lemma 3.26

Let

n=\sum_{i=1}^{s}\sum_{k=l_{i}}^{m_{i}}2^{k},

where

0\leq l_{1}\leq m_{1}\leq l_{2}-2<l_{2}\leq m_{2}\leq...\leq l_{s}-2<l_{s}\leq m_{s}.

Then

n\left|K_{n}\left(x\right)\right|\geq\frac{2^{2l_{i}}}{16},\text{ \ \ for \ \ }x\in I_{l_{i}+1}\left(e_{l_{i}-1}+e_{l_{i}}\right).

Proof
:
If we apply Lemma
3.22
for
n=\sum_{i=1}^{s}\sum_{k=l_{i}}^{m_{i}}2^{k}
we can write that

\displaystyle nK_{n}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{r=1}^{s}\sum_{k=l_{r}}^{m_{r}}\left(\underset{j=r+1}{\overset{s}{\prod}}\underset{q=l_{j}}{\overset{m_{j}}{\prod}}w_{2^{q}}\underset{j=k+1}{\overset{m_{r}}{\prod}}w_{2^{j}}\right)2^{k}K_{2^{k}}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\sum_{r=1}^{s}\sum_{k=l_{r}}^{m_{r}}\left(\underset{j=r+1}{\overset{s}{\prod}}\underset{q=l_{j}}{\overset{m_{j}}{\prod}}w_{2^{q}}\underset{j=k+1}{\overset{m_{r}}{\prod}}w_{2^{j}}\right)\left(\sum_{t=1}^{r-1}\sum_{q=l_{t}}^{m_{t}}2^{q}+\sum_{q=l_{r}}^{k-1}2^{q}\right)D_{2^{k}}.

Let
x\in I_{l_{i}+1}\left(e_{l_{i}-1}+e_{l_{i}}\right).
Then

\displaystyle n\left|K_{n}\right|\geq\left|2^{l_{i}}K_{2^{l_{i}}}\right|-\sum_{r=1}^{i-1}\sum_{k=l_{r}}^{m_{r}}\left|2^{k}K_{2^{k}}\right|-\sum_{r=1}^{i-1}\sum_{k=l_{r}}^{m_{r}}\left|2^{k}D_{2^{k}}\right|=I-II-III.

Lemma
3.23
follows that

I=\left|2^{l_{i}}K_{2^{l_{i}}}\left(x\right)\right|=\frac{2^{2l_{i}}}{4}.

(3.3)

Since
m_{i-1}\leq l_{i}-2
, we easily obtain that the following estimation is true:

\displaystyle II

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{n=0}^{l_{i}-2}\left|2^{n}K_{2^{n}}\left(x\right)\right|

(3.4)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{n=0}^{l_{i}-2}2^{n}\frac{\left(2^{n}+1\right)}{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{2^{2l_{i}}}{24}+\frac{2^{l_{i}}}{4}-\frac{2}{3}.

For
III
we get that

III\leq\sum_{k=0}^{l_{i}-2}\left|2^{k}D_{2^{k}}\left(x\right)\right|\leq\sum_{k=0}^{l_{i}-2}4^{k}=\frac{2^{2l_{i}}}{12}-\frac{1}{3}.

(3.5)

By combining (
3.3
-
3.5
) we can conclude that

n\left|K_{n}\left(x\right)\right|\geq I-II-III\geq\frac{2^{2l_{i}}}{8}-\frac{2^{l_{i}}}{4}+1.

(3.6)

Suppose that
l_{i}\geq 2
. Then

n\left|K_{n}\left(x\right)\right|\geq\frac{2^{2l_{i}}}{8}-\frac{2^{2l_{i}}}{16}\geq\frac{2^{2l_{i}}}{16}.

If
l_{i}=0
or
l_{i}=1
, then by applying (
3.6
) we get that

n\left|K_{n}\left(x\right)\right|\geq\frac{7}{8}\geq\frac{2^{2l_{i}}}{16},

Lemma is proved.

The following estimations of Fejér kernels with respect to the one-dimensional Walsh-Fourier series is proved in
[
64
]
(see also
[
74
]
):

Lemma 3.27

Let
0<p\leq 1
,
2^{k}\leq n<2^{k+1}
and
\sigma_{n}f
be Fejér means with respect to the one-dimensional Walsh-Fourier series, where
f\in H_{p}(G)
. Then, for any fixed
n\in\mathbb{N}
,

\displaystyle\left\|\sigma_{n}f\right\|_{H_{p}(G)}

\displaystyle\leq\left\|\sup_{0\leq l\leq k}\left|\sigma_{2^{l}}f\right|\right\|_{p}+\left\|\sup_{0\leq l\leq k}\left|S_{2^{l}}f\right|\right\|_{p}+\left\|\sigma_{n}f\right\|_{p}

\displaystyle\leq\left\|\widetilde{\sigma}_{\#}^{\ast}f\right\|_{p}+\left\|\widetilde{S}_{\#}^{\ast}f\right\|_{p}+\left\|\sigma_{n}f\right\|_{p}.

Proof
:
Let consider the following martingale

\displaystyle f_{\#}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(S_{2^{k}}\sigma_{n}f,\text{ }k\in\mathbb{N}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(\frac{2^{0}\sigma_{2^{0}}}{n}+\frac{(n-2^{0})S_{2^{0}}f}{n},...,\frac{2^{k}\sigma_{2^{k}}f}{n}+\frac{(n-2^{k})S_{2^{k}}f}{n},\sigma_{n}f,...,\sigma_{n}f,...\right).

By using Lemma
2.8
we immediately get

\displaystyle\left\|\sigma_{n}f\right\|_{H_{p}(G^{2})}^{p}

\displaystyle\leq\left\|\sup_{0\leq l\leq k}\left|\sigma_{2^{l}}f\right|\right\|_{p}^{p}+\left\|\sup_{0\leq l\leq k}\left|S_{2^{l}}f\right|\right\|_{p}^{p}+\left\|S_{n}f\right\|_{p}^{p}

\displaystyle\leq\left\|\widetilde{\sigma}_{\#}^{\ast}f\right\|_{p}^{p}+\left\|\widetilde{S}_{\#}^{\ast}f\right\|_{p}^{p}+\left\|\sigma_{n}f\right\|_{p}^{p}.

Lemma is proved.

3.3
Boundedness of subsequences of Fejér means with respect to the one-dimensional Walsh-Fourier series on the martingale Hardy spaces

In this section we study boundedness of subsequences of Fejér means with respect to the one-dimensional Walsh-Fourier series in the martingale Hardy spaces (For details see
[
64
]
).

First, we consider case
p=1/2
. The following estimation is true:

Theorem 3.28

a) Let
f\in H_{1/2}(G).
Then there exists an absolute constant
c,
such that

\left\|\sigma_{n}f\right\|_{H_{1/2}(G)}\leq cV^{2}\left(n\right)\left\|f\right\|_{H_{1/2}(G)}.

b) Let
\left\{n_{k}:k\in\mathbb{N}_{+}\right\}
be increasing sequence of natural numbers, such that
\sup_{k\in\mathbb{N}_{+}}V\left(n_{k}\right)=\infty
and
\Phi:\mathbb{N}_{+}\rightarrow[1,\infty)
be non-decreasing function satisfying the conditions

\Phi\left(n\right)\uparrow\infty

and

\overline{\underset{k\rightarrow\infty}{\lim}}\frac{V^{2}\left(n_{k}\right)}{\Phi\left(n_{k}\right)}=\infty.

(3.7)

Then there exists a martingale
f\in H_{1/2}(G),
such that

\underset{k\in\mathbb{N}}{\sup}\left\|\frac{\sigma_{n_{k}}f}{\Phi\left(n_{k}\right)}\right\|_{1/2}=\infty.

Proof
:
Suppose that

\left\|\frac{\sigma_{n}f}{V^{2}\left(n\right)}\right\|_{1/2}\leq c\left\|f\right\|_{H_{1/2}(G)}.

(3.8)

By combining estimations (
1.7
), (
1.15
) and Lemma
3.27
we can conclude that

\displaystyle\left\|\frac{\sigma_{n}f}{V^{2}\left(n\right)}\right\|_{H_{1/2}(G)}^{1/2}

(3.9)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left\|\frac{\sigma_{n}f}{V^{2}\left(n\right)}\right\|_{1/2}^{1/2}+\frac{1}{V^{2}\left(n\right)}\left\|\sigma_{\#}^{\ast}f\right\|_{1/2}^{1/2}+\frac{1}{V^{2}\left(n\right)}\left\|\widetilde{S}_{\#}^{\ast}\right\|_{1/2}^{1/2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left\|\frac{\widetilde{\sigma}_{n}f}{V^{2}\left(n\right)}\right\|_{1/2}^{1/2}+\left\|\widetilde{\sigma}_{\#}^{\ast}f\right\|_{1/2}^{1/2}+\left\|\widetilde{S}_{\#}^{\ast}f\right\|_{1/2}^{1/2}\leq c\left\|f\right\|_{H_{1/2}(G)}^{1/2}.

By combining Lemma
2.7
and (
3.9
), Theorem
3.28
will be proved if we show that

\int_{\overline{I_{M}}}\left(\frac{\left|\sigma_{n}a\right|}{V^{2}\left(n\right)}\right)^{1/2}d\mu\leq c<\infty,

for any
1/2
-atom
a
.

Without loss the generality we may assume that
a
is
1/2
-atom, with support
I,
for which
\mu\left(I\right)=2^{-M},

I=I_{M}.
It is easy to check that
\sigma_{n}\left(a\right)=0,
when
n\leq 2^{M}.
Therefore, we may assume that
n>2^{M}.
Set

\displaystyle II_{\alpha_{A}}^{1}\left(x\right):=2^{M}\int_{I_{M}}2^{\alpha_{A}}\left|K_{2^{\alpha_{A}}}\left(x+t\right)\right|d\mu\left(t\right),\text{\ }

\displaystyle II_{l_{A}}^{2}\left(x\right)=2^{M}\int_{I_{M}}2^{l_{A}}\sum_{k=l_{A}}^{m_{A}}D_{2^{k}}\left(x+t\right)d\mu\left(t\right).

Let
x\in I_{M}.
Since
\sigma_{n}
is bounded from
L_{\infty}(G)
to
L_{\infty}(G)
, for
n>2^{M}
and
\left\|a\right\|_{\infty}\leq 2^{2M},
by using Lemma
3.24
we can conclude that

\displaystyle\frac{\left|\sigma_{n}a\left(x\right)\right|}{V^{2}\left(n\right)}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{c}{V^{2}\left(n\right)}\int_{I_{M}}\left|a\left(x\right)\right|\left|K_{n}\left(x+t\right)\right|d\mu\left(t\right)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{c\left\|a\right\|_{\infty}}{V^{2}\left(n\right)}\int_{I_{M}}\left|K_{n}\left(x+t\right)\right|d\mu\left(t\right)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{c2^{2M}}{V^{2}\left(n\right)}\int_{I_{M}}\left|K_{n}\left(x+t\right)\right|d\mu\left(t\right)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{c2^{M}}{V^{2}\left(n\right)}\left\{\sum_{A=1}^{s}\int_{I_{M}}2^{l_{A}}\left|K_{2^{l_{A}}}\left(x+t\right)\right|d\mu\left(t\right)+\int_{I_{M}}2^{m_{A}}\left|K_{2^{m_{A}}}\left(x+t\right)\right|d\mu\left(t\right)\right\}

\displaystyle+\frac{c2^{M}}{V^{2}\left(n\right)}\sum_{A=1}^{s}\int_{I_{M}}2^{l_{A}}\sum_{k=l_{A}}^{m_{A}}D_{2^{k}}\left(x+t\right)d\mu\left(t\right)+\frac{c2^{M}}{V^{2}\left(n\right)}\int_{I_{M}}V\left(n\right)d\mu\left(t\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{c}{V^{2}\left(n\right)}\sum_{A=1}^{s}\left(II_{{}^{l_{A}}}^{1}\left(x\right)+II_{{}^{m_{A}}}^{1}\left(x\right)+II_{l_{A}}^{2}\left(x\right)\right)+c.

Hence,

\displaystyle\int_{\overline{I_{M}}}\left|\frac{\sigma_{n}a\left(x\right)}{V^{2}\left(n\right)}\right|^{1/2}d\mu\left(x\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{c}{V\left(n\right)}\left(\sum_{A=1}^{s}\int_{\overline{I_{M}}}\left|II_{l_{A}}^{1}\left(x\right)\right|^{1/2}d\mu\left(x\right)\right.

\displaystyle\left.+\int_{\overline{I_{M}}}\left|II_{m_{A}}^{1}\left(x\right)\right|^{1/2}d\mu\left(x\right)+\int_{\overline{I_{M}}}\left|II_{l_{A}}^{2}\left(x\right)\right|^{1/2}d\mu\left(x\right)\right)+c.

Since
s\leq 4V\left(n\right)
we obtain that Theorem
3.28
will be proved if we show that

\int_{\overline{I_{M}}}\left|II_{\alpha_{A}}^{1}\left(x\right)\right|^{1/2}d\mu\left(x\right)\leq c<\infty,\text{\ }\int_{\overline{I_{M}}}\left|II_{l_{A}}^{2}\left(x\right)\right|^{1/2}d\mu\left(x\right)\leq c<\infty,\text{\ }

(3.10)

where
\alpha_{A}=l_{A}
or
\alpha_{A}=m_{A},

A=1,...,s
.

Let
t\in I_{M}
and
x\in I_{l+1}\left(e_{k}+e_{l}\right),

0\leq k<l<\alpha_{A}\leq M
or
0\leq k<l\leq M\leq\alpha_{A}.
Since
x+t\in I_{l+1}\left(e_{k}+e_{l}\right),
by applying Lemma
3.23
we can conclude that

K_{2^{\alpha_{A}}}\left(x+t\right)=0\text{ \ \ and \ \ }II_{\alpha_{A}}^{1}\left(x\right)=0.

(3.11)

Let
x\in I_{l+1}\left(e_{k}+e_{l}\right),

0\leq k<\alpha_{A}\leq l\leq M.
Then
x+t\in I_{l+1}\left(e_{k}+e_{l}\right),
where
t\in I_{M}
and if we apply again Lemma
3.23
we get that

2^{\alpha_{A}}\left|K_{2^{\alpha_{A}}}\left(x+t\right)\right|\leq 2^{\alpha_{A}+k}\text{ \ \ and \ \ }II_{\alpha_{A}}^{1}\left(x\right)\leq 2^{\alpha_{A}+k}.

(3.12)

Analogously to (
3.12
) for
0\leq\alpha_{A}\leq k<l\leq M
we can prove that

2^{\alpha_{A}}\left|K_{2^{\alpha_{A}}}\left(x+t\right)\right|\leq 2^{2\alpha_{A}},\text{ \ }II_{\alpha_{A}}^{1}\left(x\right)\leq 2^{2\alpha_{A}},\text{\ }t\in I_{M},\text{\ }x\in I_{l+1}\left(e_{k}+e_{l}\right).

(3.13)

Let
0\leq\alpha_{A}\leq M-1,
where
A=1,...,s.
According to (
2.1
) and (
3.11
-
3.13
) we find that

\displaystyle\int_{\overline{I_{M}}}\left|II_{\alpha_{A}}^{1}\left(x\right)\right|^{1/2}d\mu\left(x\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\overset{M-2}{\underset{k=0}{\sum}}\overset{M-1}{\underset{l=k+1}{\sum}}\int_{I_{l+1}\left(e_{k}+e_{l}\right)}\left|II_{\alpha_{A}}^{1}\left(x\right)\right|^{1/2}d\mu\left(x\right)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\overset{M-1}{\underset{k=0}{\sum}}\int_{I_{M}\left(e_{k}\right)}\left|II_{\alpha_{A}}^{1}\left(x\right)\right|^{1/2}d\mu\left(x\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle c\overset{\alpha_{A}-1}{\underset{k=0}{\sum}}\overset{M-1}{\underset{l=\alpha_{A}+1}{\sum}}\int_{I_{l+1}\left(e_{k}+e_{l}\right)}2^{\left(\alpha_{A}+k\right)/2}d\mu\left(x\right)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle c\overset{M-2}{\underset{k=\alpha_{A}}{\sum}}\overset{M-1}{\underset{l=k+1}{\sum}}\int_{I_{l+1}\left(e_{k}+e_{l}\right)}2^{\alpha_{A}}d\mu\left(x\right)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle c\overset{\alpha_{A}-1}{\underset{k=0}{\sum}}\int_{I_{M}\left(e_{k}\right)}2^{\left(\alpha_{A}+k\right)/2}d\mu\left(x\right)+c\overset{M-1}{\underset{k=\alpha_{A}}{\sum}}\int_{I_{M}\left(e_{k}\right)}2^{\alpha_{A}}d\mu\left(x\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle c\overset{\alpha_{A}-1}{\underset{k=0}{\sum}}\overset{M-1}{\underset{l=\alpha_{A}+1}{\sum}}\frac{2^{\left(\alpha_{A}+k\right)/2}}{2^{l}}+c\overset{M-2}{\underset{k=\alpha_{A}}{\sum}}\overset{M-1}{\underset{l=k+1}{\sum}}\frac{2^{\alpha_{A}}}{2^{l}}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle c\overset{\alpha_{A}-1}{\underset{k=0}{\sum}}\frac{2^{\left(\alpha_{A}+k\right)/2}}{2^{M}}+c\overset{M-1}{\underset{k=\alpha_{A}}{\sum}}\frac{2^{\alpha_{A}}}{2^{M}}\leq c<\infty.

Let
\alpha_{A}\geq M.
Analogously to
II_{\alpha_{A}}^{1}\left(x\right)
we can prove (
3.10
), for
A=1,...,s.

Now, prove boundedness of
II_{l_{A}}^{2}
. Let
t\in I_{M}
and
x\in I_{i}\backslash I_{i+1},

i\leq l_{A}-1.
Since
x+t\in I_{i}\backslash I_{i+1},
if we apply first equality of Lemma
2.3
we get that

II_{l_{A}}^{2}\left(x\right)=0.

(3.14)

Let
x\in I_{i}\backslash I_{i+1},

l_{A}\leq i\leq m_{A}.
Since
n\geq 2^{M}
and
t\in I_{M},
if we apply first equality of Lemma
2.3
we get that

II_{l_{A}}^{2}\left(x\right)\leq 2^{M}\int_{I_{M}}2^{l_{A}}\sum_{k=l_{A}}^{i}D_{2^{k}}\left(x+t\right)d\mu\left(t\right)\leq c2^{l_{A}+i}.

(3.15)

Let
x\in I_{i}\backslash I_{i+1},

m_{A}<i\leq M-1.
Then
x+t\in I_{i}\backslash I_{i+1},
for any
t\in I_{M}
and by first equality of Lemma
2.3
we have that

II_{l_{A}}^{2}\left(x\right)\leq c2^{M}\int_{I_{M}}2^{l_{A}+m_{A}}\leq c2^{l_{A}+m_{A}}.

(3.16)

Let
0\leq l_{A}\leq m_{A}\leq M.
Then, in the view of (
2.1
) and (
3.14
-
3.16
) we can conclude that

\displaystyle\int_{\overline{I_{M}}}\left|II_{l_{A}}^{2}\left(x\right)\right|^{1/2}d\mu\left(x\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(\sum_{i=0}^{l_{A}-1}+\sum_{i=l_{A}}^{m_{A}}+\sum_{i=m_{A}+1}^{M-1}\right)\int_{I_{i}\backslash I_{i+1}}\left|II_{l_{A}}^{2}\left(x\right)\right|^{1/2}d\mu\left(x\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle c\sum_{i=l_{A}}^{m_{A}}\int_{I_{i}\backslash I_{i+1}}2^{\left(l_{A}+i\right)/2}d\mu\left(x\right)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle c\sum_{i=m_{A}+1}^{M-1}\int_{I_{i}\backslash I_{i+1}}2^{\left(l_{A}+m_{A}\right)/2}d\mu\left(x\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle c\sum_{i=l_{A}}^{m_{A}}2^{\left(l_{A}+i\right)/2}\frac{1}{2^{i}}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle c\sum_{i=m_{A}+1}^{M-1}2^{\left(l_{A}+m_{A}\right)/2}\frac{1}{2^{i}}\leq c<\infty.

Analogously, we can prove same estimations in the cases

0\leq l_{A}\leq M<m_{A}
and
M\leq l_{A}\leq m_{A}.

Now, we prove part b) of Theorem
3.28
. According to (
3.7
), there exists increasing sequence
\left\{\alpha_{k}:\text{ }k\in\mathbb{N}_{+}\right\}\subset\left\{n_{k}\text{ }:k\in\mathbb{N}_{+}\right\}
of natural numbers such that

\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\frac{\Phi^{1/4}\left(\alpha_{k}\right)}{V^{1/2}\left(\alpha_{k}\right)}\leq c<\infty.

(3.17)

Let
f=\left(f_{n},n\in\mathbb{N}_{+}\right)
be martingale form Example
2.6
, where

\lambda_{k}:=\Phi^{1/2}\left(\alpha_{k}\right)/V\left(\alpha_{k}\right).

According to (
3.17
) we get that condition (
2.3
) is fulfilled and it follows that
f=\left(f_{n},n\in\mathbb{N}_{+}\right)
.

By applying (
2.4
) we get that

\widehat{f}(j)

(3.18)

=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}2^{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|}\Phi^{1/2}\left(\alpha_{k}\right)/V\left(\alpha_{k}\right),&\text{\thinspace\thinspace}j\in\left\{2^{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|},...,2^{{}_{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|+1}}-1\right\},\text{ }k\in\mathbb{N}_{+}\\
0\,,&\text{\thinspace}j\notin\bigcup\limits_{k=0}^{\infty}\left\{2^{{}_{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|}},...,2^{{}_{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|+1}}-1\right\}.\end{array}\right.

Let
2^{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|}<j<\alpha_{k}.
If we apply (
2.6
) we get that

S_{j}f=S_{2^{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|}}f+\frac{w_{2^{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|}}D_{j-2^{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|}}\Phi^{1/2}\left(\alpha_{k}\right)}{V\left(\alpha_{k}\right)}

(3.19)

Hence,

\displaystyle\frac{\sigma_{{}_{\alpha_{k}}}f}{\Phi\left(\alpha_{k}\right)}

(3.20)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\Phi\left(\alpha_{k}\right)\alpha_{k}}\sum_{j=1}^{2^{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|}}S_{j}f

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\Phi\left(\alpha_{k}\right)\alpha_{k}}\sum_{j=2^{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|}+1}^{\alpha_{k}}S_{j}f

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\sigma_{{}_{2^{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|}}}f}{\Phi\left(\alpha_{k}\right)\alpha_{k}}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\frac{\left(\alpha_{k}-2^{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|}\right)S_{2^{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|}}f}{\Phi\left(\alpha_{k}\right)\alpha_{k}}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\frac{w_{2^{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|}}2^{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|}\Phi^{1/2}\left(\alpha_{k}\right)}{\Phi\left(\alpha_{k}\right)V\left(\alpha_{k}\right)\alpha_{k}}\sum_{j=2^{{}_{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|}}+1}^{\alpha_{k}}D_{j-2^{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle III_{1}+III_{2}+III_{3}.

For
III_{3}
we can conclude that

\displaystyle\left|III_{3}\right|

(3.21)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{2^{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|}\Phi^{1/2}\left(\alpha_{k}\right)}{\Phi\left(\alpha_{k}\right)V\left(\alpha_{k}\right)\alpha_{k}}\left|\sum_{j=1}^{\alpha_{k}-2^{{}_{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|}}}D_{j}\right|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{2^{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|}\Phi^{1/2}\left(\alpha_{k}\right)}{\Phi\left(\alpha_{k}\right)V\left(\alpha_{k}\right)\alpha_{k}}\left(\alpha_{k}-2^{{}_{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|}}\right)\left|K_{\alpha_{k}-2^{{}_{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|}}}\right|

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\frac{c\left(\alpha_{k}-2^{{}_{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|}}\right)\left|K_{\alpha_{k}-2^{{}_{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|}}}\right|}{\Phi^{1/2}\left(\alpha_{k}\right)V\left(\alpha_{k}\right).}

Let

\alpha_{k}=\sum_{i=1}^{r_{k}}\sum_{k=l_{i}^{k}}^{m_{i}^{k}}2^{k},

where

m_{1}^{k}\geq l_{1}^{k}>l_{1}^{k}-2\geq m_{2}^{k}\geq l_{2}^{k}>l_{2}^{k}-2\geq...\geq m_{s}^{k}\geq l_{s}^{k}\geq 0.

Since (see theorems
2.10
and
3.28
)

\left\|III_{1}\right\|_{1/2}\leq c,\left\|III_{2}\right\|_{1/2}\leq c,

and

\mu\left\{E_{l_{i}^{k}}\right\}\geq 1/2^{l_{i}^{k}-1},

By combining (
3.20
), (
3.21
) and Lemma
3.26
we get that

\displaystyle\int_{G}\left|\sigma_{\alpha_{k}}f(x)/\Phi\left(\alpha_{k}\right)\right|^{1/2}d\mu\left(x\right)

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\left\|III_{3}\right\|_{1/2}^{1/2}-\left\|III_{2}\right\|_{1/2}^{1/2}-\left\|III_{1}\right\|_{1/2}^{1/2}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle c\text{ }\underset{i=2}{\overset{r_{k}-2}{\sum}}\int_{E_{l_{i}^{k}}}\left|2^{2l_{i}^{k}}/\left(\Phi^{1/2}\left(\alpha_{k}\right)V\left(\alpha_{k}\right)\right)\right|^{1/2}d\mu\left(x\right)-2c

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle c\overset{r_{k}-2}{\underset{i=2}{\sum}}1/\left(V^{1/2}\left(\alpha_{k}\right)\Phi^{1/4}\left(\alpha_{k}\right)\right)-2c

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle cr_{k}/\left(V^{1/2}\left(\alpha_{k}\right)\Phi^{1/4}\left(\alpha_{k}\right)\right)

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle cV^{1/2}\left(\alpha_{k}\right)/\Phi^{1/4}\left(\alpha_{k}\right)\rightarrow\infty,\text{ as }k\rightarrow\infty.

Theorem
3.28
is proved.

Theorem 3.29

a) Let
0<p<1/2,

f\in H_{p}(G).
Then there exists an absolute constant
c_{p}
depending only on
p
such that

\text{ }\left\|\sigma_{n}f\right\|_{H_{p}(G)}\leq c_{p}2^{d\left(n\right)\left(1/p-2\right)}\left\|f\right\|_{H_{p}(G)}.

b) Let
0<p<1/2
and
\Phi\left(n\right):\mathbb{N}_{+}\rightarrow[1,\infty)
be non-decreasing function such that

\sup_{k\in\mathbb{N}_{+}}d\left(n_{k}\right)=\infty,\text{ \ \ }\overline{\underset{k\rightarrow\infty}{\lim}}\frac{2^{d\left(n_{k}\right)\left(1/p-2\right)}}{\Phi\left(n_{k}\right)}=\infty.

(3.22)

Then there exist a martingale
f\in H_{p}(G),
such that

\underset{k\in\mathbb{N}_{+}}{\sup}\left\|\frac{\sigma_{n_{k}}f}{\Phi\left(n_{k}\right)}\right\|_{weak-L_{p}(G)}=\infty.

Proof
:
Let
n\in\mathbb{N}.
Analogously to (
3.9
) it is sufficient to prove that

\int\limits_{\overline{I}_{M}}\left(2^{d\left(n\right)\left(2-1/p\right)}\left|\sigma_{n}\left(a\right)\right|\right)^{p}d\mu\leq c_{p}<\infty,

for every
p
-atom
a
, where
I
denotes support of the atom.

Analogously to Theorem
3.28
we may assume that
a

is
p
-atom with support
\ I=I_{M}
,
\mu\left(I_{M}\right)=2^{-M}
and
n>2^{M}.

Since
\left\|a\right\|_{\infty}\leq 2^{M/p}
we can conclude that

\displaystyle 2^{d\left(n\right)\left(2-1/p\right)}\left|\sigma_{n}a\right|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2^{d\left(n\right)\left(2-1/p\right)}\left\|a\right\|_{\infty}\int_{I_{M}}\left|K_{n}\left(x+t\right)\right|d\mu\left(t\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2^{d\left(n\right)\left(2-1/p\right)}2^{M/p}\int_{I_{M}}\left|K_{n}\left(x+t\right)\right|d\mu\left(t\right).

Let
x\in I_{l+1}\left(e_{k}+e_{l}\right),\,0\leq k,l\leq\left[n\right]\leq M.
Then, by applying Lemma
3.23
we get that
K_{n}\left(x+t\right)=0,
where
t\in I_{M}
and hence,

2^{d\left(n\right)\left(2-1/p\right)}\left|\sigma_{n}a\right|=0.

(3.23)

Let
x\in I_{l+1}\left(e_{k}+e_{l}\right),\,\left[n\right]\leq k,l\leq M

or
k\leq\left[n\right]\leq l\leq M.
Then Lemma
3.25
follows that

\displaystyle 2^{d\left(n\right)\left(2-1/p\right)}\left|\sigma_{n}a\right|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2^{d\left(n\right)\left(2-1/p\right)}2^{M\left(1/p-2\right)+k+l}

(3.24)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle c_{p}2^{\left[n\right]\left(1/p-2\right)+k+l}.

By combining (
2.1
), (
3.23
) and (
3.24
) we can conclude that

\displaystyle\int_{\overline{I_{M}}}\left|2^{d\left(n\right)\left(2-1/p\right)}\sigma_{n}a\left(x\right)\right|^{p}d\mu\left(x\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left(\overset{\left[n\right]-2}{\underset{k=0}{\sum}}\overset{\left[n\right]-1}{\underset{l=k+1}{\sum}}+\overset{\left[n\right]-1}{\underset{k=0}{\sum}}\overset{M-1}{\underset{l=\left[n\right]}{\sum}}+\overset{M-2}{\underset{k=\left[n\right]}{\sum}}\overset{M-1}{\underset{l=k+1}{\sum}}\right)\int_{I_{l+1}\left(e_{k}+e_{l}\right)}\left|2^{d\left(n\right)\left(2-1/p\right)}\sigma_{n}a\left(x\right)\right|^{p}d\mu\left(x\right)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\overset{M-1}{\underset{k=0}{\sum}}\int_{I_{M}\left(e_{k}\right)}\left|2^{d\left(n\right)\left(2-1/p\right)}\sigma_{n}a\left(x\right)\right|^{p}d\mu\left(x\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle c_{p}\overset{M-2}{\underset{k=\left[n\right]}{\sum}}\overset{M-1}{\underset{l=k+1}{\sum}}\frac{1}{2^{l}}2^{\left[n\right]\left(2p-1\right)}2^{p\left(k+l\right)}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle c_{p}\overset{\left[n\right]}{\underset{k=0}{\sum}}\overset{M-1}{\underset{l=\left[n\right]+1}{\sum}}\frac{1}{2^{l}}2^{\left[n\right]\left(2p-1\right)}2^{p\left(k+l\right)}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\frac{c_{p}2^{\left[n\right]\left(2p-1\right)}}{2^{M}}\overset{\left[n\right]}{\underset{k=0}{\sum}}2^{p\left(k+M\right)}<c_{p}<\infty.

Now, we prove part b) of Theorem
3.29
. According to (
3.22
) there exists an increasing sequence of natural numbers
\left\{\alpha_{k}:\text{ }k\in\mathbb{N}_{+}\right\}\subset\left\{n_{k}:\text{ }k\in\mathbb{N}_{+}\right\},
such that

\alpha_{0}\geq 3
and

\sum_{\eta=0}^{\infty}u^{-p}\left(\alpha_{\eta}\right)<c_{p}<\infty,\text{ \ \ }\text{\ }u\left(\alpha_{k}\right)=2^{d\left(\alpha_{k}\right)\left(1/p-2\right)/2}/\Phi^{1/2}\left(\alpha_{k}\right).

(3.25)

Let
f
be martingale from Example
2.6
, where

\lambda_{k}=u^{-1}\left(\alpha_{k}\right),

If we apply (
3.25
) we get that (
2.3
) is fulfilled and it follows that
f\in H_{p}(G).
According to (
2.4
) we have that

\widehat{f}(j)=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}2^{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|\left(1/p-1\right)}/u\left(\alpha_{k}\right),&j\in\left\{2^{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|},...,2^{{}_{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|+1}}-1\right\},\text{ }k\in\mathbb{N}_{+},\\
0\,,&j\notin\bigcup\limits_{k=0}^{\infty}\left\{2^{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|},...,2^{{}_{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|+1}}-1\right\}.\end{array}\right.

(3.26)

Let
2^{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|}<j<\alpha_{k}.
Then, analogously to (
3.19
) and (
3.20
), if we apply (
3.26
) we get that

\displaystyle\frac{\sigma_{{}_{\alpha_{k}}}f}{\Phi\left(\alpha_{k}\right)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\sigma_{{}_{2^{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|}}}f}{\Phi\left(\alpha_{k}\right)\alpha_{k}}+\frac{\left(\alpha_{k}-2^{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|}\right)S_{2^{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|}}f}{\Phi\left(\alpha_{k}\right)\alpha_{k}}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\frac{2^{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|\left(1/p-1\right)}}{\Phi\left(\alpha_{k}\right)u\left(\alpha_{k}\right)\alpha_{k}}\sum_{j=2^{{}_{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|}}}^{\alpha_{k}-1}\left(D_{{}_{j}}-D_{2^{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|}}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle IV_{1}+IV_{2}+IV_{3}.

Let
\alpha_{k}\in\mathbb{N}
and
E_{\left[\alpha_{k}\right]}:=I_{{}_{\left[\alpha_{k}\right]+1}}\left(e_{\left[\alpha_{k}\right]-1}+e_{\left[\alpha_{k}\right]}\right).
Since
\left[\alpha_{k}-2^{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|}\right]=\left[\alpha_{k}\right],

analogously to (
3.21
), if we apply Lemma
3.26
for
IV_{3}
we have the following estimation

\displaystyle\left|IV_{3}\right|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{2^{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|\left(1/p-1\right)}}{\Phi\left(\alpha_{k}\right)u\left(\alpha_{k}\right)\alpha_{k}}\left(\alpha_{k}-2^{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|}\right)\left|K_{\alpha_{k}-2^{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|}}\right|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{2^{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|\left(1/p-1\right)}}{\Phi\left(\alpha_{k}\right)u\left(\alpha_{k}\right)\alpha_{k}}\left|2^{\left[\alpha_{k}\right]}K_{\left[\alpha_{k}\right]}\right|

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\frac{2^{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|\left(1/p-2\right)}2^{2\left[\alpha_{k}\right]-4}}{\Phi\left(\alpha_{k}\right)u\left(\alpha_{k}\right)}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle 2^{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|\left(1/p-2\right)/2}2^{2\left[\alpha_{k}\right]-4}/\Phi^{1/2}\left(\alpha_{k}\right).

Hence,

\displaystyle\left\|IV_{3}\right\|_{weak-L_{p}(G)}^{p}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\left(\frac{2^{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|\left(1/p-2\right)/2}2^{2\left[\alpha_{k}\right]-4}}{\Phi^{1/2}\left(\alpha_{k}\right)}\right)^{p}\mu\left\{x\in G:\text{ }\left|IV_{3}\right|\geq\frac{2^{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|\left(1/p-2\right)/2}2^{2\left[\alpha_{k}\right]-4}}{\Phi^{1/2}\left(\alpha_{k}\right)}\right\}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle c_{p}\left(2^{2\left[\alpha_{k}\right]+\left|\alpha_{k}\right|\left(1/p-2\right)/2}/\Phi^{1/2}\left(\alpha_{k}\right)\right)^{p}\mu(E_{\left[\alpha_{k}\right]})

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle c_{p}\left(2^{\left(\left|\alpha_{k}\right|-\left[\alpha_{k}\right]\right)\left(1/p-2\right)}/\Phi\left(\alpha_{k}\right)\right)^{p/2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle c_{p}\left(2^{d\left(\alpha_{k}\right)\left(1/p-2\right)}/\Phi\left(\alpha_{k}\right)\right)^{p/2}\rightarrow\infty,\text{\ as }k\rightarrow\infty.

By combining Corollary
2.13
and first part of Theorem
3.29
we find that

\left\|IV_{1}\right\|_{weak-L_{p}(G)}\leq c_{p}<\infty,\text{ \ \ }\left\|IV_{2}\right\|_{weak-L_{p}(G)}\leq c_{p}<\infty.

On the other hand, for sufficiently large
n
we can conclude that

\displaystyle\left\|\sigma_{\alpha_{k}}f\right\|_{weak-L_{p}(G)}^{p}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\left\|IV_{3}\right\|_{weak-L_{p}(G)}^{p}-\left\|IV_{2}\right\|_{weak-L_{p}(G)}^{p}-\left\|IV_{1}\right\|_{weak-L_{p}(G)}^{p}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\left\|IV_{3}\right\|_{weak-L_{p}(G)}^{p}\rightarrow\infty,\text{ as }k\rightarrow\infty.

Theorem
3.29
is proved.

The proofs of Corollaries
3.30
-
3.32
are similar to the proofs of Corollaries
2.13
-
2.15
. So, we leave out the details of proofs and just present these results:

Corollary 3.30

Let
p>0
and
f\in H_{p}(G)
. Then

\left\|\sigma_{2^{k}}f-f\right\|_{H_{p}(G)}\rightarrow 0,\text{ as }k\rightarrow\infty.

Corollary 3.31

Let
p>0
and
f\in H_{p}(G)
. Then

\left\|\sigma_{2^{k}+2^{k-1}}f-f\right\|_{H_{p}(G)}\rightarrow 0,\text{ as }k\rightarrow\infty.

Corollary 3.32

Let
0<p<1/2
. Then there exists a martingale
f\in H_{p}(G)
, such that

\left\|\sigma_{2^{k}+1}f-f\right\|_{weak-L_{p}(G)}\nrightarrow 0,\text{ as }k\rightarrow\infty.

On the other hand, for any
f\in H_{1/2}(G)
the following is true:

\left\|\sigma_{2^{k}+1}f-f\right\|_{H_{1/2}(G)}\rightarrow 0,\text{ as }k\rightarrow\infty.

3.4
Modulus of continuity and convergence in norm of subsequences of Fejér means with respect to the one-dimensional Walsh-Fourier series on the martingale Hardy spaces

In this section we apply Theorem
3.28
and Theorem
3.29
to find necessary and sufficient conditions for modulus of continuity of martingale
f\in H_{p}
, for which subsequences of Fejér means with respect to the one-dimensional Walsh-Fourier series converge in
H_{p}
-norm.

First, we prove the following result:

Theorem 3.33

a) Let
f\in H_{1/2}(G),\ \ \sup_{k\in\mathbb{N}_{+}}V\left(n_{k}\right)=\infty
and

\omega_{H_{p}(G)}\left(1/2^{\left|n_{k}\right|},f\right)=o\left(1/V^{2}\left(n_{k}\right)\right),\text{ \ as \ }k\rightarrow\infty.

(3.27)

Then

\left\|\sigma_{n_{k}}f-f\right\|_{H_{1/2}(G)}\rightarrow 0,\text{ as }k\rightarrow\infty.

b) Let
\sup_{k\in\mathbb{N}_{+}}V\left(n_{k}\right)=\infty.
Then there exists a martingale
f\in H_{1/2}(G),
such that

\omega_{H_{1/2}(G)}\left(1/2^{\left|n_{k}\right|},f\right)=O\left(1/V^{2}\left(n_{k}\right)\right),\text{ \ as \ }k\rightarrow\infty

(3.28)

and

\left\|\sigma_{n_{k}}f-f\right\|_{H_{1/2}(G)}\nrightarrow 0,\text{ \ as \ }k\rightarrow\infty.

(3.29)

Proof
:
Let
f\in H_{1/2}(G)
and
2^{k}<n\leq 2^{k+1}.
Then

\displaystyle\left\|\sigma_{n}f-f\right\|_{H_{1/2}(G)}^{1/2}

\displaystyle\leq\left\|\sigma_{n}f-\sigma_{n}S_{2^{k}}f\right\|_{H_{1/2}(G)}^{1/2}

\displaystyle+\left\|\sigma_{n}S_{2^{k}}f-S_{2^{k}}f\right\|_{H_{1/2}(G)}^{1/2}

\displaystyle+\left\|S_{2^{k}}f-f\right\|_{H_{1/2}(G)}^{1/2}

\displaystyle=\left\|\sigma_{n}\left(S_{2^{k}}f-f\right)\right\|_{H_{1/2}(G)}^{1/2}

\displaystyle+\left\|S_{2^{k}}f-f\right\|_{H_{1/2}(G)}^{1/2}

\displaystyle+\left\|\sigma_{n}S_{2^{k}}f-S_{2^{k}}f\right\|_{H_{1/2}(G)}^{1/2}

\displaystyle\leq c\left(V\left(n\right)+1\right)\omega_{H_{1/2}(G)}^{1/2}\left(1/2^{k},f\right)

\displaystyle+\left\|\sigma_{n}S_{2^{k}}f-S_{2^{k}}f\right\|_{H_{1/2}(G)}^{1/2}.

It is evident that

\displaystyle\sigma_{n}S_{2^{k}}f-S_{2^{k}}f

\displaystyle=\frac{2^{k}}{n}\left(S_{2^{k}}\sigma_{2^{k}}f-S_{2^{k}}f\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{2^{k}}{n}S_{2^{k}}\left(\sigma_{2^{k}}f-f\right).

Let
p>0.
By combining Corollaries
2.13
and
3.30
we can conclude that

\displaystyle\left\|\sigma_{n}S_{2^{k}}f-S_{2^{k}}f\right\|_{H_{1/2}(G)}^{1/2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2^{k/2}}{n^{1/2}}\left\|S_{2^{k}}\left(\sigma_{2^{k}}f-f\right)\right\|_{H_{1/2}(G)}^{1/2}

\displaystyle\leq\left\|\sigma_{2^{k}}f-f\right\|_{H_{1/2}(G)}^{1/2}\rightarrow 0,\text{\ as \ }k\rightarrow\infty.

Now, we prove part b) of Theorem
3.33
. Since
\sup_{k\in\mathbb{N}_{+}}V(\alpha_{k})=\infty,
then there exists a martingale
\{\alpha_{k}:k\in\mathbb{N}_{+}\}\subset\{n_{k}:k\in\mathbb{N}_{+}\}
such that
V(\alpha_{k})\uparrow\infty,
as
k\rightarrow\infty
and

V^{2}(\alpha_{k})\leq V(\alpha_{k+1}).

(3.30)

Let
f
be martingale from Example
2.6
, where

\lambda_{k}=V^{-2}(\alpha_{k}),

If we apply (
3.30
) we get that condition (
2.3
) is fulfilled and it follows that
f\in H_{p}(G).

By using (
2.4
) we find that

\widehat{f}(j)=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}2^{{}_{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|}}/V^{2}(\alpha_{k}),&\text{\thinspace}j\in\left\{2^{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|},...,2^{{}_{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|+1}}-1\right\},\text{ }k\in\mathbb{N}_{+}\\
0\,,&j\notin\bigcup\limits_{k=0}^{\infty}\left\{2^{{}_{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|}},...,2^{{}_{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|+1}}-1\right\}.\text{ }\end{array}\right.

(3.31)

By combining (
2.7
) and (
3.30
) we can conclude that

\displaystyle w_{H_{1/2}(G)}(1/2^{n},f)=\left\|f-S_{2^{n}}f\right\|_{H_{1/2}(G)}

(3.32)

\displaystyle\leq\sum\limits_{i=n+1}^{\infty}1/V^{2}(\alpha_{i})=O\left(1/V^{2}(\alpha_{n})\right),\text{ \ as \ }n\rightarrow\infty.

Let
2^{{}_{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|}}<j\leq\alpha_{k}.
By using (
2.6
) we get that

S_{j}f=S_{2^{{}_{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|}}}f+\frac{2^{{}_{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|}}w_{2^{{}_{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|}}}D_{j-2^{{}_{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|}}}}{V^{2}(\alpha_{k})}.

Hence,

\displaystyle\sigma_{\alpha_{k}}f-f

(3.33)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{2^{{}_{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|}}}{\alpha_{k}}\left(\sigma_{2^{{}_{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|}}}f-f\right)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\frac{\alpha_{k}-2^{{}_{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|}}}{\alpha_{k}}\left(S_{2^{{}_{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|}}}f-f\right)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\frac{2^{{}_{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|}}w_{2^{{}_{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|}}}\left(\alpha_{k}-2^{{}_{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|}}\right)K_{\alpha_{k}-2^{{}_{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|}}}}{\alpha_{k}V^{2}(\alpha_{k})}.

According to (
1.2
), (
1.12
) and (
3.33
) we have that

\displaystyle\|\sigma_{\alpha_{k}}f-f\|_{1/2}^{1/2}

(3.34)

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\frac{c}{V(\alpha_{k})}\|\left(\alpha_{k}-2^{{}_{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|}}\right)K_{\alpha_{k}-2^{{}_{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|}}}\|_{1/2}^{1/2}

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle\left(\frac{2^{{}_{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|}}}{\alpha_{k}}\right)^{1/2}\|\sigma_{2^{{}_{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|}}}f-f\|_{1/2}^{1/2}

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle\left(\frac{\alpha_{k}-2^{{}_{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|}}}{\alpha_{k}}\right)^{1/2}\|S_{2^{{}_{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|}}}f-f\|_{1/2}^{1/2}.

Let

\alpha_{k}=\sum_{i=1}^{r_{k}}\sum_{k=l_{i}^{k}}^{m_{i}^{k}}2^{k},

where

m_{1}^{k}\geq l_{1}^{k}>l_{1}^{k}-2\geq m_{2}^{k}\geq l_{2}^{k}>l_{2}^{k}-2>...>m_{s}^{k}\geq l_{s}^{k}\geq 0

and

E_{l_{i}^{k}}:=I_{{}_{l_{i}^{k}+1}}\left(e_{l_{i}^{k}-1}+e_{l_{i}^{k}}\right).

By using Lemma
3.26
we get that

\displaystyle\int_{G}\left|\left(\alpha_{k}-2^{{}_{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|}}\right)K_{\alpha_{k}-2^{{}_{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|}}}\left(x\right)\right|^{1/2}d\mu

(3.35)

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{16}\underset{i=2}{\overset{r_{k}-2}{\sum}}\int_{E_{l_{i}^{k}}}\left|\left(\alpha_{k}-2^{{}_{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|}}\right)K_{\alpha_{k}-2^{{}_{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|}}}\left(x\right)\right|^{1/2}d\mu\left(x\right)

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{16}\underset{i=2}{\overset{r_{k}-2}{\sum}}\frac{1}{2^{l_{i}^{k}}}2^{l_{i}^{k}}\geq cr_{k}\geq cV(\alpha_{k}).

By combining estimations (
3.34
-
3.35
), Corollaries
2.13
and
3.30
we get that (
3.29
) holds true and Theorem
3.33
is proved.

Theorem 3.34

a) Let
0<p<1/2,

f\in H_{p}(G)
,
\ \sup_{k\in\mathbb{N}_{+}}d\left(n_{k}\right)=\infty
and

\omega_{H_{p}(G)}\left(1/2^{\left|n_{k}\right|},f\right)=o\left(1/2^{d\left(n_{k}\right)\left(1/p-2\right)}\right),\text{ as \ }k\rightarrow\infty.

(3.36)

Then

\left\|\sigma_{n_{k}}f-f\right\|_{H_{p}(G)}\rightarrow 0,\text{\ as \ }k\rightarrow\infty.

(3.37)

b) Let
\sup_{k\in\mathbb{N}_{+}}d\left(n_{k}\right)=\infty.
Then there exists a martingale
f\in H_{p}(G)

\left(0<p<1/2\right),
such that

\displaystyle\omega_{H_{p}(G)}\left(1/2^{\left|n_{k}\right|},f\right)=O\left(1/2^{d\left(n_{k}\right)\left(1/p-2\right)}\right),\text{ \ as \ }k\rightarrow\infty

(3.38)

and

\left\|\sigma_{n_{k}}f-f\right\|_{weak-L_{p}(G)}\nrightarrow 0,\text{\ as \ }k\rightarrow\infty.

(3.39)

Proof
:

Let
0<p<1/2.
Then under condition (
3.36
) if we repeat steps of the proof of Theorem
3.33
, we immediately get that (
3.37
) holds.

Let prove part b) of Theorem
3.34
. Since
\sup_{k}d\left(n_{k}\right)=\infty,
there exists
\{\alpha_{k}:k\in\mathbb{N}_{+}\}\subset\{n_{k}:k\in\mathbb{N}_{+}\}
such that
\sup_{k\in\mathbb{N}_{+}}d\left(\alpha_{k}\right)=\infty

and

2^{2d\left(\alpha_{k}\right)\left(1/p-2\right)}\leq 2^{d\left(\alpha_{k+1}\right)\left(1/p-2\right)}.

(3.40)

Let
f
be a martingale from Lemma
2.6
, where

\lambda_{k}=2^{-\left(1/p-2\right)d\left(\alpha_{i}\right)}.

If we use (
3.40
) we conclude that condition (
2.3
) is fulfilled and it follows that
f\in H_{p}(G).

According to (
2.4
) we get that

\widehat{f}(j)=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}2^{\left(1/p-2\right)\left[\alpha_{k}\right]},&\text{\thinspace\thinspace}j\in\left\{2^{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|},...,2^{{}_{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|+1}}-1\right\},\text{ }k\in\mathbb{N}_{+}\\
0\,,&\text{\thinspace}j\notin\bigcup\limits_{n=0}^{\infty}\left\{2^{{}_{\left|\alpha_{n}\right|}},...,2^{{}_{\left|\alpha_{n}\right|+1}}-1\right\}.\text{ }\end{array}\right.

(3.41)

By combining (
2.7
) and (
3.40
) we have that

\displaystyle\omega_{H_{p}(G)}(1/2^{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|},f)

(3.42)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sum\limits_{i=k}^{\infty}1/2^{d\left(\alpha_{i}\right)\left(1/p-2\right)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle O\left(1/2^{d\left(\alpha_{k}\right)\left(1/p-2\right)}\right)\text{ \ as \ }k\rightarrow\infty.

Analogously to the proof of previous theorem, if we use also Corollaries
2.13
and
3.30
, for the sufficiently large
k
we can conclude that

\displaystyle\|\sigma_{\alpha_{k}}f-f\|_{weak-L_{p}(G)}^{p}

(3.43)

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle 2^{\left(1-2p\right)\left[\alpha_{k}\right]}\|\left(\alpha_{k}-2^{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|}\right)K_{\alpha_{k}-2^{{}_{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|}}}\|_{weak-L_{p}(G)}^{p}

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle\left(\frac{2^{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|}}{\alpha_{k}}\right)^{p}\|\sigma_{2^{{}_{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|}}}f-f\|_{weak-L_{p}(G)}^{p}

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle\left(\frac{\alpha_{k}-2^{{}_{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|}}}{\alpha_{k}}\right)^{p}\|S_{2^{{}_{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|}}}f-f\|_{weak-L_{p}(G)}^{p}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle 2^{\left(1-2p\right)\left[\alpha_{k}\right]-1}\|\left(\alpha_{k}-2^{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|}\right)K_{\alpha_{k}-2^{{}_{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|}}}\|_{weak-L_{p}(G)}^{p}

Let
x\in E_{\left[\alpha_{k}\right]}.
Lemma
3.26
follows that

\displaystyle\mu\left(x\in G:\left(\alpha_{k}-2^{{}_{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|}}\right)\left|K_{\alpha_{k}-2^{{}_{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|}}}\right|\geq 2^{2\left[\alpha_{k}\right]-4}\right)

\displaystyle\geq\mu\left(E_{\left[\alpha_{k}\right]}\right)\geq 1/2^{\left[\alpha_{k}\right]-4}

and

\displaystyle 2^{2p\left[\alpha_{k}\right]-4}\mu\left(x\in G:\left(\alpha_{k}-2^{{}_{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|}}\right)\left|K_{\alpha_{k}-2^{{}_{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|}}}\right|\geq 2^{2\left[\alpha_{k}\right]-4}\right)

(3.44)

\displaystyle\geq 2^{\left(2p-1\right)\left[\alpha_{k}\right]-4}.

Hence, by combining (
1.2
), (
1.12
), (
3.43
) and (
3.44
) we get that

\left\|\sigma_{n_{k}}f-f\right\|_{weak-L_{p}(G)}\nrightarrow 0,\,\,\,\text{as}\,\,\,k\rightarrow\infty.

The proof of Theorem
3.34
is complete.

By using Theorem
3.34
we easily get an important result which was proved in
[
60
]
:

Corollary 3.35

a) Let
f\in H_{1/2}(G)
and

\omega_{H_{1/2}(G)}\left(1/2^{k},f\right)=o\left(\frac{1}{k^{2}}\right),\text{ as \ }k\rightarrow\infty.

Then

\left\|\sigma_{k}f-f\right\|_{H_{1/2}(G)}\rightarrow 0,\,\,\,\text{ \ as \ }k\rightarrow\infty.

b) There exists a martingale
f\in H_{1/2}(G),
for which

\omega_{H_{1/2}(G)}\left(1/2^{k},f\right)=O\left(\frac{1}{k^{2}}\right),\text{ \ as \ }k\rightarrow\infty

and

\left\|\sigma_{k}f-f\right\|_{1/2}\nrightarrow 0,\,\,\,\text{ \ as \ }k\rightarrow\infty.

Corollary 3.36

a) Let
0<p<1/2,

f\in H_{p}(G)
and

\omega_{H_{p}(G)}\left(1/2^{k},f\right)=o\left(1/2^{k(1/p-2)}\right),\text{ as \ }k\rightarrow\infty.

Then

\left\|\sigma_{k}f-f\right\|_{H_{p}(G)}\rightarrow 0,\,\,\,\text{as\thinspace\thinspace\thinspace}k\rightarrow\infty.

b) Then there exists a martingale
f\in H_{p}(G)

\left(0<p<1/2\right),
for which

\omega_{H_{p}(G)}\left(1/2^{k},f\right)=O\left(1/2^{k\left(1/p-2\right)}\right),\text{ \ as \ }k\rightarrow\infty

and

\left\|\sigma_{k}f-f\right\|_{weak-L_{p}(G)}\nrightarrow 0,\,\,\,\text{ \ as \ }k\rightarrow\infty.

3.5
Strong convergence of Fejér means with respect to the one-dimensional Walsh-Fourier series on the martingale Hardy spaces

In this section we consider strong convergence results of Fejér means with respect to the one-dimensional Walsh-Fourier series in the martingale Hardy spaces, when
0<p\leq 1/2
(for details see
[
59
]
).

The following is true:

Theorem 3.37

a) Let
0<p\leq 1/2
and
f\in H_{p}(G)
. Then there exists a constant
c_{p},
depending only on
p
, such that

\frac{1}{\log^{\left[1/2+p\right]}n}\overset{n}{\underset{m=1}{\sum}}\frac{\left\|\sigma_{m}f\right\|_{H_{p}(G)}^{p}}{m^{2-2p}}\leq c_{p}\left\|f\right\|_{H_{p}(G)}^{p}.

b) Let
0<p<1/2,

\Phi:\mathbb{N}_{+}\rightarrow[1,\infty)
be non-decreasing function, such that
\Phi\left(n\right)\uparrow\infty
and

\overline{\underset{k\rightarrow\infty}{\lim}}\frac{k^{2-2p}}{\Phi\left({k}\right)}=\infty.

Then there exists a martingale
f\in H_{p}(G),
such that

\underset{m=1}{\overset{\infty}{\sum}}\frac{\left\|\sigma_{m}f\right\|_{weak-L_{p}(G)}^{p}}{\Phi\left(m\right)}=\infty.

Proof
:
Suppose that

\frac{1}{\log^{\left[1/2+p\right]}n}\overset{n}{\underset{m=1}{\sum}}\frac{\left\|\sigma_{m}f\right\|_{p}^{p}}{m^{2-2p}}\leq c_{p}\left\|f\right\|_{H_{p}(G)}^{p}.

By combining (
1.7
), (
1.15
) and Lemma
3.27
we can conclude that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\log^{\left[1/2+p\right]}n}\overset{n}{\underset{m=1}{\sum}}\frac{\left\|\sigma_{m}f\right\|_{H_{p}(G)}^{p}}{m^{2-2p}}

(3.45)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{\log^{\left[1/2+p\right]}n}\overset{n}{\underset{m=1}{\sum}}\frac{\left\|\sigma_{m}f\right\|_{p}^{p}}{m^{2-2p}}+\|\widetilde{\sigma}_{\#}^{\ast}f\|_{H_{p}(G)}+\|\widetilde{S}_{\#}^{\ast}f\|_{H_{p}(G)}

\displaystyle\leq c_{p}\left\|f\right\|_{H_{p}(G)}^{p}.

According to Lemma
2.7
and (
3.45
) Theorem
3.37
will be proved if we show that

\frac{1}{\log^{\left[1/2+p\right]}n}\overset{n}{\underset{m=1}{\sum}}\frac{\left\|\sigma_{m}a\right\|_{p}^{p}}{m^{2-2p}}\leq c<\infty,\text{ \ \ \ }m=2,3,...

for any
p
-atom
a
. We may assume that
a
is
p
-atom, with support
I
,
\mu\left(I\right)=2^{-M}
and
I=I_{M}.
It is evident that
\sigma_{n}\left(a\right)=0,
when
n\leq 2^{M}.
Therefore, we may assume that
n>2^{M}.

Let
x\in I_{M}.
Since
\sigma_{n}
is bounded from
L_{\infty}(G)
to
L_{\infty}(G)
(The boundedness follows fact that Fejér kernels are uniformly bounded in the space
L_{1}(G)
, which is proved in Lemma
3.22
) and
\left\|a\right\|_{\infty}\leq 2^{M/p}
we can conclude that

\int_{I_{M}}\left|\sigma_{m}a\left(x\right)\right|^{p}d\mu\left(x\right)\leq\left\|\sigma_{m}a\right\|_{\infty}^{p}/2^{M}

\leq\left\|a\right\|_{\infty}^{p}/2^{M}\leq c<\infty,\text{ }0<p\leq 1/2.

Let
0<p\leq 1/2.
Then

\frac{1}{\log^{\left[1/2+p\right]}n}\overset{n}{\underset{m=1}{\sum}}\frac{\int_{I_{M}}\left|\sigma_{m}a\left(x\right)\right|^{p}d\mu\left(x\right)}{m^{2-2p}}

\leq\frac{c}{\log^{\left[1/2+p\right]}n}\overset{n}{\underset{m=1}{\sum}}\frac{1}{m^{2-2p}}\leq c<\infty.

It is evident that

\left|\sigma_{m}a\left(x\right)\right|\leq\int_{I_{M}}\left|a\left(t\right)\right|\left|K_{m}\left(x+t\right)\right|d\mu\left(t\right)

\leq 2^{M/p}\int_{I_{M}}\left|K_{m}\left(x+t\right)\right|d\mu\left(t\right).

Lemma
3.23
follows that

\left|\sigma_{m}a\left(x\right)\right|\leq\frac{c2^{k+l}2^{M\left(1/p-1\right)}}{m},\text{ \ \ }x\in I_{l+1}\left(e_{k}+e_{l}\right),\,0\leq k<l<M

(3.46)

and

\left|\sigma_{m}a\left(x\right)\right|\leq c2^{M\left(1/p-1\right)}2^{k},\text{ \ }x\in I_{M}\left(e_{k}\right),\,0\leq k<M.

(3.47)

If we use identity (
2.1
) and (
3.46
-
3.47
) we get that

\displaystyle\int_{\overline{I_{M}}}\left|\sigma_{m}a\left(x\right)\right|^{p}d\mu\left(x\right)

(3.48)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\overset{M-2}{\underset{k=0}{\sum}}\overset{M-1}{\underset{l=k+1}{\sum}}\int_{I_{l+1}\left(e_{k}+e_{l}\right)}\left|\sigma_{m}a\left(x\right)\right|^{p}d\mu\left(x\right)

\displaystyle+\overset{M-1}{\underset{k=0}{\sum}}\int_{I_{M}\left(e_{k}\right)}\left|\sigma_{m}a\left(x\right)\right|^{p}d\mu\left(x\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle c\overset{M-2}{\underset{k=0}{\sum}}\overset{M-1}{\underset{l=k+1}{\sum}}\frac{1}{2^{l}}\frac{2^{p\left(k+l\right)}2^{M\left(1-p\right)}}{m^{p}}+c\overset{M-1}{\underset{k=0}{\sum}}\frac{1}{2^{M}}2^{M\left(1-p\right)}2^{pk}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{c2^{M\left(1-p\right)}}{m^{p}}\overset{M-2}{\underset{k=0}{\sum}}\overset{M-1}{\underset{l=k+1}{\sum}}\frac{2^{p\left(k+l\right)}}{2^{l}}+c\overset{M-1}{\underset{k=0}{\sum}}\frac{2^{pk}}{2^{pM}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{c2^{M\left(1-p\right)}M^{\left[1/2+p\right]}}{m^{p}}+c.

Hence,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\log^{\left[1/2+p\right]}n}\overset{n}{\underset{m=2^{M}+1}{\sum}}\frac{\int_{\overline{I_{M}}}\left|\sigma_{m}a\left(x\right)\right|^{p}d\mu\left(x\right)}{m^{2-2p}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\log^{\left[1/2+p\right]}n}\left(\overset{n}{\underset{m=2^{M}+1}{\sum}}\frac{c2^{M\left(1-p\right)}M^{\left[1/2+p\right]}}{m^{2-p}}+\overset{n}{\underset{m=2^{M}+1}{\sum}}\frac{c}{m^{2-2p}}\right)<c<\infty.

The proof of part a) of theorem
3.37
is complete.

Now, we prove part b) of Theorem
3.37
. Let
\Phi\left(n\right)
non-decreasing function satisfying the condition

\underset{k\rightarrow\infty}{\lim}\frac{2^{\left(\left|n_{k}\right|+1\right)\left(2-2p\right)}}{\Phi\left(2^{\left|n_{k}\right|+1}\right)}=\infty.

(3.49)

According to (
3.49
), there exists an increasing sequence
\left\{\alpha_{k}:\text{ }k\in\mathbb{N}_{+}\right\}\subset\left\{n_{k}:\text{ }k\in\mathbb{N}_{+}\right\}
such that

\left|\alpha_{k}\right|\geq 2,\text{ \ \ \ where \ }k\in\mathbb{N}_{+}

(3.50)

and

\displaystyle\sum_{\eta=0}^{\infty}\frac{\Phi^{1/2}\left(2^{\left|\alpha_{\eta}\right|+1}\right)}{2^{\left|\alpha_{\eta}\right|\left(1-p\right)}}

(3.51)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2^{1-p}\sum_{\eta=0}^{\infty}\frac{\Phi^{1/2}\left(2^{\left|\alpha_{\eta}\right|+1}\right)}{2^{\left(\left|\alpha_{\eta}\right|+1\right)\left(1-p\right)}}<c<\infty.

Let
f=\left(f_{n},\text{ }n\in\mathbb{N}_{+}\right)\in H_{p}(G)
be a martingale from the Example
2.6
, where

\lambda_{k}=\frac{\Phi^{1/2p}\left(2^{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|+1}\right)}{2^{\left(\left|\alpha_{k}\right|\right)\left(1/p-1\right)}}

By combining (
2.3
) and (
3.51
) we get that
f\in H_{p}(G).

According to (
2.4
) we have that

\displaystyle\widehat{f}(j)=\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}\Phi^{1/2p}\left(2^{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|+1}\right)\text{ \ if \ }j\in\left\{2^{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|},...,2^{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|+1}-1\right\},\text{ }k\in\mathbb{N}_{+},\\
0\text{ },\text{ \ if \ }j\notin\bigcup\limits_{k=0}^{\infty}\left\{2^{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|},...,2^{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|+1}-1\right\}.\text{ }\end{array}\right.

Let
2^{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|}<n<2^{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|+1}.
Then

\sigma_{{}_{n}}f=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{j=1}^{2^{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|}}S_{j}f+\frac{1}{n}\sum_{j=2^{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|}+1}^{n}S_{j}f=III+IV.

(3.55)

It is evident that

S_{j}f=0,\,\ \text{ if \thinspace\thinspace}0\leq j\leq 2^{\left|\alpha_{1}\right|}

(3.56)

Let
2^{\left|\alpha_{s}\right|}<j\leq 2^{\left|\alpha_{s}\right|+1},
where
s=1,2,...,k.
If we apply (
2.6
) we get that

\displaystyle S_{j}f

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{\eta=0}^{s-1}\Phi^{1/2p}\left(2^{\left|\alpha_{\eta}\right|+1}\right)\left(D_{2^{\left|\alpha_{\eta}\right|+1}}-D_{2^{\left|\alpha_{\eta}\right|}}\right)

(3.57)

\displaystyle+\Phi^{1/2p}\left(2^{\left|\alpha_{s}\right|+1}\right)w_{2^{\left|\alpha_{s}\right|}}D_{j-2^{\left|\alpha_{s}\right|}}.

Let
2^{\left|\alpha_{s}\right|+1}\leq j\leq 2^{\left|\alpha_{s+1}\right|},

s=0,1,...k-1.
Then if we use (
2.5
) we can conclude that

S_{j}f=\sum_{\eta=0}^{s}\Phi^{1/2p}\left(2^{\left|\alpha_{\eta}\right|+1}\right)\left(D_{2^{\left|\alpha_{\eta}\right|+1}}-D_{2^{\left|\alpha_{\eta}\right|}}\right).

(3.58)

Let
x\in I_{2}\left(e_{0}+e_{1}\right).
Since (see Lemmas
2.3
and
3.23
)

D_{2^{n}}\left(x\right)=K_{2^{n}}\left(x\right)=0,\text{ where }n\geq 2

(3.59)

by combining (
3.50
) and (
3.56
-
3.59
) we get that

III=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{\eta=0}^{k-1}\Phi^{1/2p}\left(2^{\left|\alpha_{\eta}\right|+1}\right)\sum_{v=2^{\left|\alpha_{\eta}\right|}+1}^{2^{\left|\alpha_{\eta}\right|+1}}D_{v}\left(x\right)

(3.60)

=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{\eta=0}^{k-1}\Phi^{1/2p}\left(2^{\left|\alpha_{\eta}\right|+1}\right)\left(2^{\left|\alpha_{\eta}\right|+1}K_{2^{\left|\alpha_{\eta}\right|+1}}\left(x\right)-2^{\left|\alpha_{\eta}\right|}K_{2^{\left|\alpha_{\eta}\right|}}\left(x\right)\right)=0.

If we use (
3.57
) when
s=k
for
IV
we can write that

\displaystyle IV

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{n-2^{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|}}{n}\sum_{\eta=0}^{k-1}\Phi^{1/2p}\left(2^{\left|\alpha_{\eta}\right|+1}\right)\left(D_{2^{\left|\alpha_{\eta}\right|+1}}-D_{2^{\left|\alpha_{\eta}\right|}}\right)

(3.61)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\frac{\Phi^{1/2p}\left(2^{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|+1}\right)}{n}\sum_{j=2^{{}_{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|}}+1}^{n}w_{2^{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|}}D_{j-2^{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle IV_{1}+IV_{2}.

By combining (
3.50
) and (
3.59
) we can conclude that

IV_{1}=0,\text{ \ where \ }x\in I_{2}\left(e_{0}+e_{1}\right).

(3.62)

Let
\alpha_{k}\in\mathbb{A}_{0,2},

2^{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|}<n<2^{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|+1}
and
x\in I_{2}\left(e_{0}+e_{1}\right)
. Since
n-2^{{}_{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|}}\in\mathbb{A}_{0,2},
Lemmas
2.2
and
3.22
and (
3.59
) follows that

\displaystyle\left|IV_{2}\right|=\frac{\Phi^{1/2p}\left(2^{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|+1}\right)}{n}\left|\sum_{j=1}^{n-2^{\left|{}_{\alpha_{k}}\right|}}D_{{}_{j}}\left(x\right)\right|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\Phi^{1/2p}\left(2^{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|+1}\right)}{n}\left|\left(n-2^{{}_{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|}}\right)K_{n-2^{{}_{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|}}}\left(x\right)\right|

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\frac{\Phi^{1/2p}\left(2^{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|+1}\right)}{2^{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|+1}}.

Let
0<p<1/2
and
n\in\mathbb{A}_{0,2}.
By combining (
3.55
-
3.5
) we get that

\displaystyle\left\|\sigma_{n}f\right\|_{weak-L_{p}(G)}^{p}

(3.63)

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\frac{c_{p}\Phi^{1/2}\left(2^{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|+1}\right)}{2^{p\left(\left|\alpha_{k}\right|+1\right)}}\mu\left\{x\in I_{2}\left(e_{0}+e_{1}\right):\text{ }\left|\sigma_{n}f\right|\geq\frac{c_{p}\Phi^{1/2p}\left(2^{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|+1}\right)}{2^{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|+1}}\right\}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\frac{c_{p}\Phi^{1/2}\left(2^{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|+1}\right)}{2^{p\left(\left|\alpha_{k}\right|+1\right)}}\mu\left\{I_{2}\left(e_{0}+e_{1}\right)\right\}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\frac{c_{p}\Phi^{1/2}\left(2^{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|+1}\right)}{2^{p\left(\left|\alpha_{k}\right|+1\right)}}.

Hence,

\displaystyle\underset{n=1}{\overset{\infty}{\sum}}\frac{\left\|\sigma_{n}f\right\|_{weak-L_{p}(G)}^{p}}{\Phi\left(n\right)}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\underset{\left\{n\in\mathbb{A}_{0,2}:\text{ }2^{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|}<n<2^{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|+1}\right\}}{\sum}\frac{\left\|\sigma_{n}f\right\|_{weak-L_{p}(G)}^{p}}{\Phi\left(n\right)}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\Phi^{1/2}\left(2^{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|+1}\right)}\underset{\left\{n\in\mathbb{A}_{0,2}:\text{ }2^{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|}<n<2^{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|+1}\right\}}{\sum}\frac{1}{2^{p\left(\left|\alpha_{k}\right|+1\right)}}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\frac{c_{p}2^{\left(1-p\right)\left(\left|\alpha_{k}\right|+1\right)}}{\Phi^{1/2}\left(2^{\left|\alpha_{k}\right|+1}\right)}\rightarrow\infty,\text{ \qquad as \ \ }k\rightarrow\infty.

The proof of Theorem
3.37
is complete.

Theorem 3.38

Let
f\in H_{1/2}(G).
Then

\underset{n\in\mathbf{\mathbb{N}}_{+}}{\sup}\underset{\left\|f\right\|_{H_{p}}\leq 1}{\sup}\frac{1}{n}\underset{m=1}{\overset{n}{\sum}}\left\|\sigma_{m}f\right\|_{1/2}^{1/2}=\infty.

Proof
:
Let
0<p\leq 1
and

f_{k}({x}):=2^{k}\left(D_{2^{k+1}}(x)-D_{2^{k}}(x)\right)

Since

\text{supp}(f_{k})=I_{k},\text{ \ \ }\int_{I_{k}}a_{k}d\mu=0

and

\left\|f_{k}\right\|_{\infty}\leq 2^{2k}=(\text{supp}f_{k})^{-2},

we conclude that
f_{k}
is
1/2
-atom, for every
k\in\mathbb{N}

Moreover, if we use orthogonality of Walsh functions we get that

S_{2^{n}}\left(f_{k},x)\right)

=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}0,&n=0,...,k,\\
\left(D_{2^{k+1}}(x)-D_{2^{k}}(x)\right),&n\geq k+1,\end{array}\right.

and

\displaystyle\sup\limits_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\left|S_{2^{n}}\left(f_{k},x\right)\right|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left|\left(D_{2^{k+1}}(x)-D_{2^{k}}(x)\right)\right|,

where
{x}\in G
.

By combining first equality of Lemma
2.2
and Lemma
2.8
we obtain that

\displaystyle\left\|a_{k}\right\|_{H_{p}(G)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{2^{k}}\left\|\sup\limits_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\left|S_{2^{n}}\left(D_{2^{k+1}}(x)-D_{2^{k}}(x)\right)\right|\right\|_{1/2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{2^{k}}\left\|\left(D_{2^{k+1}}(x)-D_{2^{k}}(x)\right)\right\|_{1/2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{2^{k}}\left\|D_{2^{k}}(x)\right\|_{1/2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle{2^{k}}\cdot 2^{-k}\leq 1.

It is easy to easy to show that

\widehat{f}_{m}\left(i\right)=\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}\text{ }2^{m},\text{ if }i=2^{m},...,2^{m+1}-1,\\
\text{ }0,\text{otherwise}\end{array}\right.

(3.64)

and

S_{i}f_{m}=\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}2^{m}\left(D_{i}-D_{2^{m}}\right),\text{ \ if }i=2^{m}+1,...,2^{m+1}-1,\\
\text{ }f_{m},\text{ \ if }i\geq 2^{m+1},\\
0,\text{ \ otherwise.}\end{array}\right.

(3.65)

Let
0<n<2^{m}.
By using first equality of Lemma
2.2
we have that

\displaystyle\left|\sigma_{n+2^{m}}f_{m}\right|

(3.66)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n+2^{m}}\left|\overset{n+2^{m}}{\underset{j=2^{m}+1}{\sum}}S_{j}f_{m}\right|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n+2^{m}}\left|2^{m}\overset{n+2^{m}}{\underset{j=2^{m}+1}{\sum}}\left(D_{j}-D_{2^{m}}\right)\right|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n+2^{m}}\left|2^{m}\overset{n}{\underset{j=1}{\sum}}\left(D_{j+2^{m}}-D_{2^{m}}\right)\right|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n+2^{m}}\left|2^{m}\overset{n}{\underset{j=1}{\sum}}D_{j}\right|=\frac{2^{m}}{n+2^{m}}n\left|K_{n}\right|.

Let

n=\sum_{i=1}^{s}\sum_{k=l_{i}}^{m_{i}}2^{k},

where

0\leq l_{1}\leq m_{1}\leq l_{2}-2<l_{2}\leq m_{2}\leq...\leq l_{s}-2<l_{s}\leq m_{s}.

By applying Lemma
3.26
and (
3.66
) we find that

\left|\sigma_{n+2^{m}}f_{m}\left(x\right)\right|\geq c2^{2l_{i}},\text{ \ \ \ where \ \ \ }x\in I_{l_{i}+1}\left(e_{l_{i}-1}+e_{l_{i}}\right).

Hence,

\displaystyle\int_{G}\left|\sigma_{n+2^{m}}f_{m}(x)\right|^{1/2}d\mu\left(x\right)

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\text{ }\underset{i=0}{\overset{s}{\sum}}\int_{I_{l_{i}+1}\left(e_{l_{i}-1}+e_{l_{i}}\right)}\left|\sigma_{n+2^{m}}f_{m}(x)\right|^{1/2}d\mu\left(x\right)

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle c\overset{s}{\underset{i=0}{\sum}}\frac{1}{2^{l_{i}}}2^{l_{i}}\geq cs\geq cV\left(n\right).

According to the second estimation of Lemma
2.4
we can conclude that

\displaystyle\underset{n\in\mathbf{\mathbb{N}}_{+}}{\sup}\underset{\left\|f\right\|_{H_{p}}\leq 1}{\sup}\frac{1}{n}\underset{k=1}{\overset{n}{\sum}}\left\|\sigma_{k}f\right\|_{1/2}^{1/2}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2^{m+1}}\overset{2^{m+1}-1}{\underset{k=2^{m}+1}{\sum}}\left\|\sigma_{k}f_{m}\right\|_{1/2}^{1/2}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\frac{c}{2^{m+1}}\underset{k=2^{m}+1}{\overset{2^{m+1}-1}{\sum}}V\left(k-2^{m}\right)

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\frac{c}{2^{m+1}}\underset{k=1}{\overset{2^{m}-1}{\sum}}V\left(k\right)\geq c\log m\rightarrow\infty,\text{ \ as \ }m\rightarrow\infty.

The proof is complete.

4
Convergence and summability of partial sums with respect to the two-dimensional Walsh-Fourier series on the martingale Hardy spaces

4.1
Basic notations

Let denote by
\overrightarrow{x}
the two-dimensional vector
\overrightarrow{x}:=\left(x^{1},x^{2}\right)
and by
G^{2}
the direct product of two Walsh groups. Let
I_{n}^{2}:=I_{n}\left(0\right)\times I_{n}\left(0\right)
for any
n\in\mathbb{N}
and
\overline{I_{n}^{2}}:=G^{2}\backslash I_{n}^{2}
.

The norms (or quasi-norms) of the spaces of
L_{p}(G^{2})
space is defined by

\left\|f\right\|_{p}:=\left(\int_{G^{2}}\left|f(\overrightarrow{x})\right|^{p}d\mu(\overrightarrow{x})\right)^{1/p}\qquad\left(0<p<\infty\right).

The space
weak-L_{p}(G^{2})
consists of all functions
f
for which

\left\|f\right\|_{weak-L_{p}(G^{2})}:=\underset{\lambda>0}{\sup}\lambda\mu\left(\overrightarrow{x}\in{G^{2}}:|f|>\lambda\right)^{1/p}<+\infty.

Two-dimensional Walsh system is defined by

w_{n_{1},n_{2}}\left(x^{1},x^{2}\right):=w_{n_{1}}\left(x^{1}\right)w_{n_{2}}\left(x^{2}\right).

The two-dimensional Walsh system is orthonormal and complete
L_{2}\left({G}^{2}\right)
(see

[
47
]
).

For
f\in L_{1}\left(G^{2}\right)
the following number

\displaystyle\widehat{f}\left(n_{1},n_{2}\right):=\int\limits_{G^{2}}f({\overrightarrow{x}})w_{n_{1},n_{2}}({\overrightarrow{x}})d\mu({\overrightarrow{x}})

is called
\left(n_{1},n_{2}\right)
-th Fourier coeficients of function
f
.

\left({n_{1},n_{2}}\right)
-th rectangular partial sum
S_{n_{1},n_{2}}
of function
f
is defined by:

S_{n_{1},n_{2}}(f;{\overrightarrow{x}}):=\sum\limits_{i_{1}=0}^{n_{1}-1}\sum\limits_{i_{2}=0}^{n_{2}-1}\widehat{f}\left(i_{1},i_{2}\right)w_{i_{1},i_{2}}\left({\overrightarrow{x}}\right).

n
-th Marcinkiewicz (Marcinkiewicz-Fejér) means of the two-dimensional Walsh-Fourier series of function
f
is defined by

\mathscr{M}_{n}(f;{\overrightarrow{x}}):=\frac{1}{n}\sum\limits_{k=0}^{n}S_{k,...,k}(f;{\overrightarrow{x}}).

Dirichlet and Marcinkiewicz kernels of the two-dimensional Walsh-Fourier series are defined by

D_{n_{1},n_{2}}\left({\overrightarrow{x}}\right)=D_{n_{1}}\left(x^{1}\right)D_{n_{2}}\left(x^{2}\right).

and

K_{n}({\overrightarrow{x}}):=\frac{1}{n}\sum\limits_{k=0}^{n}D_{k,k}({\overrightarrow{x}}).

For the partial sums of the two-dimensional Walsh-Fourier let as define

S_{M}^{\left(1\right)}f\left(x^{1},x^{2}\right):=\int\limits_{G}f\left(s,x^{2}\right)D_{M}\left(x^{1}+s\right)d\mu\left(s\right)

and

S_{N}^{\left(2\right)}f\left(x^{1},x^{2}\right):=\int\limits_{G}f\left(x^{1},t\right)D_{N}\left(x^{2}+t\right)d\mu\left(t\right).

For the partial sums of the two-dimensional Walsh-Fourier let define the following maximal operators
S_{\#}^{\ast}
and
\widetilde{S}_{\#}^{\ast}
by

S_{\#}^{\ast}\left(f;x^{1},x^{2}\right):=\sup_{n\in\mathbb{N_{+}}}\left|S_{n,n}\left(f;x^{1},x^{2}\right)\right|

and

\widetilde{S}_{\#}^{\ast}\left(f;x^{1},x^{2}\right):=\sup_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\left|S_{2^{n},2^{n}}\left(f;x_{1},x_{2}\right)\right|.

We define the maximal operator
\mathscr{M}^{\ast}
and restricted maximal operator
\widetilde{\mathscr{M}}_{\#}^{\ast}
of Marcinkiewicz means by

\mathscr{M}^{\ast}\left(f;x^{1},x^{2}\right):=\sup_{n\in\mathbb{N_{+}}}\left|\mathscr{M}_{n}\left(f;x_{1},x_{2}\right)\right|

and

\widetilde{\mathscr{M}}_{\#}^{\ast}\left(f;x^{1},x^{2}\right):=\sup_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\left|\mathscr{M}_{2^{n}}\left(f;x_{1},x_{2}\right)\right|.

For the partial sums of the two-dimensional Walsh-Fourier let define the following weighted maximal operators

\widetilde{\mathscr{M}}^{\ast}(f;x_{1},x_{2}):=\sup_{n\in\mathbb{N_{+}}}\frac{\left|\mathscr{M}_{n}(f;x_{1},x_{2})\right|}{\log^{3/2}\left(n+1\right)}

and

\widetilde{\mathscr{M}}^{\ast,p}\left(f;x_{1},x_{2}\right)=\sup_{n\in\mathbb{N_{+}}}\left|\frac{\mathscr{M}_{n}(f;x_{1},x_{2})}{(n+1)^{2/p-3}}\right|.\quad

The
\sigma
-algebra generated by the two-dimensional cubes
