I_{n}^{2}\left(\overrightarrow{x}\right):=I_{n}\left(x^{1}\right)\times I_{n}\left(x^{2}\right),

is defined by
\digamma_{n}\left(n\in\mathbb{N}\right)
.

The conditional expectation operator with respect to

\digamma_{n}\left(n\in\mathbb{N}\right)

is denoted by
E_{n}
and in our concrete case we have the following explicit expression for it:

\displaystyle E_{n}f(\overrightarrow{x})=S_{2^{n},2^{n}}f\left(\overrightarrow{x}\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{k_{1}=0}^{2^{n}-1}\sum_{k_{2}=0}^{2^{n}-1}\widehat{f}\left(k_{1},k_{2}\right)w_{k_{1},k_{2}}(\overrightarrow{x})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\left|I_{n}^{2}\left(\overrightarrow{x}\right)\right|}\int_{I_{n}^{2}\left(\overrightarrow{x}\right)}f(\overrightarrow{x})d\mu(\overrightarrow{x}),

where
\left|I_{n}^{2}\left(\overrightarrow{x}\right)\right|=2^{-2n}
denotes measure of cube
I_{n}^{2}\left(\overrightarrow{x}\right)
.

Sequence
f=\left(f_{n},\text{ }n\in\mathbb{N}\right)
of functions
f_{n}\in L_{1}\left(G^{2}\right)
is called dyadic martingales if (for details see
[
47
]
)

\left(i\right)

f_{n}
is measurable with respect to
\sigma-
algebra
\digamma_{n}
, for any
n\in\mathbb{N}
,

\left(ii\right)

E_{n}f_{m}=f_{n}
for every
n\leq m
.

The maximal function of martingale
f
is defined by

f^{\ast}=\sup_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\left|f_{n}\right|.

If
f\in L_{1}\left(G^{2}\right),
then it is weell-known that the maximal operator is defined by

f^{\ast}\left(\overrightarrow{x}\right)=\sup\limits_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\frac{1}{\mu\left(I_{n}^{2}\left(\overrightarrow{x}\right)\right)}\left|\int\limits_{I_{n}^{2}\left(\overrightarrow{x}\right)}f\left(\overrightarrow{u}\right)d\mu\left(\overrightarrow{u}\right)\right|.

For
0<p<\infty
the one-parameter martingale Hardy space
H_{p}\left(G^{2}\right)
consist of all martingales for which

\left\|f\right\|_{H_{p}\left(G^{2}\right)}:=\left\|f^{\ast}\right\|_{p}<\infty.

Next, we define
p
-atoms, which are very important to characterize martingale Hardy spaces.

A function
a
is called a
p
-atom, if there exists an interval
I^{2}
, such that

\int_{I^{2}}ad\mu=0,\\
\qquad\left\|a\right\|_{\infty}\leq\mu\left(I^{2}\right)^{-1/p},\\
\qquad\text{supp}\left(a\right)\subset I^{2}.

It is easy to check that for every martingale
f=(f_{n},n\in\mathbb{N})
and for every
({k_{1},k_{2})}\in{\mathbb{N}}^{2}
the limit

\widehat{f}\left({k_{1},k_{2}}\right):=\lim_{{{n_{1},n_{2}}}\rightarrow\infty}\int_{{G}^{2}}f_{{{n_{1},n_{2}}}}\left({\overrightarrow{x}}\right)w_{{k_{1},k_{2}}}\left({\overrightarrow{x}}\right)d\mu\left({\overrightarrow{x}}\right)

exists and it is called
\left({k_{1},k_{2}}\right)
-th Walsh-Fourier coefficients of
f
.

If
f\in L_{1}\left(G^{2}\right)
and
(E_{n}f:n\in\mathbb{N})
is regular martingale, then

\displaystyle\widehat{f}\left({k_{1},k_{2}}\right)=\int_{{G^{2}}}f\left({{\overrightarrow{x}}}\right)w_{{k_{1},k_{2}}}({\overrightarrow{x}})d\mu\left({{\overrightarrow{x}}}\right)=\widehat{f}\left({k_{1},k_{2}}\right),\text{ }{k_{1},k_{2}}\in\mathbb{N}.

For the two-dimensional case modulus of continuity in
H_{p}(G^{2})
spaces can be defined as

\omega_{H_{p}\left(G^{2}\right)}\left(\frac{1}{2^{n}},f\right):=\left\|f-S_{2^{n},2^{n}}f\right\|_{H_{p}\left(G^{2}\right)}.

It is necessary to describe how can be understood difference
f-S_{2^{n},2^{n}}f
where
f
is martingale and
S_{2^{n},2^{n}}f
is function. The following is true:

Remark 4.39

Let
0<p\leq 1.
Since

S_{2^{n},2^{n}}f=f^{\left(n\right)}\in L_{1}(G^{2}),\text{ \ where }f=\left(f^{\left(n\right)}:n\in\mathbb{N}\right)\in H_{p}(G^{2})

and

\displaystyle\left(S_{2^{k},2^{k}}f^{\left(n\right)}:k\in\mathbb{N}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(S_{2^{k},2^{k}}S_{2^{n},2^{n}},k\in\mathbb{N}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(S_{2^{0},2^{0}}f,\ldots,S_{2^{n-1},2^{n-1}}f,S_{2^{n},2^{n}}f,S_{2^{n},2^{n}}f,\ldots\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(f^{\left(0\right)},\ldots,f^{\left(n-1\right)},f^{\left(n\right)},f^{\left(n\right)},\ldots\right)

we obtain that
f-S_{2^{n},2^{n}}f
is martingale for which

\left(f-S_{2^{n},2^{n}}f\right)^{\left(k\right)}=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}0,&k=0,.\ldots,n,\\
f^{\left(k\right)}-f^{\left(n\right)},&k\geq n+1,\end{array}\right.

and norm

\left\|f-S_{2^{n},2^{n}}f\right\|_{H_{p}\left(G^{2}\right)}

can be understood as
H_{p}(G^{2})
norm of martingale

f-S_{2^{n},2^{n}}f=(\left(f-S_{2^{n},2^{n}}f\right)^{\left(k\right)},\ \ k\in\mathbb{N})

4.2
Auxillary lemmas

In the following lemmas we investigate estimations of Marcinkiewicz means of the two-dimensional Walsh-Fourier series (see Lemma
4.40
-Lemma
4.43
).

Glukhov
[
19
]
proved that the following is true:

Lemma 4.40

There exists an absolute constant
c
, such that

\sup_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\int_{G^{2}}|K_{n}(x^{1},x^{2})|d\mu(x^{1},x^{2})\leq c.

The following lemma is proved in
[
25
]
:

Lemma 4.41

Let
n\geq 2^{N}
,
(x^{1},x^{2})\in(I_{l^{1}}\backslash I_{l^{1}+1})\times(I_{m^{2}}\backslash I_{m^{2}+1})
and
0\leq l^{1}\leq m^{2}<N
. Then

\displaystyle\int_{I_{N}\times I_{N}}|K_{n}(x^{1}+t^{1},x^{2}+t^{2})|d\mu(t^{1},t^{2})

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{c}{n2^{2N}}\left\{2^{l^{1}-m^{2}}\sum_{r^{1}=l^{1}+1}^{m^{2}+1}2^{r^{1}}D_{2^{m^{2}+1}}(x^{1}+e_{l^{1}}+e_{r^{1}})\sum_{s=m^{2}+1}^{N}D_{2^{s}}(x^{2}+e_{m^{2}}+x_{m^{2}+1,s-1}^{1})\right.

\displaystyle\left.+2^{l^{1}+m^{2}}\sum_{s=l^{1}}^{m^{2}}\sum_{r^{1}=l^{1}+1}^{s}D_{2^{s}}(x^{1}+e_{l^{1}}+e_{r^{1}})\right\},

where

x_{i,j}:=\sum_{s=i}^{j}x_{s}e_{s},\text{ \ \ }(x_{i,i-1}=0).

For our further investigation we need the following lemma (for details see
[
25
]
):

Lemma 4.42

Let
(x^{1},x^{2})\in I_{N}\times(I_{m^{2}}\backslash I_{m^{2}+1})
and
0\leq m^{2}<N.
Then

\int_{I_{N}\times I_{N}}|K_{n}(x^{1}+t^{1},x^{2}+t^{2})|d\mu(t^{1},t^{2})

\leq c\frac{2^{m^{2}}}{n2^{N}}\sum_{s=m^{2}}^{N-1}D_{2^{s}}(x^{2}+e_{m^{2}}),\text{ \ when \ }n>2^{N}.

We also need the following lemma proved by Goginava
[
20
]
:

Lemma 4.43

Let

x^{1}\in I_{4A}\left(0,...,0,x_{4m}^{1}=1,0,...,0,x_{4l}^{1}=1,x_{4l+1}^{1},...,x_{4A-1}^{1}\right)

and

x^{2}\in I_{4A}\left(0,...,0,x_{4l}^{2}=1,x_{4l+1}^{1}...,x_{4q-1}^{1},1-x_{4q}^{1},x_{4q+1}^{2},...,x_{4A-1}^{2}\right).

Then

n_{A-1}\left|K_{n_{A-1}}\left(x^{1},x^{2}\right)\right|\geq 2^{4q+4l+4m-3},

where

n_{A}=2^{4A}+2^{4A-4}+...+2^{4}+2^{0}.

Hardy martingale spaces
H_{p}\left(G^{2}\right)
have atomic decomposition for
0<p\leq 1
. The following is true (for details see
[
50
]
,
[
78
]
and
[
79
]
):

Lemma 4.44

A martingale
f=\left(f_{n},\text{ }n\in\mathbb{N}\right)
belongs to
H_{p}(G^{2})\left(0<p\leq 1\right)
if and only if there exist a sequence of p-atoms

\left(a_{k},\text{ }k\in\mathbb{N}\right)
and sequence of real numbers

\left(\mu_{k},\text{ }k\in\mathbb{N}\right)
such that for every
n\in\mathbb{N}

\qquad\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\mu_{k}S_{2^{n},2^{n}}a_{k}=f_{n}

(4.1)

and

\qquad\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\left|\mu_{k}\right|^{p}<\infty.

Moreover,

\left\|f\right\|_{H_{p}(G^{2})}\backsim\inf\left(\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\left|\mu_{k}\right|^{p}\right)^{1/p},

where infimum is taken over all decomposition of
f
of the form (
4.1
).

Lemma 4.45

Let
0<p\leq 1
and
T
be
\sigma
-sublinear operator, such that

\int\limits_{G^{2}}\left|Ta(\overrightarrow{x})\right|^{p}d\mu(\overrightarrow{x})\leq c_{p}<\infty,

for any
p
-atom
a
. Then there exists an absolute constant
c_{p},
such that

\left\|Tf\right\|_{p}\leq c_{p}\left\|f\right\|_{H_{p}(G)}.

(4.2)

In addition, if
T
is bounded from
L_{\infty}(G^{2})
to
L_{\infty}(G^{2})
, then we only have to prove that

\int\limits_{\overset{-}{I^{2}}}\left|Ta\left({\overrightarrow{x}}\right)\right|^{p}d\mu\left({\overrightarrow{x}}\right)\leq c_{p}<\infty,

for every
p
-atom
a
, where
I^{2}
denotes support of
a
and
\overline{I^{2}}:=G^{2}\backslash I^{2}
.

In special cases there exists simpler ways how to calculate
H_{p}(G^{2})
-norm
of martingale
f\in H_{p}(G^{2})
(For details see e.g.
[
50
]
,
[
78
]
and
[
79
]
):

Lemma 4.46

Let
g\in L_{1}\left(G^{2}\right)
and
f:=(E_{n}g:n\in\mathbb{N})
be regular martingale. Then
H_{p}\left(G^{2}\right)\text{ }(0<p\leq 1)
norm is calculated by

\left\|f\right\|_{H_{p}(G^{2})}=\left\|\sup\limits_{n\in\mathbb{N}}|S_{2^{n},2^{n}}g|\right\|_{p}.

Proofs of Lemma
4.47
and Lemma
4.48
are proved in
[
35
]
,
[
36
]
,
[
66
]
.

Lemma 4.47

Let
0<p\leq 1
,
2^{k}\leq n<2^{k+1}
and
S_{n,n}f
be
(n,n)
-th partial sum, where
f\in H_{p}(G^{2})
. Then for any fixed
n\in\mathbb{N},
we have the following estimation:

\displaystyle\left\|S_{n,n}f\right\|_{H_{p}(G^{2})}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left\|\sup_{0\leq l\leq k}\left|S_{2^{l},2^{l}}f\right|\right\|_{p}+\left\|S_{n,n}f\right\|_{p}

\displaystyle\leq\left\|\widetilde{S}_{\#}^{\ast}f\right\|_{p}+\left\|S_{n,n}f\right\|_{p}.

Proof
:
Let consider the following martingale

f_{\#}:=\left(S_{2^{k},2^{k}}S_{n,n}f,\text{ }k\in\mathbb{N}_{+}\right)=\left(S_{2^{0},2^{0}},S_{2^{k},2^{k}}f,...,\ \ \ S_{n,n}f,...,S_{n,n}f,...\right).

By using Lemma
4.46
we immediately get that

\displaystyle\left\|S_{n,n}f\right\|_{H_{p}(G^{2})}^{p}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left\|\sup_{0\leq l\leq k}\left|S_{2^{l},2^{l}}f\right|\right\|_{p}^{p}+\left\|S_{n,n}f\right\|_{p}^{p}

\displaystyle\leq\left\|\widetilde{S}_{\#}^{\ast}f\right\|_{p}^{p}+\left\|S_{n,n}f\right\|_{p}^{p}.

The proof is complete.

Lemma 4.48

Let
0<p\leq 1
,
2^{k}\leq n<2^{k+1}
and
\mathscr{M}_{n}f
be
n
-th Marcinkiewicz means, where
f\in H_{p}(G^{2})
. Then for any fixed
n\in\mathbb{N},
we get that

\displaystyle\left\|\mathscr{M}_{n}f\right\|_{H_{p}(G^{2})}^{p}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left\|\sup_{0\leq l\leq k}\left|\mathscr{M}_{2^{l}}f\right|\right\|_{p}^{p}+\left\|\sup_{0\leq l\leq k}\left|S_{2^{l}}f\right|\right\|_{p}^{p}+\left\|\mathscr{M}_{n}f\right\|_{p}^{p}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left\|\widetilde{\mathscr{M}}_{\#}^{\ast}f\right\|_{p}^{p}+\left\|\widetilde{S}_{\#}^{\ast}f\right\|_{p}^{p}+\left\|\mathscr{M}_{n}f\right\|_{p}^{p}.

Proof
:
Let consider the following martingale

f_{\#}=\left(S_{2^{k}}\mathscr{M}_{n}f,\text{ }k\in\mathbb{N}\right)

=\left(\frac{2^{0}\mathscr{M}_{2^{0}}}{n}+\frac{(n-2^{0})S_{2^{0}}f}{n},...,\frac{2^{k}\mathscr{M}_{2^{k}}f}{n}+\frac{(n-2^{k})S_{2^{k}}f}{n},\mathscr{M}_{n}f,...,\mathscr{M}_{n}f,...\right).

According to Lemma
4.46
we immediately get

\displaystyle\left\|\mathscr{M}_{n}f\right\|_{H_{p}(G^{2})}^{p}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left\|\sup_{0\leq l\leq k}\left|\mathscr{M}_{2^{l}}f\right|\right\|_{p}^{p}+\left\|\sup_{0\leq l\leq k}\left|S_{2^{l},2^{l}}f\right|\right\|_{p}^{p}+\left\|S_{n,n}f\right\|_{p}^{p}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left\|\widetilde{\mathscr{M}}_{\#}^{\ast}f\right\|_{p}^{p}+\left\|\widetilde{S}_{\#}^{\ast}f\right\|_{p}^{p}+\left\|\mathscr{M}_{n}f\right\|_{p}^{p}.

The proof is complete.

4.3
Strong convergence of partial sums with respect to the two-dimensional Walsh-Fourier series on the martingale Hardy spaces

In this section we investigate strong convergence of partial sums with respect to the two-dimensional Walsh-Fourier series on the martingale Hardy spaces when
0<p\leq 1
(see
[
66
]
).
The following theorem is true:

Theorem 4.49

a) Let
0<p<1
and
f\in H_{p}(G^{2})
. Then there exists an absolute constant
c_{p},
depending only on
p,
such that

\sum\limits_{n=1}^{\infty}\frac{\left\|S_{n,n}f\right\|_{H_{p}(G^{2})}^{p}}{n^{3-2p}}\leq c_{p}\left\|f\right\|_{H_{p}(G^{2})}^{p}.

b) Let
0<p<1
and
\Phi:\mathbb{N}\rightarrow[1,

\infty)
be non-decreasing function, satisfying the condition

\underset{n\rightarrow\infty}{\lim}\Phi\left(n\right)=+\infty.

(4.3)

Then there exists a martingale
f\in H_{p}(G^{2})
, such that

\underset{n=1}{\overset{\infty}{\sum}}\frac{\left\|S_{n,n}f\right\|_{weak-L_{p}(G^{2})}^{p}\Phi\left(n\right)}{n^{3-2p}}=\infty.

Proof
:
Suppose that

\sum\limits_{n=1}^{\infty}\frac{\left\|S_{n,n}f\right\|_{p}^{p}}{n^{3-2p}}\leq c_{p}\left\|f\right\|_{H_{p}(G^{2})}^{p}.

By combining Lemma
2.9
and inequality (
1.18
) we can conclude that

\displaystyle{\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}}\frac{\left\|S_{n,n}f\right\|_{H_{p}(G^{2})}^{p}}{n^{3-2p}}\leq{\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}}\frac{\left\|S_{n,n}f\right\|_{p}^{p}}{n^{3-2p}}+\left\|\widetilde{S}_{\#}^{\ast}f\right\|_{p}^{p}\leq\left\|f\right\|_{H_{p}(G^{2})}^{p}.

According to Lemma
4.45
we only have to prove that

\sum\limits_{n=1}^{\infty}\frac{\left\|S_{n,n}a\right\|_{p}^{p}}{n^{3-2p}}\leq c_{p}<\infty,

(4.4)

for avery
p
-atom
a
.

Let
a
be
p
-atom with support
I_{N}\left(z^{1}\right)\times I_{N}\left(z^{2}\right)
, where
\mu\left(I_{N}\right)=\mu\left(I_{N}\right)=2^{-N}
. Without loss the generality we may assume that
z^{1}=z^{2}=0.

Let
\left(x^{1},x^{2}\right)\in\overline{I}_{N}\times\overline{I}_{N}
. Then

D_{2^{i}}\left(x^{1}+t^{1}\right)1_{I_{N}}\left(t^{1}\right)=0,\text{ \ when \ }i\geq N

and

D_{2^{i}}\left(x^{2}+t^{2}\right)1_{I_{N}}\left(t^{2}\right)=0,\text{ \ when \ }i\geq N.

If we apply
w_{2^{j}}\left(x^{i}+t^{i}\right)=w_{2^{j}}\left(x^{i}\right),
where
t^{i}\in I_{N},\ \ i=1\vee 2
and
j<N
according to both equality of Lemma
2.3
we get that

\displaystyle S_{n,n}a\left(x^{1},x^{2}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int\limits_{G\times G}a\left(t^{1},t^{2}\right)D_{n}\left(x^{1}+t^{1}\right)D_{n}\left(x^{2}+t^{2}\right)d\mu\left(t^{1},t^{2}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int\limits_{I_{N}\times I_{N}}a\left(t^{1},t^{2}\right)D_{n}\left(x^{1}+t^{1}\right)D_{n}\left(x^{2}+t^{2}\right)d\mu\left(t^{1},t^{2}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int\limits_{I_{N}\times I_{N}}a\left(t^{1},t^{2}\right)w_{n}\left(x^{1}+t^{1}+x^{2}+t^{2}\right)\sum\limits_{i=0}^{N-1}n_{i}w_{2^{i}}\left(x^{1}+t^{1}\right)D_{2^{i}}\left(x^{1}+t^{1}\right)

\displaystyle\times\sum\limits_{j=0}^{N-1}n_{j}w_{2^{j}}\left(x^{2}+t^{2}\right)D_{2^{j}}\left(x^{2}+t^{2}\right)d\mu\left(t^{1},t^{2}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle w_{n}\left(x^{1}\right)\sum\limits_{i=0}^{N-1}n_{i}w_{2^{i}}\left(x^{2}\right)D_{2^{i}}\left(x^{1}\right)w_{n}\left(x^{2}\right)\sum\limits_{j=0}^{N-1}n_{j}w_{2^{j}}\left(x^{2}\right)D_{2^{j}}\left(x^{2}\right)

\displaystyle\times\int\limits_{I_{N}\times I_{N}}a\left(t^{1},t^{2}\right)w_{n}\left(t^{1}+t^{2}\right)d\mu\left(t^{1},t^{2}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle w_{n}\left(x^{1}+x^{2}\right)\sum\limits_{i=0}^{N-1}n_{i}w_{2^{i}}\left(x^{1}\right)D_{2^{i}}\left(x^{1}\right)\sum\limits_{j=0}^{N-1}n_{j}w_{2^{j}}\left(x^{2}\right)D_{2^{j}}\left(x^{2}\right)

\displaystyle\times\int\limits_{I_{N}}\left(\int\limits_{I_{N}}a\left(t^{2}+\tau,t^{2}\right)d\mu\left(t^{2}\right)\right)w_{n}\left(\tau\right)d\mu\left(\tau\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle w_{n}\left(x^{1}+x^{2}\right)\sum\limits_{i=0}^{N-1}n_{i}w_{2^{i}}\left(x^{1}\right)D_{2^{i}}\left(x^{1}\right)\sum\limits_{j=0}^{N-1}n_{j}w_{2^{j}}\left(x^{2}\right)D_{2^{j}}\left(x^{2}\right)\int\limits_{I_{N}}\Phi\left(\tau\right)w_{n}\left(\tau\right)d\mu\left(\tau\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle w_{n}\left(x^{1}+x^{2}\right)\sum\limits_{i=0}^{N-1}n_{i}w_{2^{i}}\left(x^{1}\right)D_{2^{i}}\left(x^{1}\right)\sum\limits_{j=0}^{N-1}n_{j}w_{2^{j}}\left(x^{2}\right)D_{2^{j}}\left(x^{2}\right)\widehat{\Phi}\left(n\right),

where

\Phi\left(\tau\right)=\int\limits_{I_{N}}a\left(t^{i}+\tau,t^{i}\right)d\mu\left(t^{i}\right)\text{ \ and \ }i=1\vee 2.

Let
x\in I_{s}\backslash I_{s+1},
where
i=1\vee 2.
By using again Lemma
2.3
can conclude that

\overset{N-1}{\underset{i=0}{\sum}}D_{2^{i}}\left(x\right)\leq c2^{s}.

By using (
2.1
) we have that

\displaystyle\int_{\overline{I}_{N}}\left(\overset{N-1}{\underset{i=0}{\sum}}D_{2^{i}}\left(x\right)\right)^{p}d\mu\left(x\right)

(4.5)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle c_{p}\underset{s=0}{\overset{N-1}{\sum}}\int_{I_{s}\backslash I_{s+1}}2^{ps}d\mu\left(x\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle c_{p}\underset{s=0}{\overset{\infty}{\sum}}2^{\left(p-1\right)s}

\displaystyle<

\displaystyle c_{p}<\infty,\text{ \qquad}0<p<1.

From (
4.5
) we get that

\displaystyle\sum\limits_{n=1}^{\infty}\frac{1}{n^{3-2p}}\int\limits_{\overline{I}_{N}\times\overline{I}_{N}}\left|S_{n,n}a\left(x^{1},x^{2}\right)\right|^{p}d\mu\left(x^{1},x^{2}\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sum\limits_{n=1}^{\infty}\frac{\left|\widehat{\Phi}\left(n\right)\right|^{p}}{n^{3-2p}}\int\limits_{\overline{I}_{N}}\left(\sum\limits_{i=0}^{N-1}D_{2^{i}}\left(x^{1}\right)\right)^{p}d\mu\left(x^{1}\right)\int\limits_{\overline{I}_{N}}\left(\sum\limits_{i=0}^{N-1}D_{2^{i}}\left(x^{2}\right)\right)^{p}d\mu\left(x^{2}\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle c_{p}\sum\limits_{n=1}^{\infty}\frac{\left|\widehat{\Phi}\left(n\right)\right|^{p}}{n^{3-2p}}.

Let
n<2^{N}
. Since
w_{n}\left(\tau\right)=1,
where
\tau\in I_{N}
we obtain that

\displaystyle\widehat{\Phi}\left(n\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int\limits_{I_{N}}\Phi\left(\tau\right)w_{n}\left(\tau\right)d\mu\left(\tau\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int\limits_{I_{N}}\left(\int\limits_{I_{N}}a\left(t^{2}+\tau,t^{2}\right)d\mu\left(t^{2}\right)\right)w_{n}\left(\tau\right)d\mu\left(\tau\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int\limits_{I_{N}\times I_{N}}a\left(t^{1},t^{2}\right)d\mu\left(t^{1},t^{2}\right)=0.

So, we may assume that
n\geq 2^{N}.
If we apply Holder’s inequality we get that

\displaystyle\sum\limits_{n=1}^{\infty}\frac{\left|\widehat{\Phi}\left(n\right)\right|^{p}}{n^{3-2p}}

(4.6)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left(\sum\limits_{n=2^{N}}^{\infty}\left|\widehat{\Phi}\left(n\right)\right|^{2}\right)^{p/2}\left(\sum\limits_{n=2^{N}}^{\infty}\frac{1}{n^{\left(3-2p\right)\cdot\left(2/\left(2-p\right)\right)}}\right)^{\left(2-p\right)/2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left(\frac{1}{2^{N\left(2\left(3-2p\right)/\left(2-p\right)-1\right)}}\right)^{\left(2-p\right)/2}\left(\int\limits_{G}\left|\Phi\left(\tau\right)\right|^{2}d\mu\left(\tau\right)\right)^{p/2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{c_{p}}{2^{N\left(4-3p\right)/2}}\left(\int\limits_{I_{N}}\left|\int\limits_{I_{N}}a\left(t^{2}+\tau,t^{2}\right)d\mu\left(t\right)\right|^{2}d\mu\left(\tau\right)\right)^{p/2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{c_{p}}{2^{N\left(4-3p\right)/2}}\left\|a\right\|_{\infty}^{p}\frac{1}{2^{Np/2}}\frac{1}{2^{Np}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{c_{p}}{2^{N\left(4-3p\right)/2}}2^{2N}\frac{1}{2^{3pN/2}}<c_{p}<\infty.

Let
\left(x^{1},x^{2}\right)\in\overline{I}_{N}\times I_{N}
. Then

\displaystyle S_{n,n}a\left(x^{1},x^{2}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle w_{n}\left(x^{1}\right)\sum\limits_{j=0}^{N-1}n_{j}w_{2^{j}}\left(x^{1}\right)D_{2^{j}}\left(x^{1}\right)

\displaystyle\times\int\limits_{G\times G}a\left(t^{1},t^{2}\right)w_{n}\left(t^{1}\right)D_{n}\left(x^{2}+t^{2}\right)d\mu\left(t^{1},t^{2}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle w_{n}\left(x^{1}\right)\sum\limits_{j=0}^{N-1}n_{j}w_{2^{j}}\left(x^{1}\right)D_{2^{j}}\left(x^{1}\right)\int\limits_{G}S_{n}^{\left(2\right)}a\left(t^{1},x^{2}\right)w_{n}\left(t^{1}\right)d\mu\left(t^{1}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle w_{n}\left(x^{1}\right)\sum\limits_{j=0}^{N-1}n_{j}w_{2^{j}}\left(x^{1}\right)D_{2^{j}}\left(x^{1}\right)\widehat{S}_{n}^{\left(2\right)}a\left(n,x^{2}\right).

By applying (
4.5
) we get that

\displaystyle\sum\limits_{n=1}^{\infty}\frac{1}{n^{3-2p}}\int\limits_{\overline{I}_{N}\times I_{N}}\left|S_{n,n}a\left(x^{1},x^{2}\right)\right|^{p}d\mu\left(x^{1},x^{2}\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sum\limits_{n=1}^{\infty}\frac{1}{n^{3-2p}}\int\limits_{\overline{I}_{N}\times I_{N}}\left(\sum\limits_{j=0}^{N-1}D_{2^{j}}\left(x^{1}\right)\left|\widehat{S}_{n}^{\left(2\right)}a\left(n,x^{2}\right)\right|\right)^{p}d\mu\left(x^{1},x^{2}\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sum\limits_{n=1}^{\infty}\frac{1}{n^{3-2p}}\int_{\overline{I}_{N}}\left(\overset{N-1}{\underset{i=0}{\sum}}D_{2^{i}}\left(x^{1}\right)\right)^{p}d\mu\left(x^{1}\right)\cdot\int\limits_{I_{N}}\left|\widehat{S}_{n}^{\left(2\right)}a\left(n,x^{2}\right)\right|^{p}d\mu\left(x^{2}\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sum\limits_{n=1}^{\infty}\frac{1}{n^{3-2p}}\int\limits_{I_{N}}\left|\widehat{S}_{n}^{\left(2\right)}a\left(n,x^{2}\right)\right|^{p}d\mu\left(x^{2}\right).

Let
n<2^{N}
. By applyng definition of
p
-atom we get that

\displaystyle\widehat{S}_{n}^{\left(2\right)}a\left(n,x^{2}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int\limits_{G}\left(\int\limits_{G}a\left(t^{1},t^{2}\right)D_{n}\left(x^{2}+t^{2}\right)d\mu\left(t^{2}\right)\right)w_{n}\left(t^{1}\right)d\mu\left(t^{1}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle D_{n}\left(x^{2}\right)\int\limits_{I_{N}\times I_{N}}a\left(t^{1},t^{2}\right)d\mu\left(t^{1},t^{2}\right)=0.

Therefore, we can suppose that
n\geq 2^{N}
. If follows that

\displaystyle\sum\limits_{n=1}^{\infty}\frac{1}{n^{3-2p}}\int\limits_{\overline{I}_{N}\times I_{N}}\left|S_{n,n}a\left(x^{1},x^{2}\right)\right|^{p}d\mu\left(x^{1},x^{2}\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sum\limits_{n=2^{N}}^{\infty}\frac{1}{n^{3-2p}}\int\limits_{I_{N}}\left|\widehat{S}_{n}^{\left(2\right)}a\left(n,x^{2}\right)\right|^{p}d\mu\left(x^{2}\right)

Since

\left\|S_{n}^{\left(2\right)}a\left(n,x^{2}\right)\right\|_{2}\leq c\left\|a\right\|_{2}

if we use Holder’s inequality we can conclude that

\displaystyle\int\limits_{I_{N}}\left|\widehat{S}_{n}^{\left(2\right)}a\left(n,x^{2}\right)\right|^{p}d\mu\left(x^{2}\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{c_{p}}{2^{N\left(1-p\right)}}\left(\int\limits_{I_{N}}\left|\widehat{S}_{n}^{\left(2\right)}a\left(n,x^{2}\right)\right|d\mu\left(x^{2}\right)\right)^{p}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{c_{p}}{2^{N\left(1-p\right)}}\left(\int\limits_{I_{N}}\left|\int\limits_{I_{N}}S_{n}^{\left(2\right)}a\left(t^{1},x^{2}\right)w_{n}\left(t^{1}\right)d\mu\left(t^{1}\right)\right|d\mu\left(x^{2}\right)\right)^{p}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{c_{p}}{2^{N\left(1-p\right)}}\left(\int\limits_{I_{N}}\left|\int\limits_{I_{N}}\left(\int\limits_{I_{N}}a\left(t^{1},t^{2}\right)D_{n}\left(x^{2}+t^{2}\right)d\mu\left(t^{2}\right)\right)w_{n}\left(t^{1}\right)d\mu\left(t^{1}\right)\right|d\mu\left(x^{2}\right)\right)^{p}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{c_{p}}{2^{N\left(1-p\right)}}\left(\int\limits_{I_{N}}\left(\int\limits_{I_{N}}\left|\int\limits_{I_{N}}a\left(t^{1},t^{2}\right)D_{n}\left(x^{2}+t^{2}\right)d\mu\left(t^{2}\right)\right|d\mu\left(x^{2}\right)\right)d\mu\left(t^{1}\right)\right)^{p}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{c_{p}}{2^{N\left(1-p\right)}}\left(\frac{1}{2^{N/2}}\int\limits_{I_{N}}\left(\int\limits_{I_{N}}\left|\int\limits_{I_{N}}a\left(t^{1},t^{2}\right)D_{n}\left(x^{2}+t^{2}\right)d\mu\left(t^{2}\right)\right|^{2}d\mu\left(x^{2}\right)\right)^{1/2}d\mu\left(t^{1}\right)\right)^{p}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{c_{p}}{2^{N\left(1-p\right)}}\left(\frac{1}{2^{N/2}}\int\limits_{I_{N}}\left(\int\limits_{I_{N}}\left|a\left(t^{1},t^{2}\right)\right|^{2}d\mu\left(t^{2}\right)\right)^{1/2}d\mu\left(t^{1}\right)\right)^{p}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{c_{p}}{2^{N\left(1-p\right)}}\left(\frac{\left\|a\right\|_{\infty}}{2^{N/2}}\frac{1}{2^{N}}\frac{1}{2^{N/2}}\right)^{p}\leq\frac{c_{p}}{2^{N\left(1-p\right)}}\left(\frac{2^{2N/p}}{2^{2N}}\right)^{p}\leq c_{p}2^{N\left(1-p\right)}.

Hence,

\displaystyle\sum\limits_{n=1}^{\infty}\frac{1}{n^{3-2p}}\int\limits_{\overline{I}_{N}\times I_{N}}{\left|S_{n,n}a\left(x^{1},x^{2}\right)\right|}^{p}d\mu\left(x^{1},x^{2}\right)

(4.7)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle c_{p}\sum\limits_{n=2^{N}}^{\infty}\frac{1}{n^{3-2p}}2^{N\left(1-p\right)}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{c_{p}}{2^{N\left(1-p\right)}}\leq c_{p}<\infty.

Analogously, we can prove that

\sum\limits_{n=1}^{\infty}\frac{1}{n^{3-2p}}\int\limits_{I_{N}\times\overline{I}_{N}}\left|S_{n,n}a\left(x^{1},x^{2}\right)\right|^{p}d\mu\left(x^{1},x^{2}\right)\leq c_{p}<\infty.

(4.8)

Let
\left(x^{1},x^{2}\right)\in I_{N}\times I_{N}
. Then by the definition of
p
-atom we get that

\displaystyle\int\limits_{I_{N}\times I_{N}}\left|S_{n,n}a\left(x^{1},x^{2}\right)\right|^{p}d\mu\left(x^{1},x^{2}\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2^{N\left(2-p\right)}}\left(\int\limits_{I_{N}\times I_{N}}\left|S_{n,n}a\left(x^{1},x^{2}\right)\right|^{2}d\mu\left(x^{1},x^{2}\right)\right)^{p/2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2^{N\left(2-p\right)}}\left(\int\limits_{I_{N}\times I_{N}}\left|a\left(x^{1},x^{2}\right)\right|^{2}d\mu\left(x^{1},x^{2}\right)\right)^{p/2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{\left\|a\right\|_{\infty}^{p}}{2^{N\left(2-p\right)}}\frac{1}{2^{Np}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle c_{p}\frac{1}{2^{N\left(2-p\right)}}2^{2N}\frac{1}{2^{Np}}\leq c_{p}<\infty.

It follows that

\displaystyle\sum\limits_{n=1}^{\infty}\frac{1}{n^{3-2p}}\int\limits_{I_{N}\times I_{N}}\left|S_{n,n}a\left(x^{1},x^{2}\right)\right|d\mu\left(x^{1},x^{2}\right)

(4.9)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle c_{p}\sum\limits_{n=1}^{\infty}\frac{1}{n^{3-2p}}\leq c_{p}<\infty.

By combining (
4.4
-
4.9
) we get that Theorem
4.49
is proved.

Let
0<p<1
and
\Phi\left(n\right)
satisfies condition
4.3
. Then there exists increasing sequence of natural numbers
\left\{\alpha_{k}:\text{ }k\in\mathbb{N}_{+}\right\}
such that

\alpha_{0}\geq 2

and

\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}{\Phi^{-p/4}\left(2^{2\alpha_{k}}\right)}<\infty.

(4.10)

Let
f=\left(f_{n},\text{ }n\in\mathbb{N}_{+}\right)
be a martingale

f_{n}(x^{1},x^{2})={\sum_{k=1}^{n}}\lambda_{k}{a_{k}(x^{1},x^{2})}

where

a_{k}(x^{1},x^{2})=2^{2\alpha_{k}(1/p-1)}\left(D_{2^{2\alpha_{k}+1}}(x^{1})-D_{2\alpha_{k}}(x^{1})\right)\left(D_{2^{2\alpha_{k}+1}}(x^{2})-D_{2^{2\alpha_{k}}}(x^{2})\right)

and

\lambda_{k}={\Phi^{-1/4}\left(2^{2\alpha_{k}}\right)}.

By applying (
4.10
) and Lemma
4.44
we obtain that
f\in H_{p}(G^{2}).

It is evident that

\widehat{f}(i,j)=\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}\frac{2^{2\alpha_{k}\left(2/p-2\right)}}{\Phi^{1/4}\left(2^{2\alpha_{k}}\right)},\\
\text{ თუ }\left(i,j\right)\in\left\{2^{2\alpha_{k}},...,\text{ ~}2^{2\alpha_{k}+1}-1\right\}^{2},k\in\mathbb{N}_{+},\\
0,\text{ \thinspace}\\
\text{\thinspace\thinspace თუ \thinspace}\left(i,j\right)\notin\bigcup\limits_{k=1}^{\infty}\left\{2^{2\alpha_{k}},...,\text{ ~}2^{2\alpha_{k}+1}-1\right\}^{2}.\end{array}\right.

(4.11)

Let
2^{2\alpha_{k}}<n<2^{2\alpha_{k}+1}
. By combining (
4.11
) and first equality of Lemma
2.2
we get that

\displaystyle S_{n,n}f\left(x^{1},x^{2}\right)

(4.12)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{i=0}^{2^{\alpha_{k-1}+1}-1}\sum_{j=0}^{2^{\alpha_{k-1}+1}-1}\widehat{f}(i,j)w_{i}\left(x^{1}\right)w_{j}\left(x^{2}\right)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\sum_{i=2^{\alpha_{k}}}^{n-1}\sum_{j=2^{\alpha_{k}}}^{n-1}\widehat{f}(i,j)w_{i}\left(x^{1}\right)w_{j}\left(x^{2}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{\eta=0}^{k-1}\sum_{i=2^{\alpha_{\eta}}}^{2^{\alpha_{\eta}+1}-1}\sum_{j=2^{\alpha_{\eta}}}^{2^{\alpha_{\eta}+1}-1}\widehat{f}(i,j)w_{i}\left(x^{1}\right)w_{j}\left(x^{2}\right)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\sum_{i=2^{\alpha_{k}}}^{n-1}\sum_{j=2^{\alpha_{k}}}^{n-1}\widehat{f}(i,j)w_{i}\left(x^{1}\right)w_{j}\left(x^{2}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{\eta=0}^{k-1}\sum_{i=2^{\alpha_{\eta}}}^{2^{\alpha_{\eta}+1}-1}\sum_{j=2^{\alpha_{\eta}}}^{2^{\alpha_{\eta}+1}-1}\frac{2^{\alpha_{\eta}\left(2/p-2\right)}}{\Phi^{1/4}\left(2^{\alpha_{\eta}}\right)}w_{i}\left(x^{1}\right)w_{j}\left(x^{2}\right)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\sum_{i=2^{\alpha_{k}}}^{n-1}\sum_{j=2^{\alpha_{k}}}^{n-1}\frac{2^{\alpha_{k}\left(2/p-2\right)}}{\Phi^{1/4}\left(2^{\alpha_{k}}\right)}w_{i}\left(x^{1}\right)w_{j}\left(x^{2}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{\eta=0}^{k-1}\frac{2^{\alpha_{\eta}\left(2/p-2\right)}}{\Phi^{1/4}\left(2^{\alpha_{\eta}}\right)}\left(D_{2^{\alpha_{\eta}+1}}\left(x^{1}\right)-D_{2^{\alpha_{\eta}}}\left(x^{1}\right)\right)\left(D_{2^{\alpha_{\eta}+1}}\left(x^{2}\right)-D_{2^{\alpha_{\eta}}}\left(x^{2}\right)\right)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\frac{2^{\alpha_{k}\left(2/p-2\right)}}{\Phi^{1/4}\left(2^{\alpha_{k}}\right)}\left(D_{{}_{n}}\left(x^{1}\right)-D_{2^{\alpha_{k}}}\left(x^{1}\right)\right)\left(D_{n}\left(x^{2}\right)-D_{2^{\alpha_{k}}}\left(x^{2}\right)\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle I+II.

Let
\left(x^{1},x^{2}\right)\in\left(G\backslash I_{1}\right)^{2}
and
n\
is odd number. Since
n-2^{2\alpha_{k}}\
is also odd, according to both equality of Lemma
2.3
we get that

\displaystyle\left|II\right|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{2^{2\alpha_{k}\left(2/p-2\right)}}{\Phi^{1/4}\left(2^{2\alpha_{k}}\right)}\left|w_{2^{2\alpha_{k}}}\left(x^{1}\right)D_{n-2^{2\alpha_{k}}}\left(x^{1}\right)w_{2^{2\alpha_{k}}}\left(x^{2}\right)D_{n-2^{2\alpha_{k}}}\left(x^{2}\right)\right|

(4.13)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{2^{2\alpha_{k}\left(2/p-2\right)}}{\Phi^{1/4}\left(2^{2\alpha_{k}}\right)}\left|w_{2^{2\alpha_{k}}}\left(x^{1}\right)w_{n-2^{2\alpha_{k}}}\left(x^{1}\right)D_{{}_{1}}\left(x^{1}\right)w_{2^{2\alpha_{k}}}\left(x^{2}\right)w_{n-2^{2\alpha_{k}}}\left(x^{2}\right)D_{{}_{1}}\left(x^{2}\right)\right|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{2^{2\alpha_{k}\left(2/p-2\right)}}{\Phi^{1/4}\left(2^{2\alpha_{k}}\right)}.

If we apply again second equality of Lemma
2.3
according to condition
\alpha_{n}\geq 2

\left(n\in\mathbb{N}\right)
for
I
we can conclude that

I=\sum_{\eta=0}^{k-1}\frac{2^{2\alpha_{k}\left(2/p-2\right)}}{\Phi^{1/4}\left(2^{2\alpha_{\eta}}\right)}\left(D_{2^{2\alpha_{\eta}+1}}\left(x^{1}\right)-D_{2^{2\alpha_{\eta}}}\left(x^{1}\right)\right)\left(D_{2^{\alpha_{\eta}+1}}\left(x^{2}\right)-D_{2^{\alpha_{\eta}}}\left(x^{2}\right)\right)=0.

(4.14)

Hence,

\displaystyle\left\|S_{n,n}f\left(x^{1},x^{2}\right)\right\|_{weak-L_{p}(G^{2})}

(4.15)

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\frac{2^{2\alpha_{k}\left(2/p-2\right)}}{2\Phi^{1/4}\left(2^{2\alpha_{k}}\right)}\left(\mu\left\{\left(x^{1},x^{2}\right)\in\left(G\backslash I_{1}\right)^{2}:\left|S_{n,n}f\left(x^{1},x^{2}\right)\right|\geq\frac{2^{2\alpha_{k}\left(2/p-2\right)}}{2\Phi^{1/4}\left(2^{2\alpha_{k}}\right)}\right\}\right)^{1/p}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\frac{2^{2\alpha_{k}\left(2/p-2\right)}}{2\Phi^{1/4}\left(2^{2\alpha_{k}}\right)}\left|(G\backslash I_{1}\right)^{2}|\geq\frac{c_{p}2^{2\alpha_{k}\left(2/p-2\right)}}{\Phi^{1/4}\left(2^{2\alpha_{k}}\right)}.

By using (
4.15
) we get that

\displaystyle\underset{n=1}{\overset{2^{2\alpha_{k}+1}-1}{\sum}}\frac{\left\|S_{n,n}f\right\|_{weak-L_{p}(G^{2})}^{p}\Phi\left(n\right)}{n^{3-2p}}

(4.16)

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\underset{n=2^{2\alpha_{k}}+1}{\overset{2^{2\alpha_{k}+1}-1}{\sum}}\frac{\left\|S_{n,n}f\right\|_{weak-L_{p}(G^{2})}^{p}\Phi\left(n\right)}{n^{3-2p}}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle c_{p}\Phi\left(2^{2\alpha_{k}}\right)\underset{n=2^{\alpha_{k}-1}+1}{\overset{2^{\alpha_{k}}-1}{\sum}}\frac{\left\|S_{2n+1,2n+1}f\right\|_{weak-L_{p}(G^{2})}^{p}}{\left(2n+1\right)^{3-2p}}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle c_{p}\Phi\left(2^{2\alpha_{k}}\right)\frac{2^{2\alpha_{k}\left(1-p\right)}}{\Phi^{1/4}\left(2^{2\alpha_{k}}\right)}\underset{n=2^{2\alpha_{k}-1}+1}{\overset{2^{2\alpha_{k}}-1}{\sum}}\frac{1}{\left(2n+1\right)^{3-2p}}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle c_{p}\Phi^{3/4}\left(2^{2\alpha_{k}}\right)\rightarrow\infty,\text{ \quad as \quad}k\rightarrow\infty.

Theorem is proved.

4.4
Strong convergence of Marcinkiewicz means with respect to the two-dimensional Walsh-Fourier series on the martingale Hardy spaces

In this section we consider strong convergence of Marcinkiewicz means with respect to the two-dimensional Walsh-Fourier series in the martingale Hardy spaces for
p=2/3
(for details see Nagy and Tephnadze
[
35
]
).

Theorem 4.50

Let
f\in H_{2/3}\left(G^{2}\right)
. Then there exists an absolute constant
c,
such that

\frac{1}{\log n}\sum_{m=1}^{n}\frac{\left\|\mathscr{M}_{m}f\right\|_{H_{2/3}(G^{2})}^{2/3}}{m}\leq c\left\|f\right\|_{H_{2/3}(G^{2})}^{2/3}.

Proof
:
Suppose that

\frac{1}{\log n}{\sum_{m=1}^{n}}\frac{\left\|\mathscr{M}_{m}f\right\|_{2/3}^{2/3}}{m}\leq c\left\|f\right\|_{H_{2/3}(G^{2})}^{2/3}.

(4.17)

By combining Lemma
4.48
and inequalities (
1.18
), (
1.22
), (
4.17
) we get that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\log n}{\sum_{m=1}^{n}}\frac{\left\|\mathscr{M}_{m}f\right\|_{H_{2/3}(G^{2})}^{2/3}}{m}

(4.18)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\log n}{\sum_{m=1}^{n}}\frac{\left\|\mathscr{M}_{m}f\right\|_{2/3}^{2/3}}{m}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\left\|\widetilde{\mathscr{M}}_{\#}^{\ast}f\right\|_{2/3}^{2/3}+\left\|\widetilde{S}_{\#}^{\ast}f\right\|_{2/3}^{2/3}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left\|f\right\|_{H_{2/3}(G^{2})}^{2/3}.

Since
\mathscr{M}_{n}
is (see Lemma
4.40
) bounded from
L_{\infty}(G^{2})
to
L_{\infty}(G^{2})
, if we use Lemma
4.45
we only have to prove that

\frac{1}{\log n}\sum_{m=1}^{n}\frac{\left\|\mathscr{M}_{m}a\right\|_{2/3}^{2/3}}{m}<c<\infty,

for every
2/3
-atom
a
.

Let
a
be
2/3
-atom with support
I^{2}
, where
\mu(I^{2})=2^{-2N}
. Without loss the generality we may assume that
I^{2}:=I_{N}^{2}
. It is easy to show that
{\mathscr{M}}_{n}a=0

for
n\leq 2^{N}.
So, we may assume that
n>2^{N}
.

We can write that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\log n}\sum_{m=1}^{n}\frac{\left\|\mathscr{M}_{m}a\right\|_{2/3}^{2/3}}{m}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\log n}\sum_{m=2^{N}}^{n}\frac{\left\|{\mathscr{M}_{m}a}\right\|_{2/3}^{2/3}}{m}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\log n}\sum_{m=2^{N}}^{n}\int_{I_{N}\times I_{N}}\frac{\left|{\mathscr{M}_{m}a}\right|^{2/3}}{m}d\mu

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\log n}\sum_{m=2^{N}}^{n}\int_{I_{N}\times\overline{I_{N}}}\frac{\left|{\mathscr{M}_{m}a}\right|^{2/3}}{m}d\mu

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\log n}\sum_{m=2^{N}}^{n}\int_{\overline{I_{N}}\times{I_{N}}}\frac{\left|{\mathscr{M}_{m}a}\right|^{2/3}}{m}d\mu

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\log n}\sum_{m=2^{N}}^{n}\int_{\overline{I_{N}}\times\overline{I_{N}}}\frac{\left|{\mathscr{M}_{m}a}\right|^{2/3}}{m}d\mu

\displaystyle=:

\displaystyle I_{1}+I_{2}+I_{3}+I_{4}.

By applying Lemma
4.40
we have that

\displaystyle I_{1}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\log n}\sum_{m=2^{N}}^{\infty}\int_{I_{N}\times I_{N}}\frac{\left|\mathscr{M}_{m}a\right|^{2/3}}{m}d\mu

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\log n}\sum_{m=2^{N}}^{\infty}\frac{1}{m}\left\|a\right\|_{\infty}^{2/3}/2^{2N}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\log n}\sum_{m=2^{N}}^{n}\frac{1}{m}<c<\infty.

Now, we estimate
I_{2}
. Set

J_{t}:=I_{t}\backslash I_{t+1},\text{ \ \ }(t\in{\mathbb{N}}).

We introduce
\overline{I_{N}}
and
J_{m^{2}}
as the following disjoint union:

\overline{I_{N}}=\bigcup_{m^{2}=0}^{N-1}J_{m^{2}},\quad J_{m^{2}}=\bigcup_{q^{2}=m^{2}+1}^{N}I_{N}^{m^{2},q^{2}},

(4.19)

where

I_{N}^{m^{2},q^{2}}:=\begin{cases}I_{q^{2}+1}(0,...,0,x_{m^{2}}=1,0,...,0,x_{q^{2}}=1),&\text{ where }\text{ \ \ }m^{2}<q^{2}<N,\\
I_{N}(0,...,0,x_{m^{2}}=1,0,...,0),&\text{ where }\text{ \ \ }q^{2}=N.\end{cases}

Let
(x^{1},x^{2})\in I_{N}\times I_{N}^{m^{2},q^{2}}.
According to Lemma
4.42
we can conclude that

\displaystyle{|{\mathscr{M}}_{n}a(x^{1},x^{2})|}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\|a\|_{\infty}\int_{I_{N}\times I_{N}}|K_{n}(x^{1}+t^{1},x^{2}+t^{2})|d\mu(t^{1},t^{2})

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle c2^{3N}\frac{2^{m^{2}}}{n2^{N}}\sum_{s=m^{2}}^{q^{2}}D_{2^{s}}(x^{2}+e_{m^{2}})

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{c2^{2N+m^{2}}}{n}\sum_{s=m^{2}}^{q^{2}}2^{s}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{c2^{2N+m^{2}+q^{2}}}{n}.

Hence,

\displaystyle I_{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{c2^{4N/3}}{\log n}\sum_{m=2^{N}}^{n}\sum_{m^{2}=0}^{N-1}\sum_{q^{2}=m^{2}+1}^{N}\int_{I_{N}\times I_{N}^{m^{2},q^{2}}}\frac{\left|{\mathscr{M}_{m}a}\right|^{2/3}}{m}d\mu

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{c2^{4N/3}}{\log n}\sum_{m=2^{N}}^{n}\sum_{m^{2}=0}^{N-1}\sum_{q^{2}=m^{2}+1}^{N}\int_{I_{N}\times I_{N}^{m^{2},q^{2}}}\frac{2^{2(m^{2}+q^{2})/3}}{m^{5/3}}d\mu

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{c2^{4N/3}}{\log n}\sum_{m=2^{N}}^{n}\sum_{m^{2}=0}^{N-1}\sum_{q^{2}=m^{2}+1}^{N}\frac{2^{2(m^{2}+q^{2})/3}}{m^{5/3}}2^{-N-q^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{c2^{N/3}}{\log n}\sum_{m=2^{N}}^{\infty}\frac{1}{m^{5/3}}\sum_{m^{2}=0}^{N-1}2^{2m^{2}/3}\sum_{q^{2}=m^{2}+1}^{N}2^{-q^{2}/3}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{c2^{N/3}}{\log n}\sum_{m=2^{N}}^{\infty}\frac{2^{N/3}}{m^{5/3}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{c2^{2N/3}}{\log n}\sum_{m=2^{N}}^{\infty}\frac{1}{m^{5m/3}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{c}{N}.

Analogously, we can prove that
I_{3}\leq c<\infty
.

Next we prove boundedness of
I_{4}
. If we apply (
4.19
) we get that

\displaystyle I_{4}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\log n}\sum_{m=2^{N}}^{n}\sum_{l^{1}=0}^{N-1}\sum_{m^{2}=0}^{l^{1}-1}\int_{J_{l^{1}}\times J_{m^{2}}}\frac{\left|{\mathscr{M}_{m}a}\right|^{2/3}}{m}d\mu

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\log n}\sum_{m=2^{N}}^{n}\sum_{l^{1}=0}^{N-1}\sum_{m^{2}=l^{1}}^{N-1}\int_{J_{l^{1}}\times J_{m^{2}}}\frac{\left|{\mathscr{M}_{m}a}\right|^{2/3}}{m}d\mu

\displaystyle=:

\displaystyle I_{4,1}+I_{4,2}.

Let consider
I_{4,2}
(Analogously we can estimate
I_{4,1}
). For
(x^{1},x^{2})\in J_{l^{1}}\times J_{m^{2}}
if we apply Lemma
4.41
we obtain that

\displaystyle{|{\mathscr{M}}_{n}a(x^{1},x^{2})|}

\displaystyle\leq\|a\|_{\infty}\int_{I_{N}\times I_{N}}|K_{n}(x^{1}+t^{1},x^{2}+t^{2})|d\mu(t^{1},t^{2})

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{2^{N+l^{1}-m^{2}}}{n}\sum_{r^{1}=l^{1}+1}^{m^{2}+1}2^{r^{1}}D_{2^{m^{2}+1}}(x^{1}+e_{l^{1}}+e_{r^{1}})\sum_{s=m^{2}+1}^{N}D_{2^{s}}(x^{2}+e_{m^{2}}+x_{m^{2}+1,s-1}^{1})

\displaystyle+\frac{2^{N+l^{1}+m^{2}}}{n}\sum_{s=l^{1}}^{m^{2}}\sum_{r^{1}=l^{1}+1}^{s}D_{2^{s}}(x^{1}+e_{l^{1}}+e_{r^{1}}).

It is evident that

\displaystyle\int_{J_{l^{1}}\times J_{m^{2}}}D_{2^{m^{2}+1}}^{2/3}(x^{1}+e_{l^{1}}+e_{r^{1}})D_{2^{s}}^{2/3}(x^{2}+e_{m^{2}}+x_{m^{2}+1,s-1}^{1})d\mu(x^{1},x^{2})

\displaystyle\leq c2^{2s/3-m^{2}/3-l^{1}}\leq c2^{-\left(m^{2}+s\right)/3}

and

\displaystyle\int_{J_{l^{1}}\times J_{m^{2}}}D_{2^{s}}^{2/3}(x^{1}+e_{l^{1}}+e_{r^{1}})d\mu(x^{1},x^{2})\leq c2^{2s/3-m^{2}-l^{1}}\leq c2^{-m^{2}-s/3}.

Hence,

\displaystyle\int_{J_{l^{1}}\times J_{m^{2}}}\left|{\mathscr{M}_{m}a}\right|^{2/3}d\mu

\displaystyle\leq\frac{c2^{2\left(N+l^{1}-m^{2}\right)/3}}{m^{2/3}}\sum_{r^{1}=l^{1}+1}^{m^{2}+1}\sum_{s=m^{2}+1}^{N}2^{2r^{1}/3}\times

\displaystyle\times\int_{J_{l^{1}}\times J_{m^{2}}}D_{2^{m^{2}+1}}^{2/3}(x^{1}+e_{l^{1}}+e_{r^{1}})D_{2^{s}}^{2/3}(x^{2}+e_{m^{2}}+x_{m^{2}+1,s-1}^{1})d\mu\left(x^{1},x^{2}\right)

\displaystyle+\frac{c2^{2\left(N+l^{1}+m^{2}\right)/3}}{m^{2/3}}\sum_{s=l^{1}}^{m^{2}}\sum_{r^{1}=l^{1}+1}^{s}\int_{J_{l^{1}}\times J_{m^{2}}}D_{2^{s}}^{2/3}(x^{1}+e_{l^{1}}+e_{r^{1}})d\mu(x^{1},x^{2})

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{c2^{2(N+l^{1}-m^{2})/3}}{m^{2/3}}\sum_{r^{1}=l^{1}+1}^{m^{2}+1}2^{2r^{1}/3}\sum_{s=m^{2}+1}^{N}2^{-\left(m^{2}+s\right)/3}+\frac{c2^{2(N+l^{1}+m^{2})/3}}{m^{2/3}}\sum_{s=l^{1}}^{m^{2}}\sum_{r^{1}=l^{1}+1}^{s}2^{-m^{2}-s/3}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{c2^{2(N+l^{1}-m^{2})/3}}{m^{2/3}}2^{-2m^{2}/3}\sum_{r^{1}=l^{1}+1}^{m^{2}+1}2^{2r^{1}/3}+\frac{c2^{2\left(N+l^{1}+m^{2}\right)/3}}{m^{2/3}}\sum_{s=l_{1}}^{m^{2}}\left(s-l_{1}-1\right)2^{-m^{2}-s/3}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{c2^{2\left(N+l^{1}-m^{2}\right)/3}}{m^{2/3}}+\frac{c2^{\left(2N+l^{1}-m^{2}\right)/3}}{m^{2/3}}.

and

\begin{split}I_{4,2}&\leq\frac{c}{\log n}\sum_{m=2^{N}}^{n}\frac{1}{m}\sum_{l^{1}=0}^{N-1}\sum_{m^{2}=l^{1}}^{N-1}\frac{2^{2(N+l^{1}-m^{2})/3}+2^{\left(2N-m^{2}+l^{1}\right)/3}}{m^{2/3}}\\
&\leq\frac{c2^{2N/3}}{\log n}\sum_{m=2^{N}}^{n}\frac{1}{m^{5/3}}\sum_{l^{1}=0}^{N-1}1\leq c.\end{split}

Corollary 4.51

Let
f\in H_{2/3}\left(G^{2}\right).
Then

\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}\frac{1}{\log n}\sum_{m=1}^{n}\frac{\left\|\mathscr{M}_{m}f-f\right\|_{H_{2/3}\left(G^{2}\right)}^{2/3}}{m}=0

and

\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}\frac{1}{\log n}\sum_{m=1}^{n}\frac{\left\|\mathscr{M}_{m}f\right\|_{H_{2/3}\left(G^{2}\right)}^{2/3}}{m}=\left\|f\right\|_{H_{2/3}\left(G^{2}\right)}^{2/3}.

4.5
Modulus of continuity and convergence in norm of Marcinkiewicz means with respect to the two-dimensional Walsh-Fourier series on the martingale Hardy spaces

In this section we investigate necessary and sufficient conditions for modulus of continuity, which provide convergence in norm of Marcinkiewicz means with respect to the two-dimensional Walsh-Fourier series in
H_{2/3}
-norm (For details see
[
37
]
).

Theorem 4.52

a) Let
f\in H_{2/3}(G^{2})
and

\omega_{H_{2/3}(G^{2})}\left(\frac{1}{2^{k}},f\right)=o\left(\frac{1}{k^{3/2}}\right),\text{\hskip 10.22217ptas \hskip 10.22217pt}k\rightarrow\infty.

(4.20)

Then

\left\|\mathscr{M}_{n}f-f\right\|_{H_{2/3}(G^{2})}\rightarrow 0,\text{ \hskip 10.22217ptas \hskip 10.22217pt}n\rightarrow\infty.

b) There exists a martingale
f\in H_{2/3}(G^{2}),
such that

\omega_{H_{2/3}(G^{2})}\left(\frac{1}{2^{2^{k}}},f\right)=O\left(\frac{1}{2^{3k/2}}\right),\text{ \hskip 10.22217ptas \hskip 10.22217pt}k\rightarrow\infty

and

\left\|\mathscr{M}_{n}f-f\right\|_{2/3}\nrightarrow 0\text{ \hskip 10.22217ptas \hskip 10.22217pt}n\rightarrow\infty.

Proof
:
In
[
34
]
it was proved that (see inequality (
1.23
)) the following inequality is true:

\left\|\mathscr{M}_{n}f\right\|_{2/3}\leq c\log^{2/3}\left(n+1\right)\left\|f\right\|_{H_{2/3}(G^{2})}.

(4.21)

If we apply inequality (
1.22
) and Lemma
4.48
according to (
4.21
) we get the following estimation

\displaystyle\left\|\mathscr{M}_{n}f\right\|_{H_{2/3}(G^{2})}^{2/3}

(4.22)

\displaystyle\leq\left\|\mathscr{M}_{n}f\right\|_{2/3}^{2/3}+\left\|\widetilde{\mathscr{M}}_{\#}^{\ast}f\right\|_{2/3}^{2/3}+\left\|\widetilde{S}_{\#}^{\ast}f\right\|_{2/3}^{2/3}

\displaystyle\leq c\log\left(n+1\right)\left\|f\right\|_{H_{2/3}(G^{2})}^{2/3}+c\left\|f\right\|_{H_{2/3}(G^{2})}^{2/3}

\displaystyle\leq c\log\left(n+1\right)\left\|f\right\|_{H_{2/3}(G^{2})}^{2/3}.

Let
2^{N}<n\leq 2^{N+1}.
If we use (
4.22
) by simple calculations we have that

\displaystyle\left\|\mathscr{M}_{n}f-f\right\|_{H_{2/3}(G^{2})}^{2/3}

\displaystyle\leq\left\|\mathscr{M}_{n}f-\mathscr{M}_{n}S_{2^{N},2^{N}}f\right\|_{H_{2/3}(G^{2})}^{2/3}

\displaystyle+\left\|\mathscr{M}_{n}S_{2^{N},2^{N}}f-S_{2^{N},2^{N}}f\right\|_{H_{2/3}(G^{2})}^{2/3}

\displaystyle+\left\|S_{2^{N},2^{N}}f-f\right\|_{H_{2/3}(G^{2})}^{2/3}

\displaystyle=\left\|\mathscr{M}_{n}\left(S_{2^{N},2^{N}}f-f\right)\right\|_{H_{2/3}}^{2/3}

\displaystyle+\left\|\mathscr{M}_{n}S_{2^{N},2^{N}}f-S_{2^{N},2^{N}}f\right\|_{H_{2/3}(G^{2})}^{2/3}

\displaystyle+\left\|S_{2^{N},2^{N}}f-f\right\|_{H_{2/3}(G^{2})}^{2/3}

\displaystyle\leq c\left(\log\left(n+1\right)+1\right)\omega_{H_{2/3}(G^{2})}^{2/3}\left(\frac{1}{2^{N}},f\right)

\displaystyle+\left\|\mathscr{M}_{n}S_{2^{N},2^{N}}f-S_{2^{N},2^{N}}f\right\|_{H_{2/3}(G^{2})}^{2/3}.

Let
2^{N}<n\leq 2^{N+1}.
Then it is evident that

\displaystyle\mathscr{M}_{n}S_{2^{N},2^{N}}f-S_{2^{N},2^{N}}f

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\sum_{k=0}^{2^{N}}S_{k,k}S_{2^{N},2^{N}}f

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\sum_{k=2^{N}+1}^{n}S_{k,k}S_{2^{N},2^{N}}f-S_{2^{N},2^{N}}f

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\sum_{k=0}^{2^{N}}S_{k,k}f

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\frac{\left(n-2^{N}\right)S_{2^{N},2^{N}}f}{n}-S_{2^{N},2^{N}}f

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{2^{N}}{n}\left(\mathscr{M}_{2^{N}}f-S_{2^{N},2^{N}}f\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{2^{N}}{n}\left(S_{2^{N},2^{N}}\mathscr{M}_{2^{N}}f-S_{2^{N},2^{N}}f\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{2^{N}}{n}S_{2^{N},2^{N}}\left(\mathscr{M}_{2^{N}}f-f\right).

By combining (
1.19
) and (
1.21
) we get that

\displaystyle\left\|\mathscr{M}_{n}S_{2^{N},2^{N}}f-S_{2^{N},2^{N}}f\right\|_{H_{2/3}(G^{2})}^{2/3}

(4.23)

\displaystyle\leq\left(\frac{2^{N}}{n}\right)^{2/3}\hskip-10.0pt\left\|S_{2^{N},2^{N}}\left(\mathscr{M}_{2^{N}}f-f\right)\right\|_{H_{2/3}(G^{2})}^{2/3}

\displaystyle\leq\left\|S_{2^{N},2^{N}}\left(\mathscr{M}_{2^{N}}f-f\right)\right\|_{H_{2/3}(G^{2})}^{2/3}

\displaystyle\leq\left\|\mathscr{M}_{2^{N}}f-f\right\|_{H_{2/3}(G^{2})}^{2/3}\rightarrow 0,\text{ where }k\rightarrow\infty,

Hence, we immediately get that if

\omega_{H_{2/3}(G^{2})}\left(\frac{1}{2^{n}},f\right)=o\left(\frac{1}{n^{3/2}}\right),\text{ as }n\rightarrow\infty,

then

\left\|\mathscr{M}_{n}f-f\right\|_{H_{2/3}(G^{2})}\rightarrow 0,\text{ as
}n\rightarrow\infty.

Now, prove part b) of Theorem
4.52
. Let

a_{i}(x^{1},x^{2})=2^{2^{i}}\left(D_{2^{2^{i}+1}}(x^{1})-D_{2^{2^{i}}}(x^{1})\right)\left(D_{2^{2^{i}+1}}(x^{2})-D_{2^{2^{i}}}(x^{2})\right)

and

f_{n}(x^{1},x^{2})={\sum_{i=1}^{n}}\frac{a_{i}(x^{1},x^{2})}{2^{3i/2}}.

Since

{\sum_{i=1}^{\infty}}\left(\frac{1}{2^{3i/2}}\right)^{2/3}<c<\infty

\displaystyle S_{2^{n},2^{n}}a_{k}(x^{1},x^{2})=\begin{cases}a_{k}(x^{1},x^{2}),&\text{ \ if \ }2^{k}\leq n,\\
0,&\text{ \ if \ }2^{k}>n\end{cases}

and

\displaystyle\text{supp }a_{k}=I_{2^{k}}^{2},

\displaystyle\int_{I_{2^{k}}^{2}}a_{k}d\mu=0,

\displaystyle\left\|a_{k}\right\|_{\infty}\leq\mu(\text{supp }a_{k})^{-3/2},

by using Lemma
4.44
, we conclude that
f\in H_{2/3}.

On the other hand, if we apply Remark
4.39
we immediately get that

\displaystyle f-S_{2^{n},2^{n}}f

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(f^{\left(1\right)}-S_{2^{n},2^{n}}f^{\left(1\right)},...,f^{\left(n\right)}-S_{2^{n},2^{n}}f^{\left(n\right)},...,f^{\left(n+k\right)}-S_{2^{n},2^{n}}f^{\left(n+k\right)},...\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(0,...,0,f^{\left(n+1\right)}-f^{\left(n\right)},...,f^{\left(n+k\right)}-f^{\left(n\right)},...\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(0,...,0,...,\sum_{i=\log n+1}^{\log n+k}\frac{a_{i}(x)}{2^{3i/2}},...\right),\text{ \ }k\in\mathbb{N}_{+},

Hence,

\displaystyle\omega_{H_{2/3}(G^{2})}\left(\frac{1}{2^{n}},f\right)\leq\sum_{i=\left[\log n\right]}^{\infty}\frac{1}{2^{3i/2}}=O\left(\frac{1}{n^{3/2}}\right)\text{ \quad as \quad}k\rightarrow\infty.

where
\left[\log n\right]
denotes integer part of
\log n
.

Set

\displaystyle n_{2^{A-2}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2^{4\cdot 2^{A-2}}+2^{4\cdot 2^{A-2}-4}+...+2^{4}+2^{0}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2^{2^{A}}+2^{2^{A}-4}+...+2^{4}+2^{0}.

If we use Lemma
4.43
we get that

\displaystyle\mathscr{M}_{n_{2^{k-2}}}f-f

(4.24)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{2^{2^{k}}\mathscr{M}_{2^{2^{k}}}f}{n_{2^{k-2}}}+\frac{1}{n_{2^{k-2}}}\sum_{j=2^{2^{k}}+1}^{n_{2^{k-2}}}S_{j,j}f

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle\frac{2^{2^{k}}f}{n_{2^{k-2}}}-\frac{n_{2^{k-2}-1}f}{n_{2^{k-2}}}.

It is evident that

\widehat{f}(i,j)=\begin{cases}\frac{2^{2^{k}}}{2^{3k/2}},&\text{ თუ }\left(i,j\right)\in\left\{2^{2^{k}},...,2^{2^{k}+1}-1\right\}^{2},\ k\in\mathbb{N}\\
0,&\text{ თუ }\left(i,j\right)\notin\bigcup\limits_{k=0}^{\infty}\left\{2^{2^{k}},...,2^{2^{k}+1}-1\right\}^{2}.\end{cases}

(4.25)

Let
2^{2^{k}}<j\leq n_{2^{k-1}}.
Since
w_{v+2^{2^{k}}}=w_{2^{2^{k}}}w_{v},
when
v<2^{2^{k}}
, if we apply (
4.25
) and first equality of Lemma
2.2
we obtain that

\displaystyle S_{j,j}f\left(x^{1},x^{2}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle S_{2^{2^{k}},2^{2^{k}}}f\left(x^{1},x^{2}\right)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\sum_{v=2^{2^{k}}}^{j-1}\sum_{s=2^{2^{k}}}^{j-1}\widehat{f}(v,s)w_{v,s}\left(x^{1},x^{2}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle S_{2^{2^{k}},2^{2^{k}}}f\left(x^{1},x^{2}\right)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\frac{2^{2^{k}}}{2^{3k/2}}\sum_{v=0}^{j-2^{2^{k}}-1}\sum_{s=0}^{j-2^{2^{k}}-1}w_{v+2^{2^{k}}}\left(x^{1}\right)w_{s+2^{2^{k}}}\left(x^{2}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle S_{2^{2^{k}},2^{2^{k}}}f\left(x^{1},x^{2}\right)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\frac{2^{2^{k}}w_{2^{2^{k}}}\left(x^{1}\right)w_{2^{2^{k}}}\left(x^{2}\right)}{2^{3k/2}}\sum_{v=0}^{j-2^{2^{k}}-1}\sum_{s=0}^{j-2^{2^{k}}-1}w_{v}\left(x^{1}\right)w_{s}\left(x^{2}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle S_{2^{2^{k}},2^{2^{k}}}f\left(x^{1},x^{2}\right)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\frac{2^{2^{k}}w_{2^{2^{k}}}\left(x^{1}\right)w_{2^{2^{k}}}\left(x^{2}\right)D_{{}_{j-2^{2^{k}},j-2^{2^{k}}}}\left(x^{1},x^{2}\right)}{2^{3k/2}}.

Hence,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n_{2^{k-2}}}\sum_{j=2^{2^{k}}+1}^{n_{2^{k-2}}}S_{j,j}f\left(x^{1},x^{2}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{n_{2^{k-2}-1}S_{2^{2^{k}},2^{2^{k}}}f\left(x^{1},x^{2}\right)}{n_{2^{k-2}}}

\displaystyle+\frac{2^{2^{k}}w_{2^{2^{k}}}\left(x^{1}\right)w_{2^{2^{k}}}\left(x^{2}\right)}{n_{2^{k-2}}2^{3k/2}}\sum_{j=1}^{n_{2^{k-2}-1}}D_{{}_{j,j}}\left(x^{1},x^{2}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{n_{2^{k-2}-1}S_{2^{2^{k}},2^{2^{k}}}f\left(x^{1},x^{2}\right)}{n_{2^{k-2}}}

\displaystyle+\frac{2^{2^{k}}w_{2^{2^{k}}}\left(x^{1}\right)w_{2^{2^{k}}}\left(x^{2}\right)n_{2^{k-2}-1}K_{n_{2^{k-2}-1}}\left(x^{1},x^{2}\right)}{n_{2^{k-1}}2^{3k/2}}.

By applying (
4.24
) we have that

\displaystyle\|\mathscr{M}_{n_{2^{k-2}}}f-f\|_{2/3}^{2/3}

(4.26)

\displaystyle\geq\frac{c}{2^{k}}\|{n_{2^{k-2}-1}}K_{n_{2^{k-2}-1}}\|_{2/3}^{2/3}

\displaystyle-\left(\frac{2^{2^{k}}}{n_{2^{k-2}}}\right)^{2/3}\|\mathscr{M}_{2^{2^{k}}}f-f\|_{2/3}^{2/3}

\displaystyle-\left(\frac{n_{2^{k-2}-1}}{n_{2^{k-2}}}\right)^{2/3}\|S_{2^{2^{k}},2^{2^{k}}}f-f\|_{2/3}^{2/3}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{c}{2^{k}}\|{n_{2^{k-2}-1}}K_{n_{2^{k-2}-1}}\|_{2/3}^{2/3}

\displaystyle-\|\mathscr{M}_{2^{2^{k}}}f-f\|_{2/3}^{2/3}

\displaystyle-\|S_{2^{2^{k}},2^{2^{k}}}f-f\|_{2/3}^{2/3}.

Set

\displaystyle x^{1}\in I_{2^{k-2}}^{m,l}

\displaystyle=:

\displaystyle I_{2^{k-2}}\left(0,...,0,x_{4m}^{1}=1,0,...,0,x_{4l}^{1}=1,x_{4l+1}^{1},...,x_{2^{k-2}-1}^{1}\right)

and

\displaystyle x^{2}\in J_{2^{k-2}}^{l,q}

\displaystyle=:

\displaystyle I_{2^{k-2}}\left(0,...,0,x_{4l}^{2}=1,x_{4l+1}^{1}...,x_{4q-1}^{1},1-x_{4q}^{1},x_{4q+1}^{2},...,x_{2^{k-2}-1}^{2}\right).

According to Lemma
4.43
we get that

n_{{}_{2^{k-2}-1}}\left|K_{n_{2^{k-2}-1}}\left(x^{1},x^{2}\right)\right|\geq 2^{4q+4l+4m-3}.

Hence,

\displaystyle\int_{G}(n_{{}_{2^{k-2}-1}}|K_{n_{2^{k-2}-1}}(x^{1},x^{2})|)^{2/3}d\mu(x^{1},x^{2})

\displaystyle\geq c\sum_{m=1}^{2^{k-2}-3}\sum_{l=m+1}^{2^{k-2}-2}\sum_{q=l+1}^{2^{k-2}-1}\sum_{x_{4l+1}^{1}=0}^{1}...\sum_{x_{{}_{2^{k-2}-1}}^{1}=0}^{1}\sum_{x_{4q+1}^{2}=0}^{1}...\sum_{x_{{}_{2^{k-2}-1}}^{2}=0}^{1}

\displaystyle\int_{I_{2^{k-2}}^{m,l}\times J_{2^{k-2}}^{l,q}}\hskip 0.0pt({n_{2^{k-2}-1}}|K_{n_{2^{k-2}-1}}(x^{1},x^{2})|)^{2/3}d\mu(x^{1},x^{2})

\displaystyle\geq c\sum_{m=1}^{2^{k-2}-3}\sum_{l=m+1}^{2^{k-2}-2}\sum_{q=l+1}^{2^{k-2}-1}\sum_{x_{4l+1}^{1}=0}^{1}...\sum_{x_{{}_{2^{k-2}-1}}^{1}=0}^{1}\sum_{x_{4q+1}^{2}=0}^{1}...\sum_{x_{{}_{2^{k-2}-1}}^{2}=0}^{1}

\displaystyle\mu\left(I_{2^{k-2}}^{m,l}\times J_{2^{k-2}}^{l,q}\right)2^{\left(8q+8l+8m\right)/3}

\displaystyle\geq c\sum_{m=1}^{2^{k-2}-3}\sum_{l=m+1}^{2^{k-2}-2}\sum_{q=l+1}^{2^{k-2}-1}2^{\left(8q+8l+8m\right)/3}2^{2^{k-2}-4l}2^{2^{k-2}-4q}\left(\frac{1}{2^{2^{k-2}}}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle\geq c\sum_{m=1}^{2^{k-2}-3}2^{8m/3}\sum_{l=m+1}^{2^{k-2}-2}2^{-4l/3}\sum_{q=l+1}^{2^{k-2}-1}2^{-4q/3}

\displaystyle\geq c\sum_{m=1}^{2^{k-2}-3}1\geq c2^{k}.

By combining (
1.17
), (
1.21
) and (
4.26
) we can conclude that

{\limsup_{k\rightarrow\infty}}\|\mathscr{M}_{n_{2^{k-2}}}f-f\|_{2/3}\geq c>0.

Theorem
4.52
is proved.
